" alt= " Na'moks , a spokesman for the Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs , says they will not meet with federal ministers despite Mounties offering to leave . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;85032;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1183576 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;85062;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A barricade has gone up on Canadian Pacific Rail lines near Kamloops by protesters who say they support of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en opponents of the Coastal GasLink pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thursday blockade of the line , crucial to trade through the Port of Vancouver , came just as the RCMP said it was prepared to remove its temporary outpost on the First Nation&amp;rsquo ; s territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal Public Safety Minister Bill Blair said the RCMP&amp;rsquo ; s offer meets conditions set by the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs opposed to the pipeline and their supporters to end blockades , but members of the Neskonlith Indian Band set up a blockade on CP tracks east of Kamloops in solidarity with the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Blair had hoped the RCMP&amp;rsquo ; s move could pave the way for a meeting between the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs and Crown-Indigenous Affairs Minister Carolyn Bennet and Indigenous Services Minister Marc Miller , who offered to do so as four of five chiefs visited the Mohawk supporters blockading CN Rail&amp;rsquo ; s mainline at Tyendinaga Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en Chief Na&amp;rsquo ; Moks , who also goes by John Ridsdale , said that meeting wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be possible because they need to make decisions as a group and three senior chiefs remained in B.C. , and they also want the pipeline builder , TC PipeLines to also move workers out of the territory as a precondition to any meeting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , divisions have emerged in communities within the region where Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people hope for an easing of tensions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s kind of hard to think about it , &amp;rdquo ; said Russell Tiljoe , an 84-year-old Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en from the Houston area . &amp;ldquo ; Some people who used to be good friends of ours , when they see us coming , they go the other direction . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tiljoe , who spoke in support of the pipeline at a public meeting in Houston , believes &amp;ldquo ; what we need is dialogue&amp;rdquo ; among all the hereditary chiefs without outside pressure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tiljoe said if the hereditary chiefs want RCMP out of their territory , outside protesters , whom he referred to as &amp;ldquo ; professional protesters , &amp;rdquo ; should also leave . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Let us work out our differences ourselves , &amp;rdquo ; Tiljoe said . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve done that for many generations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Coastal GasLink is building a $6.6 billion , 670 km underground natural gas pipeline between Dawson Creek and the LNG Canada development at Kitimat. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company has signed agreements with 20 elected First Nations band councils along the route , including five within the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , a group of hereditary chiefs remain opposed to the pipeline on the position that it is hereditary leaders who hold Aboriginal title to the land and the project needed their consent rather than elected band councils . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government response to the dispute should include &amp;ldquo ; a cooling-off period , &amp;rdquo ; during which there is no construction to provide some room for dialogue , said Jody Wilson Raybould , the independent MP for Vancouver Granville . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Raybould , a former regional chief for the Assembly of First Nations , offered the suggestion in a four-point plan she raised in the House of Commons during an emergency debate on Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that discussion , she said , should include an internal dialogue within the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en Nation &amp;ldquo ; needed to bring clarity about how they will approach the future of this project collectively . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Candice George , who lives in Fraser Lake but has Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs among her grandparents and great grandparents , said the communities should rely on their traditional bahlats system , using their language&amp;rsquo ; s word for potlatch , to resolve the dispute . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The only way we can solve these Indigenous issues is with Indigenous solutions , &amp;rdquo ; George said . &amp;ldquo ; The way I feel we need to move forward is we need to have a bahlats so each of our clans and each house needs to gather and discuss together the issues in the house and how we&amp;rsquo ; re going to move forward together . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; George , an entrepreneur and cultural educator , said there was a &amp;ldquo ; different vibe&amp;rdquo ; in the community during her last visit to Telkwa to visit family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People are really upset and don&amp;rsquo ; t know what&amp;rsquo ; s going to happen , &amp;rdquo ; George said . &amp;ldquo ; Some of my elders are concerned and don&amp;rsquo ; t want to even go to the grocery store . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;85098;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;85129;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;85167;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;85196;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;With files from The Canadian Press and Bloomberg&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerFri , 21 Feb 2020 03:42:59 **45;85227;TOOLONG ... NewsNewsDaphne Bramham : After decades of promises , has Canada finally reached a tipping point ? http : **34;85274;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;85310;TOOLONG ... title= " Supporters gather in Victoria to support Wet'suwet'en chiefs who oppose the Coastal GasLink pipeline . " alt= " Supporters gather in Victoria to support Wet'suwet'en chiefs who oppose the Coastal GasLink pipeline . " data-portal-copyright= " KEVIN LIGHT " **28;85346;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1219348 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;85376;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Countless words have been written in voluminous reports with hundreds of recommendations regarding the promise that Canada&amp;rsquo ; s relationship with Indigenous people will be transformed . Often years in the making and costing millions of dollars , the reports are encyclopedic , in both the good and the dull sense . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Warmly welcomed by politicians , the full reports with their many volumes are rarely read by them or by anyone else , including journalists and experts offering sage commentary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The excuses are easy : The reports are too long . We are too busy to read more than the executive summaries . And how much easier it is to reduce it all to a simple debate over whether using the word genocide is appropriate to describe the murders and disappearances of so many Aboriginal women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For every recommendation , promises are made to give due consideration . But after public attention has inevitably and quickly pivoted to the latest Instagram-able issues , it has been easy for politicians to decide the recommendations are too costly in dollar terms , political capital , or both . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has wasted so many opportunities . Why is anyone surprised by the disruption to national rail service , or by protests that have kept legislators and others from their work ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The only surprise is that the tipping point finally forcing a resolution to generations of talks seems to be the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs&amp;rsquo ; refusal to allow a natural gas pipeline to run through their traditional territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every little thing that makes a difference &amp;mdash ; to paraphrase the subtitle of Malcolm Gladwell&amp;rsquo ; s book The Tipping Point . He argues that there are three essential requirements to tip the balance and force change : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There must be strong leaders with influential and charismatic friends , which is a good description of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs and climate activist Greta Thunberg , who has accused Canada of violating children&amp;rsquo ; s rights because of its continued support for oil and gas development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has to be a &amp;ldquo ; stickiness factor&amp;rdquo ; that compels an idea or phenomenon to stick in people&amp;rsquo ; s minds and influence their future behaviour . That might , in this case , include small things such as the now-ubiquitous acknowledgements at every gathering of whose unceded territory people are meeting on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The final element is context , and for this , I&amp;rsquo ; ll stick to only the most recent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eight years ago , Idle No More shut down multiple border crossings , blocked railway lines , disrupted traffic with flash mobs and garnered international attention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It petered out when then-Prime Minister Stephen Harper promised high-level dialogue , not with Idle No More&amp;rsquo ; s female founders and leaders , but with then-Assembly of First Nations Chief Shawn Atleo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five years ago , the Truth and Reconciliation Commission&amp;rsquo ; s 11 pages of recommendations provided a roadmap for overcoming the legacy of residential schools . &amp;nbsp ; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau committed to implementing all 94 recommendations in December 2015 . The government&amp;rsquo ; s progress is tracked online at &lt;a href= " http : **34;85412;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That raised Indigenous peoples&amp;rsquo ; expectations , as did the appointment of Jody Wilson-Raybould as justice minister . But many hopes were dashed with the government&amp;rsquo ; s purchase of the Trans Mountain Pipeline and its subsequent re-approval , as did **25;85448;TOOLONG ; s demotion and eventual ouster from the Liberal caucus following her testimony about how Trudeau tried to convince her not to prosecute SNC-Lavalin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which brings us to the gas pipeline going through Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en territory that is backed by John Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s NDP government &amp;mdash ; the same government that less than a year ago promised to bring B.C. laws in line with the UN Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous People . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adding to the frustrations are 1,200 murdered and missing women , health outcomes that lag other Canadians by a wide margin , and 27 years of B.C. treaty negotiations that have yielded only eight finalized agreements , involving only 38 per cent of the province&amp;rsquo ; s Indian Act bands . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , yes , there&amp;rsquo ; s climate change that also disproportionately affects Indigenous people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Senator Murray Sinclair , who chaired the Truth and Reconciliation Commission , wrote in a recent blogpost that what is needed to resolve the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en pipeline dispute are &amp;ldquo ; positive acts . &amp;rdquo ; But he is not optimistic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Frankly , given Canada&amp;rsquo ; s intransigence , and the rising sense of injustice felt by Indigenous leadership throughout the country , I do not like where this is heading . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is a rich irony that among the strongest voices deriding protesters as radical activists and urging police to take action against them are themselves using the spectre of western separatism to get their way on pipeline approvals. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada is in the midst of an existential crisis with the intersection of Indigenous rights , climate change and the economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is no simple solution . Certainly , it can&amp;rsquo ; t be solved by company-sought court injunctions enforced by police , or by any other court rulings . It hasn&amp;rsquo ; t worked before in Canada and it hasn&amp;rsquo ; t worked in other democratic societies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s future depends on federal , provincial , Indigenous , business and community leaders as well as its citizens talking in good faith and with respect about what Canada should be and could be , not only a decade from now , but a century from now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;85475;TOOLONG " **36;85506;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamThu , 20 Feb 2020 17:33:57 **45;85544;TOOLONG ... **34;85591;TOOLONG investments needed in climate-friendly infrastructure , says resources ministerhttp : **32;85627;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;85661;TOOLONG ... title= " Recognizing the importance of oil and gas " does not mean we hide from the reality of climate change , " Natural Resources Minister Seamus O'Reagan. " alt= " Recognizing the importance of oil and gas " does not mean we hide from the reality of climate change , " Natural Resources Minister Seamus O'Reagan. " data-portal-copyright= " BLAIR GABLE " **28;85697;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1214170 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Regardless of what Canada does to reduce its own carbon emissions , &amp;ldquo ; what the world does matters , &amp;rdquo ; a session of the Globe 2020 conference in Vancouver was told Wednesday morning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What the world does on climate is going to be very impactful for Canada , &amp;rdquo ; said Jonathan Arnold , senior research associate with the recently launched think-tank Canadian Institute for Climate during a morning panel discussion . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Because we are a very small , trade-dependent economy , what the rest of the world does matters . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Arnold offered that observation against a background established by Natural Resources Minister Seamus O&amp;rsquo ; Regan who opened the session by reminding the audience that Canada is the world&amp;rsquo ; s fourth-largest oil producer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Oil and gas is an essential component of our national economy and will continue to be in 2025 and 2030 , &amp;rdquo ; said O&amp;rsquo ; Regan , while acknowledging that Canada does need an aggressive plan to meet the government&amp;rsquo ; s commitment to net zero emissions by 2020 . The term means emissions are matched by methods to remove carbon from the atmosphere . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However recognizing the importance of oil and gas &amp;ldquo ; does not mean we hide from the reality of climate change , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Regan said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Planning that transition will involve big investments in electrification and infrastructure and require rapid and consistent policy support from governments , panelists in the session said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s no time to waste , &amp;rdquo ; Arnold said . &amp;ldquo ; Infrastructure is a huge piece , those need to be happening now , if not yesterday , &amp;rdquo ; for Canada to meet its goals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And a possibility of what the world will do to transition off fossil fuels could be that 40 per cent of its energy needs are met by electricity by 2050 , said energy consultant Ken Elser , with 80 per cent of that power coming from renewable sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That depends on a massive adoption of electric vehicles in the transportation mix , said Elser , senior vice-president at the international consultancy DNV GL . The company is projecting cost parity for electric vehicles with gasoline-powered vehicles in the next five or six years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We see a tipping point early in the 2030s where half of new vehicles globally will be electric vehicles , &amp;rdquo ; Elser said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If trends hold , Elser said the DNV GL forecast estimates that the peak for global energy supplies , but not demand , will also occur sometime in the 2030s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That will be driven by energy efficiency , along with an increase in electrification of energy supplies , said Elser , but the demand will also have to be shaped on the policy side in ways that are friendly for consumers , added panelist Mallika Ishwaran. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You have to take your consumers , customers and society with you , &amp;rdquo ; said Ishwaran , an economist and policy adviser for energy giant Shell International . &amp;ldquo ; All these changes require changes to how we live today so the changes need to be done in a way that is acceptable for customers to go on this journey with you . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Regan said whatever plan Canada devises to reach its 2050 climate goals , Canadians &amp;ldquo ; must be honest with each other about just how fundamental this transition will be and how fast we&amp;rsquo ; ll be able to sustain such change . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And the path to net-zero emissions has to be devised &amp;ldquo ; thoughtfully so that entire regions of the country aren&amp;rsquo ; t left behind , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Regan said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;85727;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;85758;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;85796;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;85825;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerThu , 13 Feb 2020 00:55:31 **45;85856;TOOLONG ... NewsNewsAs protesters besiege legislature , B.C. throne speech offers few new promiseshttp : **32;85903;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;85937;TOOLONG ... title= " Protesters at the B.C. Legislature on Tuesday , Feb. 11 , 2020. " alt= " Protesters at the B.C. Legislature on Tuesday , Feb. 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Victoria Times Colonist " **28;85973;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212565 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;86003;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; Hundreds of protesters tried but failed to derail the B.C. NDP government&amp;rsquo ; s throne speech Tuesday as they demonstrated in support of hereditary Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs who oppose a gas pipeline through their traditional territory in northeastern B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provincial politicians returning for the first day of the spring session of the legislature faced protesters gathered in each of the building&amp;rsquo ; s entrances , linking arms and , in several cases , physically preventing MLAs and staff from entering the building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Agriculture Minister Lana Popham was faced down by a protester brandishing a feather who refused to let her pass . NDP MLA George Chow tried to jump down a stairwell to avoid protesters and fell , but said he was not hurt . Education Minister Rob Fleming had to be pulled by one arm through a crowded doorway by security guards . There were several empty seats inside the chamber belonging to MLAs who couldn&amp;rsquo ; t get into the building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The protesters called for government to halt construction of the Coastal GasLink pipeline near Houston , which is opposed by a group of Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;86039;TOOLONG ... title= " Wet'suwet'en supporter Eva Grant with the St'at'imc Nation chants with protesters on the steps of legislature before the throne speech in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " alt= " Wet'suwet'en supporter Eva Grant with the St'at'imc Nation chants with protesters on the steps of legislature before the throne speech in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;86075;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212783 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their position is in contrast to at least 20 elected First Nations councils along the pipeline route that have signed agreements with the company . &amp;nbsp ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The hereditary chiefs , however , say the band council established by the Indian Act only has authority over reserve lands , not 22,000-square-kilometre traditional territory . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NDP government supports the project , has issued all the permits and firmly backs the $40-billion LNG Canada terminal in Kitimat which the pipeline is to supply natural gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The First Nations LNG Alliance represents the First Nations councils in favour of the pipeline . Alliance chair Crystal Smith &amp;mdash ; chief councillor of the Haisla Nation , where LNG Canada is being built &amp;mdash ; acknowledged there was conflict between some Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs and the elected council of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , she said the project had widespread First Nations support and urged &amp;ldquo ; outside interests&amp;rdquo ; to let the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en deal with the issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We said yes to LNG Canada and Coastal GasLink because the proponents and the provincial government have respected our expertise when it comes to our territory and our culture , &amp;rdquo ; Smith wrote in an oped published Feb. 3 , adding the Haisla opposed the failed Northern Gateway bitumen pipeline proposal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tuesday&amp;rsquo ; s protests led to the cancellation of the ceremonial military honour guard , the firing of cannons and the traditional meet-and-greet between Lt.-Gov . Janet Austin and Premier John Horgan on the front steps of the legislature . It also delayed the start of morning session . However , Austin was ultimately able to read Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s throne speech in the afternoon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speech proved to be a 21-page rehash of the NDP&amp;rsquo ; s feats during its first two-and-a-half years as a minority government . At one point , the speech even repeated an 2017 NDP election slogan of &amp;ldquo ; putting people first . &amp;rdquo ; It made no mention of the protests raging outside . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At home , in our communities , and throughout our province , people are starting to make progress , &amp;rdquo ; read the speech . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; They are more able to face the challenges ahead , because they know they aren&amp;rsquo ; t alone . They have a government that is a partner in their future . &amp;hellip ; It&amp;rsquo ; s been two and a half years , but the hard work has only begun . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;86105;TOOLONG ... title= " Saul Brown with the Heiltsuk Nuu-chah-nulth First Nations speaks to protesters in support of Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs camped out in front of legislature before the throne speech in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " alt= " Saul Brown with the Heiltsuk Nuu-chah-nulth First Nations speaks to protesters in support of Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs camped out in front of legislature before the throne speech in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;86141;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212782 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan abruptly cancelled a news conference to talk about his agenda , instead issuing a statement saying his government&amp;rsquo ; s passage of laws supporting the United Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples in December was a first step on a long journey to reconciliation , &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These events show us why meaningful reconciliation with Indigenous peoples is our shared responsibility and is critical to our province and our country , &amp;rdquo ; the statement read . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; This was a commitment my government made in good faith two and a half years ago , and as premier , I am determined to see it through . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Opposition Liberal leader Andrew Wilkinson accused Horgan of failing at a crucial leadership moment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;86171;TOOLONG ... title= " Protesters in support of Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs block the entrance to the east wing entrance of legislature before the speech from the throne in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " alt= " Protesters in support of Wet'suwet'en hereditary chiefs block the entrance to the east wing entrance of legislature before the speech from the throne in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;86207;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212781 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The legislature protest came after several days in which protesters blocked highways , bridges and ports on Vancouver Island and in Metro Vancouver . Tuesday , protesters blocked the key Cambie and West Broadway intersection in Vancouver for several hours while CN Rail warned of national rail chaos if protesters continued to block the main line to the port of Prince Rupert and lines near Belleville , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When our roads are blockaded , our ports aren&amp;rsquo ; t working , our legislature is blockaded , John Horgan runs away from the protests and hides in his office , &amp;rdquo ; said Wilkinson , calling the disruptions totally inappropriate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The premier of the province has to get out and say publicly what&amp;rsquo ; s acceptable . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;86237;TOOLONG ... title= " Wet'suwet'en protesters camp out in front of legislature before the speech from the throne in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " alt= " Wet'suwet'en protesters camp out in front of legislature before the speech from the throne in Victoria , B.C. , on Tuesday , February 11 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;86273;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212493 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The throne speech contained few promises for 2020 , though it did mention additional measures to fight renovictions against renters , unspecified new investments for post-secondary training , more MRI machines for health diagnostics , and a plan to reduce plastic pollution that is expected to include a ban on single-use plastic shopping bags . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government also pledged to provide five paid work days to people fleeing domestic violence ( on top of 10 unpaid days legislated last year ) , expand funding for children with mental-health problems , raise the earned income threshold for people on disability and social assistance , and pay a previously announced Child Opportunity Benefit in October . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speech contained no update on how the NDP plans to achieve $10-a-day child care , which government has said will take a decade . Nor did it offer extra money to settle the contract dispute with the B.C. Teachers&amp;rsquo ; Federation that could lead to classroom disruptions later this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Metro Vancouver mayors may get more certainty on transportation infrastructure spending , if government follows through on a throne speech promise to extend capital planning from three years to five . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speech also mentioned upcoming legislation to ban firearms in schools &amp;mdash ; though it failed to say how that would be accomplished beyond existing federal firearms laws . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the second consecutive year , the throne speech pledged government action to reduce cellphone bills . But provincial officials have admitted it is the federal government and not the province that controls telecommunications companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Later this month , B.C. government representatives will travel to Ottawa to fight for more affordable cellphone plan options and transparency in billing , &amp;rdquo ; read the speech . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson said the lack of new ideas in the throne speech showed government was out of gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Green party house leader , and leadership candidate , Sonia Furstenau said many of the NDP accomplishments were Green ideas and only possible with Green support . &amp;ldquo ; Overall , the speech felt pretty flat and it was ultimately a pretty backwards-looking speech , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speech lacked recognition that society and economy are in transition due to climate change , said Furstenau. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The protests that gripped the building were a result of the NDP government&amp;rsquo ; s support for LNG at the expense of reducing pollution and fighting climate change , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;With files from David Carrigg&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;86303;TOOLONG " **33;86331;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Rob ShawWed , 12 Feb 2020 02:41:52 **45;86366;TOOLONG ... First Nations and environmental groups&amp;nbsp ; are promising to continue the fight against the Trans Mountain pipeline expansion . But B.C. business leaders are celebrating Tuesday&amp;rsquo ; s court decision that upheld the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s approval of the project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Tsleil-Waututh Nation , Squamish Nation , Coldwater Indian Band and a coalition of small First Nations in the Fraser Valley filed a legal challenge in the Federal Court of Appeal arguing that the government&amp;rsquo ; s consultation with them was inadequate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The court ruled in a unanimous 3-0 decision on Tuesday that the government met its duty to consult , clearing a major legal hurdle for construction to continue on the expansion of the pipeline from Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s oilsands to B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s coast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Coun. Khelsilem of the Squamish Nation told a news conference in Vancouver that oil and gas is not the future for the economy when the effects of climate change have been felt in Canada through wildfires. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When we&amp;rsquo ; re looking down the barrel of a gun &amp;hellip ; we must act swiftly and boldly , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; Australia is burning . " data-portal-copyright= " JONATHAN HAYWARD " **28;86584;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1207249 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For too long , we&amp;rsquo ; ve treated First Nations as second-class citizens . It also shows the world that Canada can get its natural resources to market in a safe way that limits environmental impacts and respects human rights , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Today&amp;rsquo ; s unanimous decision is obviously inspiring news for TMX , but more broadly this is an important development for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s resource sector . " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;86794;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 883662 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;86824;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. Utilities Commission wants to know what the public thinks about B.C. Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s application to waive charges for customers affected by evacuation orders during wildfires and other emergencies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hydro company has applied to the Commission to allow it to waive any charges for customers forced from their homes due to an evacuation order . The application , which is jointly with Fortis B.C. , was posted publicly on Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the past , the Commission approved one-off B.C. Hydro applications to provide relief in the form of bill credits to residents during the 2017 and 2018 wildfire season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The current application seeks the ability to grant emergency billing relief on the spot to any customer affected by an evacuation order &amp;mdash ; whether caused by wildfire , landslide or otherwise &amp;mdash ; during a declared state of emergency . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The billing relief would be provided only to residential customers , small businesses and farms , as they are more vulnerable to the financial impacts of evacuation orders , according to the application . Those whose homes are destroyed in a disaster would also see their charges waived up to the last effective billing period . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The emergency relief would apply to customers subject to evacuation orders extending a minimum of five consecutive days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those with opinions can submit a letter of comment , register as an interested party , or request intervener status . The deadline to register as an intervener is Jan. 29 , while the deadline to file information request with B.C. Hydro or Fortis B.C. is Feb. 5. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More information can be found at &lt;a href= " http : //www.bcuc.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **26;86860;TOOLONG . NewsB.C . NDP out of tools to stop Trans Mountain pipeline expansionhttp : **31;87167;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;87200;TOOLONG ... title= " Premier John Horgan had promised to use every tool it had to stop the pipeline expansion . Thursday , the Supreme Court of Canada left the tool box empty . " alt= " Premier John Horgan had promised to use every tool it had to stop the pipeline expansion . Thursday , the Supreme Court of Canada left the tool box empty . " data-portal-copyright= " Nick Procaylo " **28;87236;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1179533 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;87266;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the 2017 election , NDP Leader John Horgan pledged his government would &amp;ldquo ; use every tool in the tool box&amp;rdquo ; to stop the Trans Mountain pipeline expansion . On Thursday , the B.C. premier was left defeated and disappointed with no more tools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Supreme Court of Canada swiftly shut down B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s fight to regulate what can flow through an expanded Trans Mountain pipeline from Alberta . The country&amp;rsquo ; s top court unanimously agreed with a B.C. Court of Appeal ruling last May which said the province couldn&amp;rsquo ; t impose restrictions on the pipeline&amp;rsquo ; s contents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are all of the view to dismiss the appeal for the unanimous reasons of the Court of Appeal for British Columbia , &amp;rdquo ; Chief Justice Richard Wagner said after several hours of hearings Thursday in Ottawa . Usually the court reserves its decisions for consideration . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The decision removed one of the remaining obstacles for the Trans Mountain expansion , which would twin an existing pipeline running between Edmonton and Burnaby. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Clearly , we are disappointed by the decision , but this does not reduce our concerns regarding the potential of a catastrophic oil spill on our coast , " Horgan said in a statement Thursday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " When it comes to protecting our coast , our environment and our economy , we will continue do all we can within our jurisdiction . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal lawyers argued B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s attempt to require provincial permits for heavy oil to flow through pipelines was a clear imposition on the constitutional authority given to Ottawa over interprovincial pipelines. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several of the judges on the Supreme Court agreed in their statements and questions throughout Thursday&amp;rsquo ; s hearings , before issuing their quick dismissal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Attorney General David Eby also called their decision &amp;ldquo ; disappointing&amp;rdquo ; and echoed Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s pledge to fight to protect the environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " It 's &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;important to remember this was one of a suite of regulations the province put in place around toxic substances that can be brought into our province by train or pipeline or truck , " Eby said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Of that suite , there was one regulation in particular the province of Alberta &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;was concerned about so we referred that to the court . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Shahin Dashtgard , a professor in the department of earth sciences at Simon Fraser University , said Horgan has run out of options . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;87302;TOOLONG ... title= " Pipes at a Trans Mountain facility in Hope , B.C. " alt= " Pipes at a Trans Mountain facility in Hope , B.C. " data-portal-copyright= " Jonathan Hayward/The Canadian Press files " **28;87338;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1191080 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it was a political decision that he went with , an attempt to satisfy the Green party , something that he promised them from the get-go , &amp;rdquo ; said Dashtgard , referring to the NDP and Greens&amp;rsquo ; confidence and supply agreement , in which the three Green MLAs promised to support the NDP minority government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t think there was really a leg to stand on from the beginning , &amp;rdquo ; Dashtgard said . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s an interprovincial thing that&amp;rsquo ; s always been under federal jurisdiction and now that he&amp;rsquo ; s taken it all the way to the Supreme Court , that&amp;rsquo ; s the end of it , I think . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although construction has begun , there remain other barriers to the expansion&amp;rsquo ; s completion , including a legal challenge by Indigenous communities affected by the construction . They argue they were not properly consulted by Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eby said the province is not participating in those cases and &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;we do n't anticipate at this stage participating &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;in those . " &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;George Heyman , the B.C. environment minister , said in a statement that the province is &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;reviewing conditions attached to a provincial environmental certificate for the project as directed by last year 's B.C. Court of Appeal decision . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Today 's decision provides some further context for that work , " Heyman said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;B.C . Liberal Leader Andrew Wilkinson on Thursday called for the NDP government to now throw its support behind the pipeline project . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " John Horgan knew the federal government held clear jurisdiction over the pipeline but he spent millions of dollars just in political posturing , " Wilkinson said in a statement . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " When will the NDP stop the political games and let British Columbians get to work on a project supported by a majority of people in our province ? " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Wilkinson called the government 's work to stop against the project a " total waste of B.C. taxpayer money " and demanded that Eby explain how much was spent . E&lt;/span&gt; xternal legal fees alone had reached nearly $1 million by last March 31 , Wilkinson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eby said the government hasn&amp;rsquo ; t yet determined the final cost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; " But I can tell you in terms of costs versus a catastrophic oil spill , bitumen spill or another toxic substance that 's brought into the province this has been a fraction of that kind of cost and our efforts to protect our environment , " Eby said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal Liberals argue the pipeline is needed to get more Canadian oil to foreign markets beyond the United States and bought the existing pipeline with a view to completing the expansion once the legal and political challenges were out of the way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It intends to sell it back to the private sector after the expansion is complete . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta Premier Jason Kenney took immediately to Twitter to express gratitude for the court decision Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Today , the Supreme Court of Canada dismissed B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s attempt to regulate the heavy oil content in a pipeline , &amp;rdquo ; he wrote . &amp;ldquo ; We are very pleased with this outcome and look forward to construction continuing on the Trans Mountain Pipeline . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Natural Resources Minister Seamus O&amp;rsquo ; Regan said he welcomed the ruling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is a core responsibility of the federal government to help get Canada&amp;rsquo ; s resources to market and support good , middle-class jobs , &amp;rdquo ; he said in a statement Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know this is only possible when we earn public trust and work to address environmental , Indigenous peoples&amp;rsquo ; and local concerns , which we are doing every step of the way on TMX. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the B.C. NDP government was elected in 2017 partly on a promise to oppose the expansion , it had limited options because of the Constitution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , B.C. argued it has jurisdiction to protect the environment in its borders and since that province would bear the brunt of any damage from a spill if the pipeline ruptured , it should get a say in what can flow through it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In April 2018 , it filed a reference case with the B.C. Court of Appeal asking it to rule on whether the province could change its environment laws so that it could require permits for any heavy oil that was to flow through pipelines. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. Court of Appeal ruled in May 2019 that it could not , noting that would overstep the constitutional authority given to Ottawa . The court also noted while B.C. was trying to portray the legislation as a general change to its environment laws , it was clearly targeting the Trans Mountain expansion , since that was the only project to which the changes would apply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The case was the catalyst that led to the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s decision to buy the existing pipeline in May 2018 for $4.5 billion . Kinder Morgan Canada said the political risk that the project would never get built was too much to bear and was planning to halt the expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- With files from Rob Shaw and The Canadian Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;87368;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;87399;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;87437;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;87464;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Nick EaglandFri , 17 Jan 2020 17:57:18 **45;87497;TOOLONG ... NewsPoliticsBear necessities cause havoc for B.C. Hydro customers more than once in 2019http : **36;87544;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;87582;TOOLONG ... title= " File photo of a black bear . " alt= " File photo of a black bear . " data-portal-copyright= " Postmedia Archives " **28;87618;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1082961 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;87648;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; As 2019 winds to a close , B.C. Hydro is shining a light on some of the incidents that left British Columbians in the dark , many of them caused by animals behaving badly , such as an itchy bear in Williams Lake and a clumsy eagle in Clinton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bears exercising their ursine impulses knocked power out to B.C. Hydro customers not once but twice in 2019 , among the more unusual of some 50,000 trouble reports submitted to the utility , a spokesperson said Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And a bald eagle caused a failure northwest of Cache Creek when it dropped its lunch &amp;mdash ; a goose &amp;mdash ; directly onto a power line . The eagle&amp;rsquo ; s goose was cooked , and the same was true for the unfortunate Clinton customers who had to resort to candlelight until the power was restored . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wildlife incursions are not out of the ordinary for B.C. Hydro , but the more usual interruptions come from birds landing on exposed switches or critters gnawing on wires among the 25 to 30 power outages crews respond to every year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Beavers falling trees onto power lines is also not unheard of . That happened twice in 2019 , according to a list of unusual outages distributed by B.C. Hydro &amp;mdash ; once near Dawson Creek and once near Hazelton. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The largest animal-caused outage happened on Nov. 25 , &amp;rdquo ; said B.C. Hydro spokesperson Susie Rieder , &amp;ldquo ; when a rodent managed to shut off power to 10,850 B.C. Hydro customers in large areas of central and east Richmond . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rodent , presumably a squirrel , contacted a transformer and caused a converter fault at Cambie substation . Fortunately , power was restored within 90 minutes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t just animals knocking out the lights in B.C. in 2019 . Humans were also behind several of the utility&amp;rsquo ; s most memorable service calls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A customer in Surrey flew his drone into power lines , resulting in an outage at their home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A customer on Vancouver Island looking to surreptitiously spy on his neighbours climbed a power pole to install a security camera atop the structure . B.C. Hydro crews were alerted and the camera was swiftly removed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And this past summer , hunters near Stewart used a transmission tower for target practice , resulting in an outage for 170 customers and over $60,000 in damages . Two similar incidents also occurred near Coombs and near Qualicum Beach , as hunters literally shot the lights out on Vancouver Island . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Balloons were particularly problematic for B.C. Hydro as well in 2019 &amp;mdash ; a trouble that began as soon as the new year got underway . On New Year&amp;rsquo ; s Day in Richmond , a bundle of rogue balloons contacted power lines in Richmond , resulting in a power interruption to 20 customers . That was one of 52 outages caused by balloons this year , according to the utility &amp;mdash ; 53 if you count the big hot air balloon that struck power lines on descent in Vernon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; None of these incidents , however , affected as many people as the Northern Interior blackout in September , which was the single largest interruption of the year in terms of total number of customers in the dark at one time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The cause was a transmission circuit failure caused by a suspected lightning strike that left 124,000 customers without power in the Northern Interior , including Prince George , &amp;rdquo ; said Susie Rieder. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And let us not forget the February windstorm , in which 150,000 customers lost power in the Lower Mainland and Vancouver Island over the course of a very blustery day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro reminds customers to exercise caution , not just before outages but after . Anyone who happens upon a downed or damaged power line is urged to stay back at least 10 metres and report it by calling 911. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from Derrick Penner &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **28;87684;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **35;87714;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **28;87751;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow **25;87781;TOOLONG &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;87808;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? The first two of those are in Alberta , the rest are in B.C. with Spread 7 , the final section , running through the Lower Mainland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gardner said the contractor on that section , the Kiewit Ledcor Trans Mountain Expansion Partnership , hasn&amp;rsquo ; t mobilized a workforce for the work yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to see the pipeline built and hopefully now it will go ahead , &amp;rdquo ; Gardner said , &amp;ldquo ; but we&amp;rsquo ; re not seeing that much on-the-ground activity in British Columbia . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gardner said there has been enough uncertainty over the project , &amp;ldquo ; there is a degree of skepticism about some of these announcements and activities we see . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Federal Court of Appeal , in a decision in September , ruled that six First Nations could file new legal challenges of the Trans Mountain project on the question of whether the federal government has fulfilled its obligation to consult with First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judy Wilson , secretary treasurer for the Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs and Chief of the Neskonlith First Nation in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s interior , said she will &amp;ldquo ; continue doing what we need to do&amp;rdquo ; to uphold the collective rights of First Nations people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trans Mountain has signed 57 impact benefit agreements with First Nations along the pipeline&amp;rsquo ; s route , but Wilson said those agreements should only be in effect on federal First Nations reserve lands , not territorial lands outside of reserves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilson argued that Aboriginal title to territorial land still rests with the First Nations families associated with those areas , not Band Councils , and government hasn&amp;rsquo ; t received their consent , under the principles of the United Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Plus cities such as Vancouver , Burnaby and Victoria remain opposed to the project , so &amp;ldquo ; it is a wider issue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They can do all the photo ops they want , but there are substantive issues that haven&amp;rsquo ; t been dealt with , &amp;rdquo ; Wilson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hounsel said the agreements that Trans Mountain has with First Nations are for the project&amp;rsquo ; s right of way and &amp;ldquo ; demonstrate the breadth&amp;rdquo ; of support for it . She added that the agreements aren&amp;rsquo ; t closed , the company is in continuing discussions with First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll continue to build with the appropriate permits and approvals in place , &amp;rdquo ; Hounsel said . &amp;ldquo ; If there are challenges , it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t negate the approvals we have until such time as they are upheld in court . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;88154;TOOLONG ... title= " An aerial view of construction progress on the terminal and tank farm on Burnaby Mountain for Trans Mountain 's pipeline expansion as of November , 2019. NewsFortune beams on Tsilhqot'in solar-power project on remote Chilcotin plateauhttp : **33;88396;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;88431;TOOLONG ... title= " Aerial photos of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm , a 1.25 megawatt independent power project owned by the First Nation group , located 80 km west of Williams Lake on Highway 20. " alt= " Aerial photos of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm , a 1.25 megawatt independent power project owned by the First Nation group , located 80 km west of Williams Lake on Highway 20. " data-portal-copyright= " Tsilhqot'in National Government " **28;88467;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1146595 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;88497;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in solar farm emerges as 16 neat rows of silvery panels on the tawny and green-mottled Chilcotin Plateau&amp;rsquo ; s landscape just off Highway 20 , about 80 kilometres west of Williams Lake on the drive to Bella Coola. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Made up of 3,456 solar modules perched at the edge of an old sawmill site , the recently completed facility is rare &amp;mdash ; one of a handful of small independent power projects to go ahead before the province hit pause on B.C. Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s program for such projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it represents a hopeful model&amp;nbsp ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;for the &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;solar power needed to fill in the province 's future power needs and to generate economic opportunities for remote First Nations -- once government figures out how it wants to proceed with such projects . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For us , ( the project demonstrates ) that we can be leaders in clean energy , &amp;rdquo ; said Chief Russell Myers Ross , vice-chair of the Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in National Government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re sort of unsure at least where B.C. Hydro stands , but we still are trying to have a relationship with the province to try and pursue other energy ( opportunities ) , &amp;rdquo ; for other Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in communities , Myers Ross added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;88533;TOOLONG ... title= " Aerial photos of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm , a 1.25 megawatt independent power project owned by the First Nation group , located 80 km west of Williams Lake on Highway 20. " alt= " Aerial photos of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm , a 1.25 megawatt independent power project owned by the First Nation group , located 80 km west of Williams Lake on Highway 20. " data-portal-copyright= " Tsilhqot'in National Government " **28;88569;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1146595 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The idea for the approximately $2.6 million solar facility started with discussions Myers Ross had with B.C. Hydro , as chief of the Yunesit&amp;rsquo ; in First Nation , about ways to give his community some energy security in the face of rising electricity costs on their reserve . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the old sawmill site , which lies outside of the Yunesit&amp;rsquo ; in&amp;rsquo ; s immediate territory , proved to be a better site for sunshine , with ready access to Highway 20 and direct access to a power transmission line that wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t require expensive upgrading , said Myers Ross . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So the solar farm was developed , along with the non-profit corporation EcoSmart , as a project for the overall Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in National Government , which involved training and labour for members of all six of its First Nations communities , which is a point of pride for Myers Ross . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It represents the first successful project , moving from ( beginning ) to completion , to actually generate revenue , &amp;rdquo ; Myers Ross said . &amp;ldquo ; Even though it&amp;rsquo ; s not a lot of revenue , it&amp;rsquo ; s significant in that it is , finally , own-source revenue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;88599;TOOLONG ... title= " Chief Russell Myers Ross , vice-chair of the Tsilhqot'in National Government , alongside solar panels that are part of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm . " alt= " Chief Russell Myers Ross , vice-chair of the Tsilhqot'in National Government , alongside solar panels that are part of the Tsilhqot'in solar farm . " data-portal-copyright= " Tsilhqot'in National Government " **28;88635;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1146594 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in are still waiting for B.C. Hydro to complete its connection to link the solar farm to its grid , the final step before power starts to flow , which a spokeswoman said should be within the next couple of months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The First Nation has a 25-year contract for the 1.25 megawatt solar farm to supply up to 2.5 gigawatt-hours a year of electricity , enough to power the equivalent of about 250 homes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in solar farm was one of five projects to proceed under B.C. Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s standing-offer program for small power-generation projects as part of an impact-benefit agreement with First Nations , said spokeswoman Tanya Fish . The standing-offer program was indefinitely suspended in February . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other First Nations interested in solar power , the Upper Nicola Indian Band is pursuing a proposal for a more substantial 15 megawatt solar farm on its Quilchena reserve near Merritt with Fortis B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For now , the Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in project is just the second utility-sized solar plant that B.C. Hydro is putting on the grid , Fish said . The other is the Kimberly SunMine solar farm , which is also small &amp;mdash ; about 1.1 megawatts generating about 2.1 gigawatt hours of electricity a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Fish said 36 other First Nations have incorporated solar installations into their energy plans under B.C. Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s net metering program , which allows customers to generate power that offsets their own power needs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the program allows customers to feed electricity back into B.C Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s system , and earn credits for doing so when their solar panels generate more power than they are using , Fish said the utility doesn&amp;rsquo ; t intend for net metering to become a source of provincial grid supply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In total , Fish said some 2,400 Hydro customers have enlisted with its net metering program , mostly with small solar-power systems , generating about 17 megawatt-hours of power a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. , with some particularly sun-scorched Interior locations , has great potential for large solar projects , according to Martin Mullany , the interim executive director at Clean Energy B.C. , the main industry organization that represents private-power producers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The first lesson is that solar works in B.C. , &amp;rdquo ; Mullany said of the Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in project , which serves an example for other communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the Tsilhqot&amp;rsquo ; in , Mullany said that besides providing a business opportunity to the First Nation , having a source of electric power close to their communities helps stabilize the power grid for the entire region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The cost of ( solar ) technology has dropped 90 per cent in the last 10 years , Mullany added . He said that could revive the prospects for First Nations that examined solar options about five years ago only to discover it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t viable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If they re-examined them today , they may find they are economically viable , &amp;rdquo ; Mullany said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First Nations might also be helped by a recent &lt;a href= " https : **33;88665;TOOLONG ... Utilities Commission decision&lt;/a&gt; that paves the way for their governments to establish their own utilities , though with limits that would prevent them from selling power off their reserves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judith Sayers , a board member at Clean Energy B.C. and president of the Nuu-chah-nulth Tribal Council on Vancouver Island , &lt;a href= " https : **33;88700;TOOLONG ... Postmedia&lt;/a&gt; that the decision falls short of First Nations&amp;rsquo ; needs for economic development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;88735;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;88766;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;88804;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;88833;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerTue , 03 Dec 2019 18:18:21 **45;88864;TOOLONG ... NewsNumber of electric vehicles on B.C. roads on the rise : provincehttp : **32;88911;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;88945;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. is ahead of its goals when it comes to electric vehicle adoption . " alt= " B.C. is ahead of its goals when it comes to electric vehicle adoption . " data-portal-copyright= " Patrick T. Fallon/Bloomberg " **28;88981;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1053799 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;89011;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 30,000 electric vehicles are now on the road in B.C. , where the provincial government has set goals of reducing greenhouse-gas emissions by 60 per cent and allowing sales of only zero-emission new vehicles by 2040. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2019 , 10 years after the first electric vehicles hit the road in B.C. , they make up nine per cent of light-duty vehicles that are being sold in the province , according to the provincial figures . That&amp;rsquo ; s from four per cent in 2018 and ahead of the targets set under B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s climate strategy , which was released late last year . Five per cent of the electric cars sold this year were fleet vehicles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The strategy , called CleanBC , had electric vehicles &amp;mdash ; which include battery electric and plug-in hybrid electric &amp;mdash ; making up 10 per cent of light-duty sales by 2025 . The plan also stipulates that by 2040 all new vehicles sold in B.C. must be zero-emission vehicles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We expect that we&amp;rsquo ; re going to be five years ahead of our own schedule , &amp;rdquo ; said&amp;nbsp ; Energy Minister Michelle Mungall. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the New Car Dealers Association of B.C. , more than half of the electric vehicles sold in the province were battery electric ( 65 per cent ) , with the rest being plug-in hybrid , with or without range extenders . Between January and October this year , 47 per cent of the approved applications for the provincial incentive were for Teslas. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " visualisation/1011709 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; ... &lt;p&gt; Electric vehicle sales in B.C. are the highest per capita in North America , ahead of Quebec at seven per cent and California at eight per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although 30,000 vehicles on the road and nine per cent of new vehicles sold may not sound like a lot , New Car Dealers Association CEO Blair Qualey said compared to many other jurisdictions , B.C. is doing well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re rocking it here , &amp;rdquo ; said Qualey. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province set new greenhouse-gas emissions targets last spring . Compared to 2007 levels , the goal is to reduce emissions by 40 per cent by 2030 , 60 per cent by 2040 and 80 per cent by 2050. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mungall said the transportation sector is one of the biggest contributors to greenhouse-gas emissions , and although larger vehicles tend to be the worst emitters , switching personal vehicles to zero emissions can help the province meet its goals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;89047;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. Energy Minister Michelle Mungall. " alt= " B.C. Energy Minister Michelle Mungall. " data-portal-copyright= " Don Craig Government Communica " **28;89083;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1156497 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Emissions that we produce with our vehicles are often some of the biggest emissions that we produce as individuals and so that contribution that every single one of us can make is important to our overall reduction in our emissions , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mungall attributes the increase in electric vehicles on the road , at least in part , to the rebates the provincial and federal governments are offering , which can reduce the price of a new electric vehicle by around $8,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think that the rebate really helped where people are making the decision they need to buy a new car , so are they going to choose between electric vehicle , which is what they would like to do , or do they choose between an internal-combustion engine vehicle that might be a little less expensive on the ticket price , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neil MacEachern , program manager , sustainable transportation , for the Fraser Basin Council , which manages the Plug in B.C. program , agreed that rebates take the &amp;ldquo ; sting&amp;rdquo ; out of the initial purchase cost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think generally British Columbians tend to be environmentally conscientious , and a good chunk of the population is interested in moving to cleaner transportation , and it has to fit in their budget and their needs , &amp;rdquo ; MacEachern said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;89113;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; ... Click here . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the province , there are about 44 electric vehicle models for sale in B.C. , with most of them priced in the $30,000 to $50,000 range , before rebates . Installing a charging station at home can cost between $700 and $2,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are three vehicle incentive programs in B.C. , plus a specialty-use vehicle incentive . The federal iZEV program offers up to $5,000 off a new battery-electric vehicle , $2,500 to $5,000 on a plug-in hybrid and up to $5,000 for a hydrogen fuel cell vehicle . A provincial program offers up to $3,000 off a new battery-electric vehicle , up to $1,500 off a plug-in hybrid and up to $3,000 for a hydrogen fuel cell vehicle . B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s SCRAP-IT program offers incentives for up to $6,000 for a new and $3,000 for a used qualifying electric vehicle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province also offers &lt;a href= " http : **34;89360;TOOLONG ... for installing high-speed charging stations&lt;/a&gt; in single-family homes , condo and apartment buildings and workplaces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Qualey said cost is a key issue for consumers , and until there is parity between the cost of electric and gas vehicles , that will continue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s why the incentives and continuing them are so important , &amp;rdquo ; he said , noting that incentives will be key to the province achieving its goals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While buying an electric vehicle and installing charging infrastructure can be expensive , owners will save on fuel &amp;mdash ; about $1,800 a year , according to the provincial government &amp;mdash ; and maintenance compared to gas vehicles because there are fewer moving parts . Electric vehicle drivers who apply for and receive a special permit can also drive in HOV lanes , even if there is only one person in the car . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;89396;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? Cars - Driving UnpluggedEnergyLocal NewsB.C. introduces bill to force companies to hand over gas price datahttp : **36;89539;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;89577;TOOLONG ... title= " Proposed B.C. legislation to provide transparency on gasoline prices will do little to lower the cost at the pump , says an expert . " alt= " Proposed B.C. legislation to provide transparency on gasoline prices will do little to lower the cost at the pump , says an expert . " data-portal-copyright= " Peter J Thompson/Financial Post " **28;89613;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1054722 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;89643;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; Oil and gas companies will be forced to hand over sensitive data on fuel imports , supply and gasoline pricing , under proposed legislation introduced at the B.C. legislature Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Fuel Transparency Act will help in &amp;ldquo ; pulling back the curtain&amp;rdquo ; on sudden unexplained hikes to gasoline prices , which have occurred several times in the past year , said Trade Minister Bruce Ralston. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This legislation brings us greater transparency at the gas pumps and sends a message to the oil and gas companies that the days of setting your gas prices in secrecy are coming to an end , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government hopes that forcing oil and gas companies to provide pricing data might make them less eager to abruptly hike the price of gasoline by as much as 10 cents overnight to hit prices in the Lower Mainland that are among the highest in Canada , as has occurred in the past year . However , experts say the move is &lt;a href= " https : **33;89679;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;unlikely to reduce gasoline prices&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ralston pointed to two recent investigations by the B.C. Utilities Commission that found an unexplained &lt;a href= " https : **33;89714;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;10 to 13 cents a litre&lt;/a&gt; additional markup on gasoline at B.C. retail pumps , which is costing motorists an additional $490 million a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ralston has called this gouging by oil and gas companies , though the commission stopped short of using such terminology and instead pointed to a quasi-monopoly by approximately five companies in B.C. that control almost all the gasoline supply and retail outlets , which reduces competitive pricing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;89749;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;89791;TOOLONG ... are we being charged so much ? ' Comparing gas prices across B.C. causes **30;89826;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;89858;TOOLONG ... Utilities Commission . The schedule would be decided later by cabinet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The goal , said Ralston , is to begin &lt;a href= " https : **33;89922;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;collecting a standard set of data&lt;/a&gt; that will allow government to properly evaluate future policy measures to ensure gas prices are fair at the pump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most of that data will be made public , Ralston said . However , some proprietary or sensitive corporate data might be kept confidential . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The proposed legislation , if passed , would also allow for inspections , document seizures and penalties of up to $1 million for companies and officials who fail to comply with providing documents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;89957;TOOLONG " **33;89985;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;90020;TOOLONG " &gt;t ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;90049;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; ... BusinessLocal NewsPoliticsNatural gas drilling credits eat up royalty revenue from extraction industry : reporthttp : **34;90343;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;90379;TOOLONG ... title= " Gas is flared as waste from a shale formation where gas and oil extraction using hydraulic fracturing , or fracking , takes place in California . Oil and Gas Commission . " alt= " Gas is flared as waste from a shale formation where gas and oil extraction using hydraulic fracturing , or fracking , takes place in California . Deep well and horizontal drilling is used extensively in fracking , which was used to extract natural gas from 98 per cent of wells brought into production in 2017 , according to the B.C. Oil and Gas Commission . " data-portal-copyright= " David McNew " **28;90415;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1143456 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Credits issued by the provincial government to natural gas companies could cost taxpayers more than $2 billion in foregone revenue , according to the Canadian Centre for Policy Alternatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between 2016 and 2018 , about $1.2 billion in credits for deep-well and horizontal drilling were issued to gas producers , who can use them to reduce future natural gas royalty payments to the government when wells go into production . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. collected $145 million in natural gas royalties in the 2017-18 fiscal year and $164 million in 2018-19 , according to the budget and fiscal plan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The deep well credit program has been in place for 17 years to compensate companies for the higher costs associated with so-called unconventional gas production , which is now so common that the credits are an &amp;ldquo ; embedded subsidy&amp;rdquo ; to the industry , said CCPA policy analyst Ben Parfitt. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Is the government lowering royalty fees and effectively propping up fossil fuel extraction that would otherwise be unprofitable ? &amp;rdquo ; wonders a report by the CCPA , a progressive think tank . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The top three credit earners in the gas field in 2017-18 accrued $344 million in credits : Cutbank Encana Partnership , Painted Petroleum and Tourmaline Oil Corp . In all , 26 companies earned $703 million in deep well credits last year , the documents show . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas producers have $2.6 billion in credits already on the books , according to &lt;a href= " https : **33;90445;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;B.C. ' s most recent public accounts report&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These are revenues that are being foregone , &amp;rdquo ; said Parfitt , who obtained credit figures with a Freedom of Information request . &amp;ldquo ; Down the line , those credits are reimbursed in the form of lower royalty payments . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The program was initiated as an incentive to take on more expensive extraction projects , which Parfitt argues have now become mainstream . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Deep well and horizontal drilling is used extensively in hydraulic fracturing , which was used to extract natural gas from 98 per cent of wells brought into production in 2017 , according to the B.C. Oil and Gas Commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Virtually all the new wells being drilled in B.C. are deep wells and horizontal wells for to extract natural gas and valuable liquids such as condensate , which is used to dilute bitumen , &amp;rdquo ; said Parfitt. &amp;ldquo ; But when it comes time to pay the province &amp;mdash ; the taxpayers &amp;mdash ; for those extracting those products , the royalties are reduced . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government&amp;rsquo ; s three-year fiscal plan notes that the royalty rate is expected to decline in the next two years due to &amp;ldquo ; increased utilization of royalty programs and infrastructure credits . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we would like to know is how much those ( gas ) companies are actually paying in royalties for a publicly owned resource , and it&amp;rsquo ; s been like pulling teeth with this government , &amp;rdquo ; said Parfitt. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What forest companies pay for cutting trees on public lands is freely available to the public online , he noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Energy Minister Michelle Mungall defended the program earlier this year , noting that companies can only use credits to reduce their royalty rate , not to avoid royalties altogether . She added , &amp;ldquo ; many of the credits that ( Green party Leader Andrew Weaver ) speaks of will actually likely never be used as the wells are closed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;With a file from Vaughn Palmer&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;90480;TOOLONG " **34;90509;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;90545;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;90587;TOOLONG ...
