Email &lt;a href= " **28;90748;TOOLONG " **35;90778;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Randy ShoreWed , 13 Nov 2019 12:00:58 **45;90815;TOOLONG ... News'Something wrong ' : Horgan says B.C. 's gas report shows pricing market flawedhttp : **34;90862;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;90898;TOOLONG ... title= " Motorists are still paying for an unexplained 13 cents a litre margin on the price of gas at the pump , according to a report by the independent energy regulator. " alt= " Motorists are still paying for an unexplained 13 cents a litre margin on the price of gas at the pump , according to a report by the independent energy regulator. " data-portal-copyright= " Mike Bell " **28;90934;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1140236 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;90964;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; Premier John Horgan says a new report by the province&amp;rsquo ; s independent energy regulator has failed to clear up confusion over gasoline profit margins and has only intensified his worry that consumers are getting gouged at the pumps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " Clearly there is something wrong with the gas market in British Columbia and we want to get to the bottom of that , " Horgan told reporters on Tuesday , following the release of a second report by the B.C. Utilities Commission that did not clarify an unexplained 13-cent-a-litre margin on the price of gasoline at the pumps . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Horgan said he plans to take the issue to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau because the federal government can act on anti-competitive practices . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the federal government has a role to play as well , &amp;rdquo ; said Horgan. &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not just about supply and we need more gasoline &amp;hellip ; we need to have more gasoline here , but why are we paying 13 cents more a litre than anywhere else in the country ? Those are questions that still remain unanswered . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Horgan government is &lt;a href= " https : **33;91000;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;readying legislation&lt;/a&gt; that would force oil and gas companies to hand over sensitive supply and pricing data to provincial regulators on a regular basis , in order to better understand why gas prices can suddenly spike without warning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That kind of legislation will only result in more delays , as will appealing to Ottawa to intervene , said Opposition Liberal leader Andrew Wilkinson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve seen nothing but excuses , &amp;rdquo ; said Wilkinson , who urged a cut to provincial gas taxes and efforts to boost supply of gasoline from pipelines. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government forbid the utilities commission from examining the impact of provincial fuel taxes or pipeline policies on the price of gasoline in the two reports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;91035;TOOLONG ... title= " Premier John Horgan. " alt= " Premier John Horgan. " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;91071;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1120806 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;The utilities commission concluded in its supplementary report Tuesday that oil and gas companies provided inconclusive or conflicting data&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; to explain the &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;13-cent-a-litre margin customers pay at retail stations &lt;/span&gt;that were first identified in &lt;a href= " https : **33;91101;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;an August report&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It also noted B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s gas supply and retail outlets are owned by a small number of companies , creating an oligopoly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The commission reviewed new submissions from five major B.C. companies : Parkland Fuel Corporation , the owner of Metro Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s only refinery , located in Burnaby ; Imperial Oil Limited ; Suncor Energy ; 7-Eleven Canada and Advanced Biofuels Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The commission concluded that the price of all gas in B.C. is set by the most expensive five per cent of the province&amp;rsquo ; s supply , from Seattle , called the Pacific Northwest Spot Price . That creates a profit margin for companies that get cheaper gas from Alberta , or elsewhere . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second margin is the difference between Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s price and Seattle&amp;rsquo ; s price , which has grown to 20 cents a litre since 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas companies cited increased costs for transportation , B.C. and federal fuel standards , overhead , U.S. credits , exchange rates , inflation and other factors , but the commission said that still left 10 to 13 cents per litre unexplained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s 13 cents more that they are charging for something , presumably , they don&amp;rsquo ; t have to pay for , &amp;rdquo ; said commission chair David Morton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The unexplained margin costs B.C. motorists approximately $490 million annually at the pumps , according to the commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 13-cent price difference can be better explained if you consider B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s scarcity premium due to a limit in refining and pipeline capacity , as well as the profit margin built in for oil companies due to B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; oligopolistic market structure , &amp;rdquo ; said Werner Antwiler , associate professor of the University of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s Sauder School of Business , who researches gas prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " https : **34;91136;TOOLONG ... &lt;div style= " width:100% ! ; margin-top:4px ! important ; text-align:right ! important ; " &gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;91172;TOOLONG ... target= " top " class= " flourish-credit " &gt;&lt;img style= " width:105px ! important ; height:16px ! important ; border:none ! important ; margin:0 ! important ; " src= " https : **34;91207;TOOLONG ... alt= " Made with Flourish " **26;91243;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The oil and gas companies had at first fought the commission to avoid handing over sensitive data on profit margins and supply . Eventually , after subsequent security assurances , they did provide some of that information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Morton said during the supplementary phase of the report , released Tuesday , that the companies provided only hypothetical data to explain why the 13 cent price gap was incorrect , and not actual invoices and costs the companies incurred in the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " The oil companies that made those arguments were not able to provide any single piece of evidence that would persuade us that was the case , " said Morton . " All they did was send us a spreadsheet , this is what a hypothetical gas company would look like . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " They did n't send us an invoice and say this is what it costs to provide a shipment in . There was no concrete evidence provided . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " At the end of the day we said we really do n't have anything definitive , so we 're not going to back off this 13-cent number . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;91271;TOOLONG ... title= " PetroCan fuelling station at East Broadway and Clark " alt= " PetroCan fuelling station at East Broadway and Clark " data-portal-copyright= " Arlen Redekop " **28;91307;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1061507 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The commission reports were less clear in breaking down the cost structure at the retail level of gas stations , which are mostly owned and operated by the major companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Morton said retail gas pricing , which starts at a daily high around 10 a.m. and lowers to a 6 p.m. price , was impossible to break down with the data provided . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t really know what the full story is on retail sales , &amp;rdquo ; he said . Morton cited protests in Squamish and Powell River over higher gas prices than elsewhere in the province , and was unable to explain why . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The retail side warrants more investigation , he said . But the NDP government&amp;rsquo ; s cabinet order giving the commission jurisdiction to investigate gasoline pricing expired on the weekend , and it can do no further work without provincial approval . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Morton said he would be pleased if government did pass legislation that gave his commission more routine data from the oil and gas companies on supply and pricing , but it would need to come with a mandate from government to do something with that information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Certainly if we were asked to participate further in trying to unravel this mystery , so to speak , yes it would be very helpful . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;91337;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **33;91365;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;91400;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;91429;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;91460;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;91502;TOOLONG ... preps ' fuel transparency ' bill to track gas price **28;91537;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;91567;TOOLONG ... inquiry finds B.C. motorists paying at least 13 cents a litre too **25;91602;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;91629;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Stuart McNish " **28;91916;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1116513 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;91946;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A group of North Coast First Nations believes it&amp;rsquo ; s possible to export B.C. liquefied natural gas while meeting Canada&amp;rsquo ; s climate-change goals &amp;mdash ; with &amp;ldquo ; the **31;91982;TOOLONG ; possible LNG projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think there is a balance that can be reached , if we work together , where we can have the cleanest , greenest projects go ahead , at the same time addressing some of those ( social ) problems that we face , &amp;rdquo ; said John Helin , mayor of the Lax Kw&amp;rsquo ; alaams band , based near Prince Rupert . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Lax Kw&amp;rsquo ; alaams , Metlakatla , Nisga&amp;rsquo ; a and Haisla First Nations signed a memorandum of understanding Wednesday under the name of the Northwest Coast First Nations Collaborative Climate Initiative . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Helin said the effort will work toward a &amp;ldquo ; shared vision&amp;rdquo ; with the provincial and federal governments to displace coal-fired electrical power generation elsewhere in the world with LNG produced in B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That , however , is contingent upon countries finalizing implementation of an article of the Paris Agreement on climate to allow countries that cut emissions by displacing coal to share their emission credits with the countries that produced the natural gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Haisla Nation already has a 25-year export licence from the National Energy Board and proposed a floating , liquid natural gas facility south of Kitimat . The participating First Nations and their territories are all in northwestern B.C. , generally in the area between Prince Rupert and Kitimat. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our territories aren&amp;rsquo ; t in a bubble , &amp;rdquo ; said Haisla Chief Crystal Smith , &amp;ldquo ; and aren&amp;rsquo ; t protected by what&amp;rsquo ; s happening in China and Japan . We need to have real solutions for the global impacts of ( greenhouse-gas emissions ) , and that&amp;rsquo ; s what we as leaders are working together to provide so that what we live off of , which has sustained us from time immemorial in our traditional territories , has a chance to survive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;92015;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman and her daughter from the Haisla First Nation spend time along the Douglas Channel in Kitamaat Village . The Haisla are one of the 25 Indigenous groups that signed agreements with LNG Canada and Coastal GasLink. " alt= " A woman and her daughter from the Haisla First Nation spend time along the Douglas Channel in Kitamaat Village . The Haisla are one of the 25 Indigenous groups that signed agreements with LNG Canada and Coastal GasLink. " data-portal-copyright= " Supplied " **28;92051;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 941613 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the same time , Smith said the Haisla want to have &amp;ldquo ; a stake and a say&amp;rdquo ; in resource developments happening in their territory and the community is already seeing transformational benefits from the Shell-Canada-led $18 billion LNG Canada development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have the opportunity in front of us as Haisla to share these opportunities with our neighbours , such as Lax Kw&amp;rsquo ; alaams and Nisga&amp;rsquo ; a , &amp;rdquo ; Smith said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Helin said the memorandum is just the start of a process that will involve discussions with government and First Nations in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s northeast , where natural gas is produced , the so-called upstream segment of the industry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a lot of concern out there and we do have a lot of different points of view on the subject , &amp;rdquo ; Helin said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In January , protesters representing five of 13 clans in the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en First Nation blocked the construction route for the Coastal GasLink pipeline , which would supply the LNG Canada plant , highlighting the concerns of other Aboriginal communities over its climate impact and hydraulic fracturing or fracking. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , with an obligation to alleviate poverty in their communities , Helin said First Nations should be able to make decisions about resource development &amp;ldquo ; based on good science and clean energy , &amp;rdquo ; that finds a middle ground , if there is one to be found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; LNG development remains contentious because a plant such as LNG Canada would increase B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s greenhouse-gas emissions , particularly through fugitive emissions in natural-gas drilling , which environmentalists argue makes the fuel as harmful to the atmosphere as coal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;92081;TOOLONG ... title= " Site preparations in Kitimat began almost a year ago and construction will soon begin on a processing plant that is designed to produce the lowest greenhouse gas emissions of any LNG project operating in the world today . Supplied " alt= " Site preparations in Kitimat began almost a year ago and construction will soon begin on a processing plant that is designed to produce the lowest greenhouse gas emissions of any LNG project operating in the world today . Supplied " data-portal-copyright= " Supplied " **28;92117;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1092422 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the federal political sphere , Green party Leader Elizabeth May is campaigning on a platform that would ban hydraulic fracturing , the drilling method used to extract natural gas from shale rock , in part because of methane emissions . Accounting for such methane emissions and establishing whether fuel from a B.C. LNG plant would enable China or Japan to achieve a net reduction in GhGs would have to be part of the process , said Alex Grzybowski , a facilitator hired by the First Nations to gather technical information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Gryzbowski said they&amp;rsquo ; re proceeding on the basis that if northeast gas fields are electrified , the fuel is drilled and LNG is chilled using renewable electricity , the exported gas could displace enough coal power to reduce greenhouse-gas emissions by the equivalent of all of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s carbon-dioxide emissions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If it&amp;rsquo ; s possible for an LNG project to go forward that enables Canada and British Columbia to exceed our ( emission-reduction ) targets and enables these leaders to alleviate poverty in their communities , why wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t we do that ? , &amp;rdquo ; Gryzbowski said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The North Coast First Nations unveiled their initiative alongside the World Indigenous Business Forum happening at the Vancouver Convention Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Maynard Johnny , a Coast Salish and Kwakwaka&amp;rsquo ; wakw artist taking part in an artisans&amp;rsquo ; gallery at the event , said the memorandum brings up a lot of issues that need to be balanced in resource development . Johnny said he understands the taps of energy development can&amp;rsquo ; t simply be turned off instantly , but First Nations need to learn to &amp;ldquo ; work within the beast . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within that , Johnny said industry needs to listen more to the concerns of First Nations and put more effort into reducing the need for potentially damaging practices such as fracking with a view to eventually ending them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Before ( European ) contact we lived off the earth , the earth provided us a living , &amp;rdquo ; Johnny said . &amp;ldquo ; We didn&amp;rsquo ; t own it , it owned us . Without nature and the animals , the ocean and the land , we wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have survived . I think we need to get somewhere close back to that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;92147;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;92178;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;92216;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;92245;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;mdash ; With files from Randy Shore , Postmedia News &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerThu , 10 Oct 2019 01:33:00 **45;92276;TOOLONG ... NewsTrans Mountain pipeline expansion expected to be big issue in Burnaby North -- Seymour ridinghttp : **34;92323;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;92359;TOOLONG ... title= " The Westridge Marine Terminal , the terminus of the Trans Mountain Pipeline , in Burrard Inlet in Burnaby. " alt= " The Westridge Marine Terminal , the terminus of the Trans Mountain Pipeline , in Burrard Inlet in Burnaby. " data-portal-copyright= " JASON REDMOND " **28;92395;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1111310 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;92425;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this federal election , Burnaby North&amp;ndash ; Seymour is ground zero for the oil pipeline debate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s the terminus for the $9.3-billion Trans Mountain oil pipeline expansion that will increase oil tanker traffic in the Burrard Inlet and increase the size of an existing oil tank farm on Burnaby Mountain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Burrard Inlet runs through the riding , redrawn in the last election , and includes Burnaby north of the Lougheed Highway and part of the District of North Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The riding is where hundreds of people have been arrested violating a B.C. Supreme Court injunction that was meant to keep protesters from hampering construction work . &amp;nbsp ; It&amp;rsquo ; s also the home of the Tsleil-Waututh First Nation whose people are adamantly opposed to the project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Liberals &amp;mdash ; who promised in the last election that projects such as this can not go ahead without community or First Nation support &amp;mdash ; bought the pipeline for $4.5 billion last year . It did so because it was worried that U.S.-based Kinder Morgan was going to pull the plug on the expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earlier , the Trudeau government approved Trans Mountain , at the same time killing the Northern Gateway oil pipeline project . Along with a national carbon tax and pledges by Albert to cap oilsands emissions and phase out coal-generated power , the Liberals argued it balanced environmental and economic imperatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;92461;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;92497;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; With parties staking out starkly different positions on the pipeline and climate policy , Simon Fraser University political scientist Cara Camcastle believes there is a distinct possibility those issues will be a major factor in this riding . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It depends on how important is the environment and the climate issue&amp;rdquo ; is to voters , said Camcastle. &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s more than just the pipeline . Where are we going to put our resources ? Where is the emphasis going to be as a society &amp;mdash ; as a government ? Is it away from fossil fuels ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recent election polls have pegged climate change as a key issue for voters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The website&amp;nbsp ; &lt;a href= " http : //338canada.com/ " &gt;338Canada.com ... &amp;mdash ; which makes projections based on polling , electoral history and demographic data &amp;mdash ; this week forecast the Burnaby North&amp;mdash ; Seymour riding as a toss-up. &amp;nbsp ; It has the Liberals leading with the Conservatives in second place , followed by the NDP and the Greens . The margin of error of the polling is quite large , however . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Liberal candidate Terry Beech rode in on the wave of Trudeau support in the 2015 election , however , his margin of victory over an NDP candidate was less than six per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;92533;TOOLONG ... title= " Burnaby North-Seymour MP Terry Beech , in his Parliament Hill office " alt= " Burnaby North-Seymour MP Terry Beech , in his Parliament Hill office " data-portal-copyright= " Charlie Croskery " **28;92569;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 506380 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Beech voted against the Trans Mountain expansion in parliament to reflect the &amp;ldquo ; on balance&amp;rdquo ; opposition of his constituents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Beech says it&amp;rsquo ; s important to remember that east of his riding , the majority of voters support the project , and some in his own riding support it , too . Because his government wants the project built , he said he has focused on marine and tanker issues , climate change and the tank farm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He noted there are also housing affordability and traffic concerns in his riding , both of which have been met with investments . &amp;ldquo ; If you look at our government through that lens , investing in Canadians , you can see a lot of what our approach is , &amp;rdquo ; said Beech . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative candidate Heather Leung , who is not talking to media , has taken a pro-pipeline stance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NDP candidate Svend Robinson is mounting a comeback after his political career ended in 2004 when he admitted to stealing a valuable ring from an auction house . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;92599;TOOLONG ... title= " Svend Robinson addresses supporters in Burnaby , BC. , January 13 , 2019. " alt= " Svend Robinson addresses supporters in Burnaby , BC. , January 13 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Nick Procaylo " **28;92635;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 943223 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robinson said he is hearing from voters there is a great sense of the betrayal by the Liberal government and Beech over Trans Mountain . &amp;ldquo ; Lots of people who believed the promises &amp;mdash ; who voted Liberals last time &amp;mdash ; are saying that&amp;rsquo ; s it . They are supporting my candidacy , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Greens , represented by Amita Kuttner , oppose the Trans Mountain expansion . The People&amp;rsquo ; s Party of Canada , whose candidate is Rocky Dong , supports the project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As for affordability , Murray Martin , a spokesperson for Burnaby ACORN ( Association of Community Organizations for Reform Now ) , said he has been disappointed by the lack of discussion about the high cost of renting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renters might be concerned about the pipeline , but it&amp;rsquo ; s an abstract issue when you are paying more than half your income in rent , said Martin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;92665;TOOLONG " **37;92697;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;92736;TOOLONG " &gt; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;92766;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; ... Click here . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; Gordon HoekstraFri , 04 Oct 2019 18:36:14 **45;93167;TOOLONG ... 2019EnergyLocal NewsB.C. municipalities brace for north-south split on climate-change lawsuitshttp : **32;93214;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;93248;TOOLONG ... title= " An undated aerial photo of the Chevron Refinery in Burnaby. " alt= " An undated aerial photo of the Chevron Refinery in Burnaby. " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;93284;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 364464 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;93314;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A simmering north-south schism threatens to flare up at the Union of B.C. Municipalities conference with back-to-back resolutions on the agenda , both for and against suing oil companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Victoria city council wants the provincial government to pave a legal pathway for lawsuits against oil companies in the hope of recovering some of its climate adaptation costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 20 municipalities &amp;mdash ; nearly all of them in southwestern B.C. &amp;mdash ; have already voted to send letters to multinational oil companies asking to be compensated for their climate-change related expenses . The City of Vancouver has sent 20 such letters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Fort St. John has submitted a resolution to the UBCM calling on local governments to support the energy sector while the province transitions to a low-carbon economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city contends that letter writing , lawsuits and other &amp;ldquo ; symbolic&amp;rdquo ; climate-related actions are inappropriate and are likely to &amp;ldquo ; hinder rather than help&amp;rdquo ; communities trying to cope with climate change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That resolution is endorsed by the North Central Local Government Association , which includes Terrace , Prince George , Prince Rupert , Dawson Creek and about three dozen other communities in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s energy heartland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;93350;TOOLONG ... title= " Fort St. John Mayor Lori Ackerman , pictured in 2016. " alt= " Fort St. John Mayor Lori Ackerman , pictured in 2016. " data-portal-copyright= " Derrick Penner " **28;93386;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1096993 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our relationship with oil companies is different because we understand the realities , &amp;rdquo ; said Fort St. John Mayor Lori Ackerman. &amp;ldquo ; We aren&amp;rsquo ; t extracting , producing and shipping petroleum products for our own use . We ship it because there is a demand . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Northern communities are aware of climate change and understand that humans play a role in that , she said , but added that Canadian energy companies are world leaders in efficiency , with tougher environmental standards than other fossil fuel-producing regions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are ways of leaving a lighter footprint , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; We have a regulatory environment that marries an industry with the environmental demands of this province . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Litigation will lead to significant legal costs for taxpayers and settlements will be paid for by consumers with higher energy prices , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;93416;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. Environment Minister George Heyman. " alt= " B.C. Environment Minister George Heyman. " data-portal-copyright= " Arlen Redekop " **28;93452;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1098395 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; West Coast Environmental Law has been campaigning for more than two years to encourage local governments to send climate compensation letters to the 20 biggest oil companies in the world , said WCEL staff lawyer Andrew Gage . None of those companies is based in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gage has also urged councils to support a class-action lawsuit against oil companies , not unlike lawsuits against pharmaceutical and asbestos firms seeking compensation for the harm their products have caused , some of which have resulted in financial settlements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a legal basis for recovering costs in your own courts even against international parties , &amp;rdquo ; said Gage. &amp;ldquo ; The letters are a first step to establishing the moral and legal responsibility for those harms. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Results of letter writing have been mixed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Letters sent to Chevron by Victoria and View Royal were answered by parent company Shell , which detailed the firm&amp;rsquo ; s multibillion-dollar pivot toward low-carbon energy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But a letter sent by Whistler to Canadian Natural Resources Ltd. demanding the company pay a &amp;ldquo ; fair share&amp;rdquo ; of the municipality&amp;rsquo ; s climate-related costs prompted oil and gas companies to pull out of a conference scheduled at the resort . &lt;a href= " https : **33;93482;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Mayor Jack Crompton apologized&lt;/a&gt; for the letter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provincial Environment Minister George Heyman responded to a letter from West Vancouver , saying &amp;ldquo ; at the present time the government is not considering either litigation or legislation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &amp;ldquo ; strict liability&amp;rdquo ; approach to litigation &amp;mdash ; in which there is no proof of fault &amp;mdash ; has not been tested in Canada , according to a report to Burnaby council obtained in a Freedom of Information request by the resource sector advocate Resource Works . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pursuing legal action &amp;ldquo ; is a contentious and uncertain undertaking , with high costs , long time frame , and high uncertainty of success , &amp;rdquo ; Burnaby staff wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Victoria Mayor Lisa Helps had campaigned actively for a class action suit &amp;mdash ; even sending letters to other civic leaders &amp;mdash ; but then reversed herself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite a series of emails from Gage urging her to reconsider her new position , Victoria&amp;rsquo ; s lawsuit resolution will go to the floor without Helps&amp;rsquo ; support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;93517;TOOLONG ... title= " Victoria Mayor Lisa Helps . " alt= " Victoria Mayor Lisa Helps . " data-portal-copyright= " DARREN STONE " **28;93553;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1098399 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , Helps will champion a resolution urging the federal government to end fossil fuel industry subsidies and funnel some of that money to municipalities struggling to pay climate-related costs for infrastructure upgrades. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While lawsuits may be a useful tool , &amp;ldquo ; we can get further faster by redirecting those subsidies , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; We can&amp;rsquo ; t wait 20 years to get the money from oil companies . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The annual federal government subsidy to the fossil fuel industry is $46 billion , &amp;rdquo ; the motion reads . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Lori Ackerman , myself and every other mayor would agree that local governments are absolutely ill-equipped to financially to deal with climate change , &amp;rdquo ; Helps said . &amp;ldquo ; We already have more costs and we don&amp;rsquo ; t have any more revenue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ackerman and Helps will lead a panel on B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s energy future at the UBCM conference next week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Litigation against oil companies faces an uncertain legal landscape . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While it is possible to calculate the amount of carbon dioxide that an oil company has caused to be released into the atmosphere , it is much more difficult to determine what direct harm it has caused in the complex swirl of climate and extreme weather events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That problem has caused my stress for years , &amp;rdquo ; said Gage with a laugh . &amp;ldquo ; The much simpler case &amp;mdash ; the one we have been advocating in B.C. &amp;mdash ; is to determine the cost of preparing for climate change and then figure out how much of that ( oil companies ) should pay for . That , to me , is the low-hanging fruit . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Governments can enact legislation to help people and communities recover damages as they did in Canada with the tobacco industry , led by B.C. , he noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It puts you in a better position when you can say , &amp;lsquo ; Look , we could be suing you under this legislation . Wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t you rather negotiate ? &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; said Gage. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A bill intended to open the door for such lawsuits , drafted with the assistance of Greenpeace , was introduced in the Ontario legislature last year but appears to have stalled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; City councils are right to be skeptical of a litigation strategy with a &amp;ldquo ; nebulous end goal and an unlikely end result at uncertain expense , &amp;rdquo ; said Resource Works executive director Stewart Muir . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Maybe the rock-em , sock-em approach of lawsuits is a way to get attention , but I would question it as a way to achieve constructive engagement , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; When lawyers show up , people will retreat . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;93583;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;93612;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;93648;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;93690;TOOLONG ... Email &lt;a href= " **28;93908;TOOLONG " **35;93938;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Randy ShoreFri , 20 Sep 2019 22:57:26 **45;93975;TOOLONG ... NewsGasoline inquiry finds B.C. motorists paying at least 13 cents a litre too muchhttp : **36;94022;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;94060;TOOLONG ... title= " Gas priced at $163.9 at an Esso Station in Vancouver , BC. , April 4 , 2019. " alt= " Gas priced at $163.9 at an Esso Station in Vancouver , BC. , April 4 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;94096;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 990475 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;94126;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. government promised Friday to examine suggestions by its gas-pricing inquiry that found motorists are paying more for gas than they would in a truly competitive market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Potential actions include regulating prices , as is done in Quebec and Atlantic Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The provincial government-commissioned gas-pricing inquiry undertaken by a B.C. Utilities Commission panel found there is an unexplained difference in wholesale gas prices of 13 cents a litre in Metro Vancouver with the U.S. Pacific Northwest market that helps set prices in the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The difference is about six cents in northern B.C. , according to the inquiry&amp;rsquo ; s findings released Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The commission estimated that discrepancy costs B.C. motorists $490 million annually . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jobs , Trade and Technology Minister Bruce Ralston said the inquiry&amp;rsquo ; s findings were &amp;ldquo ; very significant&amp;rdquo ; and showed that price gouging does exist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said he would recommend the province implement an extended one-month comment period suggested by the panel , which could possibly provide more answers around the unexplained 13-cent higher price . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;94162;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;94204;TOOLONG ... confirms B.C. motorists getting hosed at pumps via big **28;94239;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ralston said the B.C. government would digest the report and its suggestions , including whether price regulation was appropriate , but stressed that no decisions have been made . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; British Columbians deserve fairness and transparency on the price of gas they have to pay , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In releasing the report , BCUC chairman and CEO David Morton said : &amp;ldquo ; ( The public ) should know they are paying at least 13 cents more a litre than they would otherwise pay if the market was more competitive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The commission also found there were higher retail margins in B.C. than other parts of Canada and this difference has increased since 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;94269;TOOLONG ... title= " Bruce Ralston " alt= " Bruce Ralston " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;94305;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 633232 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The BCUC panel did not find there was collusion among the major suppliers that include Parkland Fuel , which operates the Burnaby refinery , Suncor , Imperial Oil , and Shell . However , the panel said because the four firms control about 90 per cent of the market in southern B.C. , it is an oligopoly that exerts wholesale market power . Husky Energy is the other supplier in B.C. that has a refinery in Prince George . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Parkland , Suncor , Shell and Imperial Oil all said they would not be commenting until they had examined the inquiry&amp;rsquo ; s final report . &amp;ldquo ; We have only recently received the British Columbia Utilities Commission findings and will be reviewing the report thoroughly before providing any further response , &amp;rdquo ; Parkland spokesman Leroy McKinnon said in a written statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The BCUC panel suggested the B.C. government could encourage more refining capacity in the province , but acknowledged that would be a challenge as fuel use is projected to decline and there are high environmental costs to building a refinery . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another option is to use regulation , either of pricing or regulating the supply chain , as is done in some form in Quebec , Newfoundland , New Brunswick , Nova Scotia and Prince Edward Island , introduced between the 1980s through to the 2000s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The panel also suggested beefing up pipeline capacity to B.C. , but Morton noted that if Trans Mountain is expanded it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily mean more fuel will be carried or that it would necessarily lower prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked if the commission was hamstrung by not being able to look at taxes at the pump , Morton noted those were transparent and known and didn&amp;rsquo ; t diminish the panel&amp;rsquo ; s findings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Premier John Horgan ordered the gas-price inquiry in May when prices at the pump reached $1.70 in the Lower Mainland , saying the public deserved answers about why prices were so much more expensive and variable than in other jurisdictions . At the time , the B.C. Liberals and the Alberta government bought ads blaming Horgan and linking his government&amp;rsquo ; s resistance to the Trans Mountain Pipeline expansion and taxes to the surging costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In response to the inquiry&amp;rsquo ; s findings , the B.C. Liberals continued to blame provincial taxes for the high price of gas in the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Horgan and the NDP dragged their feet with a sham review that was barred from looking at the impact of their policies and taxation on the costs of fuel , &amp;rdquo ; said Peter Milobar , MLA for Kamloops-North Thompson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Industry players and observers had noted B.C. relies heavily on Alberta for refined fuel products that can be constrained by refinery outages and space on the Trans Mountain Pipeline . Both mean higher costs for transportation , via truck or rail from Alberta , or farther afield in markets in the U.S. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province is also subject to regulations that don&amp;rsquo ; t exist elsewhere , including a low-carbon fuel standard . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Morton said those factors had been taken into consideration , explaining seven cents of a 20-cent difference in wholesale prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;94335;TOOLONG " **37;94367;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;94406;TOOLONG " &gt; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;94436;TOOLONG ... title= " Large delivery trucks get set to fill up in front of the giant storage tanks outside of the Parkland refinery site in North Burnaby. " alt= " Large delivery trucks get set to fill up in front of the giant storage tanks outside of the Parkland refinery site in North Burnaby. " data-portal-copyright= " Mark van Manen " **28;94472;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1054959 " /&gt; Gordon HoekstraFri , 30 Aug 2019 23:59:05 **45;94502;TOOLONG ... NewsPoliticsB.C . Hydro seeks special rate to offer TransLink , B.C. Transit EV conversionhttp : **30;94549;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;94581;TOOLONG ... title= " TransLink is transitioning to more battery-electric buses for its fleet . " alt= " TransLink is transitioning to more battery-electric buses for its fleet . " data-portal-copyright= " TRANSLINK HANDOUT " **28;94617;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1073438 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro is proposing to give TransLink , B.C. Transit and other heavy-duty fleet owners a break on their electricity bills , if they are converting to electric vehicles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Hydro submitted an application to the B.C. Utilities Commission to create special charging rates for EV fleet owners , with the deepest discounts , of up to 60 per cent , going to those that can recharge batteries at depot facilities overnight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The call for specific &amp;ldquo ; charging rates&amp;rdquo ; comes in response to a plea for such rates from TransLink and B.C. Transit as both organizations set goals for the adoption of battery-electric buses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The new services and rates described in this ( application ) will contribute to B.C. Transit 's and TransLink 's respective long term strategies to reduce greenhouse gases related to the operation of their fleet , " B.C. Hydro wrote in the application . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And doing so will be necessary for the agencies to do their part in meeting B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s overall provincial objective of cutting greenhouse-gas emissions by 80 per cent from 2007 levels by 2050. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the end of July , B.C. Transit announced its objective to &lt;a href= " https : **33;94647;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;convert to an all-electric fleet&lt;/a&gt; for transit systems outside of Metro Vancouver by 2040. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Metro Vancouver , TransLink aims to replace 1,500 buses in its fleet with electric buses over the same time frame . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;94682;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. Transit announced in late July that it is taking its entire fleet of buses electric . The service operates 1,000 buses outside Metro Vancouver . " alt= " B.C. Transit announced in late July that it is taking its entire fleet of buses electric . The service operates 1,000 buses outside Metro Vancouver . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;94718;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1062483 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro is proposing two optional fleet-charging rates with the discount being offered by giving the customers a break on so-called demand charges normally applied to &amp;ldquo ; large **25;94748;TOOLONG ; accounts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the option referred to as the &amp;ldquo ; overnight rate , &amp;rdquo ; B.C. Hydro proposes to forego demand charges for customers that can recharge batteries at depot charging stations between 10 p.m. and 6 a.m. when system demand is lowest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its justification for an overnight rate , B.C. Hydro said in its filing that the amount customers will pay for the additional new load , effectively the new electricity they will need , &amp;ldquo ; will meet or exceed the marginal cost of serving new load . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which means that Hydro expects the overnight rate will cover its costs , including the price for the power used as well as the cost to deliver it , without the demand-related charges that would normally be charged to &amp;ldquo ; large general service&amp;rdquo ; customers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , for a transit operator , the difference , at the outset , would be a $2,000-per-month electricity bill for an EV bus versus $3,700 if it had to pay the usual account demand charge , according to an estimate in the filing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hydro calls the second option the &amp;ldquo ; demand transition rate , &amp;rdquo ; for operators that plan to use route-charging systems where bus batteries are charged in short bursts at stops along their routes with&amp;nbsp ; high-powered chargers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this scenario , the utility proposes to give fleet customers a six-year holiday from demand charges , between 2021 and 2026 , before phasing them in between 2027 and 2032. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The difference , however , would be a $3,500 electricity bill for a bus using route-charging , owing to a higher electricity price , versus $4,500 if a demand charge were applied . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In an emailed statement , B.C. Hydro spokeswoman Tanya Fish said the proposed rates will only be available to commercial customers that are businesses or government agencies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fish said Hydro anticipates a decision will only be made after a regulatory process , including hearings , to determine if the rates are &amp;ldquo ; fair , just , reasonable and not unduly discriminatory . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;94775;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;94806;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;94844;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;94873;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;94904;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ?
