Hydro crisis fund costs less than half its estimate , utility sayshttp : **36;95047;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;95085;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. Hydro was under pressure to find ways to help cash-strapped customers with rising electricity rates . Their solution wound up being less expensive than expected . " alt= " B.C. Hydro was under pressure to find ways to help cash-strapped customers with rising electricity rates . Their solution wound up being less expensive than expected . " data-portal-copyright= " bill keay " **28;95121;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 409773 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;95151;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A special fund that B.C. Hydro set up to help customers in financial crisis with their bills paid out less than a quarter of what it expected in its first year so the utility wants to reduce the amount account holders pay in to cover its costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro set up the customer-crisis fund in 2018 as a three-year pilot project as part of its last rate-setting application to the B.C. Utilities Commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its first year , however , B.C. Hydro collected $4.5 million through a 25-cents-per-month rate rider on customer bills , but only spent $1.7 million on the program , according to an interim report filed with the Utilities Commission at the end of July . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We found our program costs to be lower than we had anticipated . For example , the set-up costs for the program were around half of what we originally estimated , &amp;rdquo ; B.C. Hydro spokeswoman Tanya Fish said in an email response to questions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Participation during the first year of the pilot was also lower than estimated , largely due to us not having have a comparable program to base participation on . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The crisis fund paid out just $847,518 in direct grants to 2,282 customers unable to pay their bills , at an average of $371 per customer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its initial estimate , B.C. Hydro estimated it would pay out $4.1 million to some 10,250 customers when it launched the fund in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 6,416 applications for help from the fund that the utility received in the first year of operation was less than half the 15,000 it anticipated , according to the interim report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So the utility wants to cut the rate rider , the special charge to customers , to 13 cents per month from 25 cents , effective Oct. 1 . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Based on the ( crisis fund ) rate rider , the current ( fund ) costs and the forecast costs of operating the ( program ) for the remainder of the three-year pilot to April 30 , 2021 , B.C. Hydro expects that the revenue collected will continue to be greater than the forecast costs , &amp;rdquo ; said Fred James , Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s chief regulatory officer in a letter to the Utilities Commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If Hydro kept collecting the rate rider at the 25-cent level , James wrote that the surplus in the account could reach as high as $7.9 million by the end of the three years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cutting it to 13 cents could still leave a $3.8-million surplus at the end , but would provide Hydro with leeway to accommodate increased participation as more customers become aware of it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The idea for the crisis fund came from the B.C. Old Age Pensioners Organization during B.C. Hydro&amp;rsquo ; s 2015 hearing before the B.C. Utilities Commission to set electricity rates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With electricity rates ratcheting up , Hydro was under pressure from anti-poverty groups to find some way to help low-income customers facing financial difficulty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The grants are not intended to cover the costs of customers who are simply behind on their bills , but rather to help customers facing a financial crisis and face potential disconnection. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Wednesday , B.C. Old Age Pensioners Organization president Jerry Gosling said in an email that the group&amp;rsquo ; s members are &amp;ldquo ; grateful that something is in the works to accommodate some hard-up members . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That is not to say that the program doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have its critics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some Hydro customers were caught off guard by the new rate rider and took offence to the additional 25-cents-per-month charge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro received 1,472 complaints from customers about the additional charge , according to its report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Comparing it to a charity that customers have no choice about contributing to and questions about why it should be up to customers who are paying their bills and absorbing the same increases in electricity rates were among common complaints . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its report , B.C. Hydro said it was too soon to determine whether the program has helped reduce its costs related to disconnections and re-connections . In her email , Fish said the utility hasn&amp;rsquo ; t seen significant changes in its delinquent accounts rate , which stands at about 10,000 per month on average . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fish said at the end of the pilot program , if no permanent crisis fund is created , a final decision on what to do with a surplus would be up to the Utilities Commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;95187;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;95218;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;95256;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;95285;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerThu , 08 Aug 2019 16:20:43 **45;95316;TOOLONG ... NewsNational Energy Board rejects argument LNG Canada 's pipeline should have had federal reviewhttp : **34;95363;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;95399;TOOLONG ... title= " A Smithers resident is challenging the Coastal GasLink pipeline , intended to supply the porposed LNG Canada liquefied natural has plant , shown here in the conceptual drawing . " alt= " A Smithers resident is challenging the Coastal GasLink pipeline , intended to supply the porposed LNG Canada liquefied natural has plant , shown here in the conceptual drawing . " data-portal-copyright= " LNG Canada " **28;95435;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 843904 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The National Energy Board on Friday rejected a Smithers environmental consultant 's argument that the Coastal GasLink pipeline should have been subjected to a federal environmental review . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Michael Sawyer filed an application with the NEB in July 2018 challenging the jurisdiction of B.C. 's Environmental Assessment Office , which reviewed and , in 2014 , approved Coastal GasLink 's proposal for a 670-km pipeline from B.C. 's northeast to Kitimat. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the new pipeline would &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; be entirely within B.C. , Sawyer argued that it would be " functionally integrated " with TransCanada Corp . ' s federally regulated Nova Gas Transmission system , by extension of being another TransCanada subsidiary , sharing common management and having the potential for physical connections with the Nova Gas infrastructure . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NEB panel heard that Coastal GasLink&amp;rsquo ; s design included a three-km right of way between its line and Nova Gas&amp;rsquo ; infrastructure that extends into Alberta . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;That question was the subject of an NEB hearing in May . On Friday , the board ruled against Sawyer 's arguments . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Based on the totality of the record before it , the board does not find a basis to conclude that the project is properly within federal jurisdiction , &amp;rdquo ; presiding NEB member Philip Davies wrote in the decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Put another way , the board does not find that the ( Coastal GasLink ) pipeline forms part of a single indivisible undertaking with the ( Nova Gas ) system or any other federal undertaking , &amp;rdquo ; the decision continued , and therefore did not fit under the precedent arguments that Sawyer&amp;rsquo ; s counsel relied on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the Coastal GasLink design allowed for the prospect of future linkages with the Nova Gas system , the project was being built for the sole purpose of delivering natural gas to the LNG Canada facility in Kitimat. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Rather , the ( Coastal GasLink ) project is a local work and undertaking properly regulated by the province of B.C. , &amp;rdquo ; the NEB decision concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The Coastal GasLink project , a 48-inch diameter , 670-km natural-gas pipeline , is a crucial link in the $40-billion LNG Canada liquefied natural gas development being led by Shell Canada that has been approved by regulators and received a final investment decision by investors last fall . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The NEB hearing involved 13 participants and included the filing of evidence by relevant parties and included written and oral arguments on the question whether the Coastal GasLink project forms part of a federal undertaking as contemplated by Section 92 of the Constitution Act . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;In its decision , the NEB found that , based on the evidence it heard , the Coastal GasLink project " is properly regulated by the province of British Columbia . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;&lt;a href= " **29;95465;TOOLONG " **49;95496;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;&lt;a href= " http : **27;95547;TOOLONG " &gt;tw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;95576;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;95618;TOOLONG ... spend $275 million on $40-billion LNG Canada project , including cash to buy gas **29;95653;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;95684;TOOLONG ... Canada project in Kitimat given green light to build by **33;95719;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Derrick PennerSat , 27 Jul 2019 00:50:57 **45;95754;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsGas-price fixing ? No , but Vancouver stations have greater margins than any other city in Western Canadahttp : **34;95801;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;95837;TOOLONG ... title= " Fuel delivery driver David Pirzek fills underground tanks at a Petro-Canada in North Delta on July 4. " alt= " Fuel delivery driver David Pirzek fills underground tanks at a Petro-Canada in North Delta on July 4. " data-portal-copyright= " Arlen Redekop " **28;95873;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1047969 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;95903;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; You don&amp;rsquo ; t have to drive too far in Vancouver to see that price fixing is not going on among the dozens of retailers that sell hundreds of thousands of litres of gasoline on any given day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On July 3 , at 12th and Clark , the Shell station was selling a litre of regular gas for 154.9 cents , 15 cents more than the Super Save across the road . And those prices changed at least three times that day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the morning of Canada Day &amp;mdash ; notorious for price jumps at the pump &amp;mdash ; the Super Save was selling for 26 cents a litre less than the Petro-Canada on Broadway in Kitsilano six kilometres away . By the end of that day , two Vancouver Chevrons and a Mobil had matched that lowest price of 128.9 cents a litre ; then Super Save brought its price up to 139.9. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;95938;TOOLONG ... title= " Jass Dhillon at the Super Save gas station at 12th Avenue and Clark Drive in Vancouver on July 3. " alt= " Jass Dhillon at the Super Save gas station at 12th Avenue and Clark Drive in Vancouver on July 3. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;95974;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1047854 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jass Dhillon , who works the gas bar at Super Save&amp;rsquo ; s Clark Drive location , said gas there is always cheaper than at the Shell across the road . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Nobody goes there , &amp;rdquo ; he quipped. &amp;ldquo ; We always have lineups because this station is the cheapest of any of our locations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Postmedia News visited the two locations , Super Save was pumping gas at full capacity , but there were a few drivers at Shell . One , John Sliziak , said he knew he was paying more than across the road but was getting a five-cent-a-litre discount and found Super Save gas to be &amp;ldquo ; sluggish . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At Super Save , Dan Sims and Edna David said they were driving past when they noticed the gas was cheaper than anywhere else , while Brant Dorman found out the price from a gas-pricing website . None knew how gasoline was priced , though Sims &amp;mdash ; originally from New Zealand &amp;mdash ; said the price fluctuations &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;were very noticeable&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; here compared to back home . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a major city that pays more for regular gas than any other in North America &amp;mdash ; peaking in late April at an average 172.9 cents a litre &amp;mdash ; these wild and localized price fluctuations get noticed . The provincial government&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;96004;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Gasoline and Diesel Price Inquiry&lt;/a&gt; is set for release in late August against a backdrop of growing consumer concern and increasing retail margins . Coast Capital Savings last month released a phone survey that found gas prices in Metro Vancouver were affecting the ability of half the respondents to save , while a report released Wednesday shows the &lt;a href= " https : **33;96039;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;retail margin for Vancouver gas stations&lt;/a&gt; is 8.4 cents a litre higher than the average in other western Canadian cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;96074;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " **39;96116;TOOLONG ... prices rose with land costs but full differentials unexplained : **27;96157;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " **39;96186;TOOLONG ... gas price inquiry boosts confidential status , but firms still **30;96227;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " **39;96259;TOOLONG ... companies refuse to cough up profit margins to B.C. **28;96300;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;96330;TOOLONG ... Palmer : A taxing effort to quickly uncover reasons for high gas prices in **25;96365;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " **39;96392;TOOLONG ... prices : 5 things to know about refined petroleum in **25;96433;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96460;TOOLONG ... title= " Dan Sims fills up at the Super Save gas station at 12th Avenue and Clark Drive on July 3. " alt= " Dan Sims fills up at the Super Save gas station at 12th Avenue and Clark Drive on July 3. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;96496;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1047860 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Arco Fuels opened salvo in late ' 90s gas war&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas-price wars first came to Vancouver in the summer of 1998 when U.S.-based Arco Fuels bought a string of Lower Mainland gas stations and began selling discounted gas that it barged in from a refinery in Washington state . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before that , gas stations would change their price infrequently , usually when there was a significant change in the price of crude oil or a tax change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Arco began offering cheaper gas , the big players &amp;mdash ; Petro-Canada , Shell , Esso , Chevron and Husky &amp;mdash ; fought back hard . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This led to the gas war , with prices at one time dipping below 30 cents a litre . Some of the independent gas stations couldn&amp;rsquo ; t compete and closed , but the big companies hung in and Arco left the market in 2001 . From that point on , local gas stations began monitoring each other&amp;rsquo ; s prices more closely , changing them accordingly , and the price wars never really went away . And the cut-price retailers still exist in the form of Super Save , Costco and Safeway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96526;TOOLONG ... title= " Dec. 16 , 1998 -- Arco gas stations in Langley ( 208th Ave ) had customers lining up for 35 cent a litre gas . " alt= " Dec. 16 , 1998 -- Arco gas stations in Langley ( 208th Ave ) had customers lining up for 35 cent a litre gas . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;96562;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1047985 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the Kent Group&amp;rsquo ; s 2018 National Retail Petroleum Site Census , the number of gas stations in the Lower Mainland is relatively stable at 582 &amp;mdash ; 432 in Metro Vancouver and 150 in the Fraser Valley . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the Kent report said the ownership landscape was changing , with traditional pit-to-pump operators &amp;mdash ; those responsible for extraction , refining , transportation and retailing &amp;mdash ; getting out of retail . In 2016 , Esso sold the bulk of its Metro Vancouver stations to 7-Eleven and Parkland Fuels . In 2017 , Chevron sold its B.C. retail assets to Parkland , including Metro Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s only refinery , and at the start of 2019 Husky announced it was considering selling its retail operations and its refinery in Prince George , the second of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s two refineries. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Werner Antweiler , an economics professor at UBC&amp;rsquo ; s Sauder School of Business , said profit margins are higher in refining than in retail and refiners are less interested in controlling the process from extraction to sales at the pump , preferring to allow companies or individuals with expertise in storefront retail to take over . This is because gas stations are more and more reliant on food and drink sales and car washes to make more profit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the 1980s , most gas stations were owned and operated by one of the big players . Now , across B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;1,368 gas stations , &lt;/span&gt; the decision on what to charge at the pump depends on the relationship between the station operator and the supplier of fuel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More gas stations are independently owned , with owners selling fuel under the banner of a recognized brand and deciding themselves what price to charge . Generally speaking , in B.C. about half the gas stations set their own prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dhillon said part of his job is to check the prices at nearby gas stations three times a day and report the information to Super Save&amp;rsquo ; s Surrey head office . He is then told what price to set and makes the change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Parkland Fuel Corporation &amp;mdash ; with 180 Chevron stations , 46 Esso stations and 27 Fas Gas stations in B.C. &amp;mdash ; has a price-control centre for its directly controlled stations where staff &lt;strong&gt; &lt;/strong&gt; gather price information from retailers across the province and set prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Antweiler said a lot of &amp;ldquo ; time-of-day pricing&amp;rdquo ; goes on . For example , prices will be higher during peak driving hours and lower in the evening , as retailers try to maintain their sales volumes , hoping to draw in people who might also buy a litre of milk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96592;TOOLONG ... title= " Gas on sale for 139.9 at the Super Save gas station at 12th Ave . and Clark Drive in Vancouver , B.C .. , July 3 , 2019. " alt= " Gas on sale for 139.9 at the Super Save gas station at 12th Ave . and Clark Drive in Vancouver , B.C .. , July 3 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;96628;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1048035 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Retail margin easier to calculate than refining margin&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The smell of gasoline fills the night air at Petro-Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth and Cypress station as a delivery driver pours 40,000 litres of fuel into three below-ground tanks &amp;mdash ; one for each of the three grades of fuel for sale . Generally , regular gas makes up 90 per cent of sales ; however , in some areas , like Richmond , that percentage is less . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a Denwill Enterprises truck , though if you look closely at the tank you can just make out an old Chevron sign , likely painted over when Chevron sold its Metro Vancouver assets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The operator , in blue coveralls with a high-visibility vest and wearing thick black gloves , measures the depth of each tank with a simple long rod , then signs off on the order . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96658;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;96694;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; He says one load filled one station and the delivery took around an hour to complete . The company&amp;rsquo ; s trucks run around the clock servicing Petro-Canada , Shell , Chevron and Costco in the Lower Mainland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The cost to deliver this load is one small part of the complicated set of commodity prices , refining and transportation costs and profit used to calculate what you pay at the pump on any given day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It starts with the crude oil used by refineries in Alberta , Burnaby and Washington state to meet British Columbia&amp;rsquo ; s roughly 5.2-billion-litre yearly demand for refined gasoline &amp;mdash ; enough to fill over 2,000 Olympic-sized pools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The refiners add their refining cost , including their profit , to the cost of the oil , and then add the cost of transportation to a storage terminal where refiners charge their &amp;ldquo ; rack price&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; or wholesale price &amp;mdash ; to gas stations . The rack is simply the physical location where delivery trucks get filled . In Burnaby , there are 10 racks at three locations ( Parkland , Shell and Imperial ) that supply all the Lower Mainland , with Imperial being the largest provider . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When coming up with the rack price , the refiners also take into account supply &amp;mdash ; for example , if a refinery is running at reduced capacity , the price will be higher &amp;mdash ; and what the spot price for gasoline is on the Pacific Northwest commodity market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rack price minus the spot price of a barrel of oil reveals the refiner&amp;rsquo ; s margin , while the pump price minus the rack price and taxes reveals the retailer&amp;rsquo ; s margin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within that refiner&amp;rsquo ; s margin is a complex set of production components that the big refiners are reluctant to share . The gasoline price inquiry is currently dealing with B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s major refiners , assuring them those details would be kept secret once they are handed over to the inquiry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the retailer&amp;rsquo ; s margin is simpler to understand , since it involves just the cost of operating the gas station and getting the gas from the terminal rack to the pump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96724;TOOLONG ... title= " Gas trucks fill up at a rack at Imperial Oil 's facility in Burnaby , July 3 , 2019. " alt= " Gas trucks fill up at a rack at Imperial Oil 's facility in Burnaby , July 3 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;96760;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1048037 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rack prices are not secret &amp;mdash ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;96790;TOOLONG ... and &lt;a href= " https : **32;96825;TOOLONG ... posting them daily on their websites &amp;mdash ; and can vary as much as 10 cents on any given day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The amount the retail gas station pays at the rack can vary a bit , with some receiving a few cents discount if they buy a lot of gas , and others paying a few cents more for the cost of injecting additives for branded gasoline like Shell , Chevron , Petro-Canada and Husky . Super Save and Costco don&amp;rsquo ; t inject additives , which is one reason they can generally sell for less at the pump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96859;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;96895;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; For example , on July 5 , the rack price in Vancouver was 89.5 cents a litre and the average retail price of the day was 154.9 cents a litre . In Vancouver , you pay taxes of 44.39 cents a litre , plus five per cent sales tax . So the average gas retailer was making a margin on the day of 13.26 cents a litre , give or take a few cents for volume or additives. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Antweiler said the profit in retail is all about volume , and what one charges over the course of the day is a balancing act that factors in that a lower price brings more people into the station to buy other things and maybe get a car wash . The average Metro Vancouver gas station sells about four million litres of fuel a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Last month , the B.C. Utilities Commission inquiry commissioned Vancouver-based Deetken Group to provide &lt;a href= " https : **33;96925;TOOLONG ... primer&lt;/a&gt; on B.C. 's market for refined petroleum&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the report , Vancouver gas stations pay a higher rack price than stations in any other city in Western Canada . They also set a higher margin on their sales of gas than in those cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A comparison of B.C. to other parts of Western Canada reveals that wholesale gasoline prices ( the rack price ) in B.C. have risen above historical price differentials , and prices in Vancouver have experienced a more significant increase when compared to average prices in other western Canadian cities , &amp;rdquo ; said the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report states that since 2015 , the rack price difference between Vancouver and the rest of Western Canada has risen as much as 15 cents a litre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report said retail margins fluctuate frequently on a month-to-month basis , making it hard to discern systemic changes . However , the report looked at a six-month rolling average and found &amp;ldquo ; that retail margins in Vancouver specifically have risen above the average in the Western region and those observed in Kamloops . Vancouver appears to have outpaced the growth in retail margin compared to the rest of Western Canada since 2015 , a year after crude prices plunged . Victoria and Abbotsford have followed suit . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A report filed to the inquiry by Parkland Fuels states that retail margin is not the same as a profit margin and must take into account B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s higher land values and property taxes , its higher minimum wage and high credit-card processing costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Antweiler adds that the loss of several gas stations in Vancouver &amp;mdash ; particularly on the westside &amp;mdash ; over the past few years has had an effect on margins . There is less competition , so prices can be higher . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , he said , the bigger reason for the higher margins was simply the &amp;ldquo ; spillover from the housing market&amp;rdquo ; because land , leasing and property taxes are now so much higher here than elsewhere in Western Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Deetken Group&amp;rsquo ; s latest report , released Wednesday , &lt;a href= " https : **33;96960;TOOLONG ... the increased retail margins&lt;/a&gt; were due almost entirely to higher land and credit-card costs . So far this year , the retail margin for Vancouver gas stations is 8.4 cents a litre higher than the average in other western Canadian cities . It was 5.9 cents a litre higher in 2018 . The Deetken Group found that 1.4 cents a litre of that 8.4-cents-a-litre difference could not be attributed to land or other costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;96995;TOOLONG ... title= " Gas being pumped into a Rover TC 2000 at the Super Save station at 12th and Clark Drive on July 3. " alt= " Gas being pumped into a Rover TC 2000 at the Super Save station at 12th and Clark Drive on July 3. " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;97031;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1048112 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the inquiry is not expected to offer any price predictions , Antweiler expects prices to increase over time because of the growing population and car use in the Lower Mainland , coupled with continuing supply problems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;h3&gt;GAS BY THE NUMBERS&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;strong&gt;46 billion : &lt;/strong&gt; The National Energy Board says Canadians burned through 46 billion litres of gasoline in 2018 &amp;mdash ; four billion litres more than in 2013. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;strong&gt;24.6 million : &lt;/strong&gt; According to Statistics Canada , there were 24.6 million &amp;ldquo ; road motor vehicles&amp;rdquo ; registered in 2017 . So , an average Canadian driver in a car with a 70-litre tank burned through 26 tanks a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;strong&gt;2.4 million : &lt;/strong&gt; &amp;nbsp ; In 2017 there were 2.4 million passenger vehicles registered in B.C. , which is 330,000 more than four years earlier . The average British Columbian driver in a car with a 70-litre tank burned through 30 tankloads a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;strong&gt;$3,150 : &lt;/strong&gt; At an average price of 150 cents a litre , that&amp;rsquo ; s $3,150 a year . The same person living in Edmonton paying $1 a litre would be spending $2,100 a year on gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;97061;TOOLONG ... title= " The first gas station in Canada , at Cambie and Smithe streets in Vancouver . It opened in June 1907. " alt= " The first gas station in Canada , at Cambie and Smithe streets in Vancouver . It opened in June 1907. " data-portal-copyright= " Imperial Oil " **28;97097;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1048034 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;FIRST GAS STATION&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first gas station in Vancouver , as described by the late city archivist James Matthews , opened in June 1907 at the southeast corner of Cambie and Smithe alongside an Imperial Oil office . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until that time , the few gasoline-powered vehicles in the city had been fuelled with a pail out of a tub of gasoline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That first pump dispensed gas from an old 60-litre kitchen hot-water tank , via a &amp;ldquo ; length of rubber garden hose , &amp;rdquo ; and was bought for 20 cents a gallon and sold for 40 cents a gallon &amp;mdash ; just under 10 cents a litre , which would have been very expensive at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;97127;TOOLONG " **36;97158;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;i&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;97196;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/i&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;i&gt;Is there more to this story ? NewsIndigenous women vulnerable to ' man camps ' : MMIWG report . So , what 's at stake with the pipeline approval ? http : **31;97339;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;97372;TOOLONG ... title= " Connie Greyeyes at a lookout over the valley in Fort St. John . " alt= " Connie Greyeyes at a lookout over the valley in Fort St. John . " data-portal-copyright= " Amber Bracken " **28;97408;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1048023 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is &amp;ldquo ; substantial evidence&amp;rdquo ; that vulnerable women face increased violence in boomtowns full of transient labourers building big resource projects . It&amp;rsquo ; s a concern raised by the Murdered and Missing Indigenous Women and Girls report and one that could intensify with the approval of the Trans Mountain pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Work camps , or &amp;lsquo ; man camps , &amp;rsquo ; associated with the resource extraction industry are implicated in higher rates of violence against Indigenous women at the camps and in the neighbouring communities , &amp;rdquo ; says the report , released in June after the national commission gathered testimony from 1,500 people , including survivors and relatives of murdered and missing women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Increased crime levels , including drug- and alcohol-related offences , sexual offences , and domestic and &amp;lsquo ; gang&amp;rsquo ; violence , have been linked to &amp;lsquo ; boom town&amp;rsquo ; and other resource development contexts . &amp;hellip ; There is an urgent need to consider the safety of Indigenous women consistently in all stages of project planning . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The vast majority of resource workers are law-abiding and the large projects they work on are important for the economy , but now that the &lt;a href= " https : **33;97438;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Trans Mountain pipeline has been approved&lt;/a&gt; , towns along its route need to take precautions to protect women , experts say . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Much research has been done on the negative impacts of suddenly bringing in a large group of people to a new place when they don&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily have a clear connection to the local community . It&amp;rsquo ; s been highlighted by the MMIWG Commission , which pointed out the negative consequences can disproportionately hurt Indigenous women and girls , &amp;rdquo ; Premier John Horgan told Postmedia. &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s the responsibility of governments in all jurisdictions to ensure public safety and to prevent violence . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although he provided no specifics , Horgan said his NDP government would work with local communities to prevent violence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan is among those opposed to Trans Mountain , which Prime Minister Justin Trudeau said would &amp;ldquo ; have shovels in the ground&amp;rdquo ; this year after the federal government gave its second approval in mid-June to twin the pipeline from Alberta to Burnaby. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some B.C. First Nations , including the Cheam and Whispering Pines , have signed benefit agreements that will provide money and jobs from the project , while other Indigenous groups adamantly oppose the pipeline , largely over environmental concerns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;97473;TOOLONG ... title= " Grand Chief Stewart Phillip , president of the Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs , demonstrates against Kinder Morgan outside National Energy Board hearings on the proposed Trans Mountain pipeline expansion . " alt= " Grand Chief Stewart Phillip , president of the Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs , demonstrates against Kinder Morgan outside National Energy Board hearings on the proposed Trans Mountain pipeline expansion . " data-portal-copyright= " DARRYL DYCK " **28;97509;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 491797 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the opponents is Grand Chief Stewart Phillip , who has demanded Ottawa quickly implement the more than 200 recommendations in the MMIWG report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The federal Liberals are ramming through a controversial pipeline expansion whose industrial man-camps will further put Indigenous women , girls , and two-spirit people at risk , &amp;rdquo ; Phillip , president of the Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs , &lt;a href= " https : **32;97539;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;said in June&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The MMIWG report calls on industries to consider the safety of Indigenous women during the planning and construction phases of resource projects . It encourages companies , governments and service providers to work together to expand social infrastructure in communities , which includes &amp;ldquo ; ensuring that policing , social services and health services are adequately staffed and resourced. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trans Mountain has &amp;ldquo ; met extensively with communities&amp;rdquo ; where construction will occur along the pipeline , and has a worker code of conduct for its employees , spokeswoman Ali Hounsell said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Community safety is a top priority for Trans Mountain , &amp;rdquo ; she said , adding the company understands the importance of ensuring the presence of temporary workers and worker accommodation is managed and that related social effects are minimized . That includes any potential impacts on Indigenous groups , Indigenous women and girls and other vulnerable groups , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The issues of harassment , respectful behaviour , lawfulness , drugs and alcohol , violence and possession of weapons are directly and overtly covered in the code of conduct . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trans Mountain said it provided information to key organizations , including Indigenous groups , about the number of employees and where they would live in each community . It has also held meetings with local governments and service agencies , and noted it is a National Energy Board condition that the company monitor the socioeconomic effects of the pipeline construction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Trans Mountain will gather information about Indigenous social experiences during construction , including those relating to worker accommodation , worker conduct , as well as Indigenous social and cultural well-being , &amp;rdquo ; Hounsell said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;97573;TOOLONG ... title= " Tanks at the Trans Mountain Westridge Marine Terminal , Burnaby , in 2016. " alt= " Tanks at the Trans Mountain Westridge Marine Terminal , Burnaby , in 2016. " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;97609;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1038234 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous advocate Connie Greyeyes said she is not against pipelines and knows many honourable resource workers where she lives in Fort St. John , the home of several big projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she has also &amp;ldquo ; had so many interactions with women who are assaulted by people who they didn&amp;rsquo ; t know , who were here as workers . &amp;rdquo ; This could be curtailed , she argued , if industry , government and communities provided more resources to vulnerable women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While I understand that progress happens , there has to be solutions to lessen the impacts on women and girls , &amp;rdquo ; Greyeyes said . &amp;ldquo ; This was never an attack on people or an industry , but it&amp;rsquo ; s about raising awareness that there are things that need to change . We need to make sure that communities are protected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Greyeyes worked with Amnesty International to investigate abuses against Indigenous women and girls in Manitoba after construction began on a large hydroelectric dam in 2014 . The MMIWG report supports calls for a public inquiry into alleged &amp;ldquo ; sexual violence and racism&amp;rdquo ; during that project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also contributed to the 2016 Amnesty report Out of Sight , Out of Mind about the safety of Indigenous women in northeast B.C. , where there are many large hydroelectric , oil , gas and pipeline projects . This report , Greyeyes testified at the MMIWG inquiry , concluded some workers come into local towns after many days of labour in remote locations looking to &amp;ldquo ; blow off steam&amp;rdquo ; in places where they are largely anonymous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The MMIWG commissioners raised concerns about the increase in the number of vulnerable women entering the sex trade in towns near mines , pipelines and other developments , quoting a 2018 report by Northern Health and the B.C. Provincial Health Services Authority . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This has largely been attributed to the influx of hundreds to thousands of temporary workers who are often young , male and single , have high disposable incomes , and spend long stretches of time in isolated camp settings , &amp;rdquo ; says the report , The Social Determinants of Health Impacts of Resource Extraction and Development in Rural and Northern Communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Family violence and economic and housing insecurity are reported impacts of resource development , which are factors that are known to contribute to the entry of individuals into the sex trade . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Women are made vulnerable by the combination of exclusion from high-paying resource jobs and having to make ends meet in a town where the cost of living is rising , Greyeyes added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The majority of work in the resource industry is for men . And you try to live as a single mom in Fort St. John waitressing , then you are living in poverty , &amp;rdquo ; said Greyeyes , a case manager with the Indian Residential School Survivors Society . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The non-profit Women&amp;rsquo ; s Resource Society spends thousands running a small food bank in Fort St. John , and could use some financial help . She encouraged large companies doing temporary work in small towns to help fund organizations that assist with housing , addictions , counselling and job training . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has been progress on this front . Shell , which runs a large gas project outside Fort St. John , hired Greyeyes and another woman in 2018 to speak to its workers about sexual harassment and other abuse in the workplace . And Black Swan , an energy company with an operation near Fort St. John , has &amp;ldquo ; invested heavily&amp;rdquo ; in the community , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;97639;TOOLONG ... title= " Aluminum stacked on the wharf at Kitimat B.C. , ready to be shipped to market from Rio Tinto 's aluminum smelter . " alt= " Aluminum stacked on the wharf at Kitimat B.C. , ready to be shipped to market from Rio Tinto 's aluminum smelter . " data-portal-copyright= " Courtesy of Rio Tinto " **28;97675;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 809542 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two academics from the University of Northern B.C. tracked social and economic changes from 2011 to 2016 in Kitimat , where there have been several large-scale industrial projects , including the recent multimillion-dollar upgrades to the Rio Tinto aluminum smelter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their report , &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s Community That Matters , &amp;rdquo ; said safety concerns emerged about escort workers in 2013 , which prompted Kitimat to revise its bylaws to require escort businesses to be licensed to better monitor the safety of employees . As well , a local committee spoke to industry groups about concerns and cultural awareness programs were established , including through the Tamitik Status of Women non-profit agency . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kitimat is now gearing up for the construction of LNG Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;97705;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;$40-billion liquefied natural gas development&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kitimat Mayor Phil Germuth said his town of 8,000 hired more police officers and firefighters during the Rio Tinto upgrades , and kept them on in anticipation of LNG being approved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He would like to hire even more officers before LNG construction begins , although he said &amp;ldquo ; there wasn&amp;rsquo ; t some massive increase in sexual assaults or anything like that&amp;rdquo ; involving resource workers during the Rio Tinto upgrades. &amp;ldquo ; The overwhelming majority of them are good people who are just there to do their jobs , &amp;rdquo ; Germuth said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the newest project that is about to hit Kitimat , LNG Canada has done &amp;ldquo ; an outstanding job&amp;rdquo ; meeting with various local organizations , and has also donated money to a local dementia cottage and worked with Tamitik , which wants to build a new women&amp;rsquo ; s shelter , the mayor said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When you are having the potential of 7,000 workers coming to your community , there&amp;rsquo ; s going to be some issues , &amp;rdquo ; Germuth said . &amp;ldquo ; You need ( the companies ) to be a part of it to make sure they really are committed to making the effects as small as possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;97740;TOOLONG ... title= " Haisla Chief Councillor Crystal Smith and Kitimat Mayor Phil Germuth speak during a LNG press conference in 2018. " alt= " Haisla Chief Councillor Crystal Smith and Kitimat Mayor Phil Germuth speak during a LNG press conference in 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;97776;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1046039 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Greyeyes believes the issues raised in the MMIWG report can be addressed if there is improved respect for both First Nations traditional territories and a commitment by industry and government to protect the vulnerable people impacted by these large projects , either because of violence or financial inequity. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I do believe that there is a way they can work together for the Trans Mountain . They need to be respectful , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; There has to be some middle ground where you take Indigenous people&amp;rsquo ; s concerns seriously . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;97806;TOOLONG " **36;97837;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;97875;TOOLONG " &gt;twit ... &lt;/p&gt; Lori CulbertSat , 06 Jul 2019 10:00:46 **45;97902;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal **28;97949;TOOLONG companies refuse to cough up profit margins to B.C. inquiryhttp : **33;97979;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;98014;TOOLONG ... title= " Prices at the Petro-Canada station at Clark and East Broadway in Vancouver on Tuesday . " alt= " Prices at the Petro-Canada station at Clark and East Broadway in Vancouver on Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " Arlen Redekop " **28;98050;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1045236 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;98080;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; The companies behind most of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s major gas stations are refusing to provide details about their profit margins to a government-ordered inquiry into retail gasoline prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Husky Energy , Suncor Energy and Shell Canada &amp;mdash ; with a combined almost 500 gas stations under retail brand names that include Petro-Canada and Esso &amp;mdash ; declined to give the independent B.C. Utilities Commission information about their retailing margins , which would show how much money they make per litre of gasoline at the pump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Husky called the information &amp;ldquo ; commercially sensitive and confidential , &amp;rdquo ; while Suncor replied that it &amp;ldquo ; is not prepared to provide its retailing margins at controlled sites as this information is competitively sensitive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Due to the confidential and commercially sensitive nature of such information , Shell is not in a position to provide specific information regarding its retail and wholesale gasoline and diesel retail margins , &amp;rdquo ; Shell Canada said in its written submission to the commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The three companies are among the largest players in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s retail gas market . Their refusal to volunteer key data may prove problematic for the commission inquiry , which was ordered in May by the provincial government to examine the factors behind record high gas prices in the Lower Mainland . Premier John Horgan has said he wanted to know if price gouging was at play . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;We will continue to cooperate and to provide information to the BCUC that is not competitively sensitive , " Suncor spokesperson Paul Newmarch said in a statement Tuesday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Husky spokesperson Kim Guttormson said the commission is unable to safeguard the information to the company&amp;rsquo ; s satisfaction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Under its governing legislation and the inquiry mandate from the B.C. government , the BCUC was unable to provide an appropriate level of confidentiality for the information , " he wrote . " We continue to discuss the issue with the BCUC. " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Commission hearings begin next week in Vancouver . The government &lt;a href= " https : **33;98116;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;forbid the commission&lt;/a&gt; from examining the role of provincial taxes on the price of gasoline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas prices spiked to more than $1.70 per litre in the Lower Mainland in May , but have since declined to the $1.40 to $1.50 range . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;98151;TOOLONG ... title= " The Husky at 7389 River Rd. in Delta was selling as at 178.9 on April 23. " alt= " The Husky at 7389 River Rd. in Delta was selling as at 178.9 on April 23. " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;98187;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1003691 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; 7-Eleven Canada , which operates 124 retail gas stations in B.C. , was the only international retailer to provide profit margin data on gasoline and diesel . It asked the utilities commission to keep the information confidential , and redacted the figures from its publicly posted written reply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Release of such information to the public , including suppliers and competitors of 7-Eleven , would harm 7-Eleven&amp;rsquo ; s economic interests , compromise its relationships with suppliers , harm its competitive position , and/or provide an undue advantage to its competitors and suppliers , &amp;rdquo ; the company wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is also in the interests of the administration of justice that the commission receive commercially sensitive information in confidence . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Super Save Gas , a smaller player with 40 gas stations in B.C , also handed over financial data confidentially . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The National Energy Board , in its submission , said the refining margin on regular gasoline in Vancouver in April averaged 52.1 cents per litre , roughly double the Canadian average refining margin . The retail margin was a further 10.5 cents a litre , roughly 54 per cent higher than the Canadian average , according to the NEB . Transit , provincial and federal taxes add another 52 cents per litre in Metro Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The BCUC could compel testimony and documents from companies as part of its review . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The inquiry panel is currently reviewing these submissions and will determine if further information is required once the review has been completed , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;chair and CEO David Mortin said in a statement . &lt;/span&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If part of a submission has been identified as &amp;lsquo ; commercially sensitive&amp;rsquo ; , the BCUC is required to safeguard the confidential information . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The oil and gas companies offered various explanations for the price of gas and diesel at the pumps in their written submissions , with many citing U.S. benchmarks , limited capacity on the existing Trans Mountain Pipeline , the increased cost of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s low-carbon gasoline standards , a rising minimum wage , and other operational cost pressures . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suncor was one of several companies that blamed the federally owned Trans Mountain Pipeline for changes that have limited both the flow of crude and refined oil and gas for use in B.C. markets . Premier John Horgan has said he has talked to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau about trying to get more refined gas to B.C. through the existing pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;98217;TOOLONG ... title= " Tanks at the Trans Mountain Westridge Marine Terminal , Burnaby , November 29 2016. " alt= " Tanks at the Trans Mountain Westridge Marine Terminal , Burnaby , November 29 2016. " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;98253;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1038234 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Over the last 3-5 years , the Trans Mountain Pipeline has decreased its line space ( capacity ) allocated to finished products ( gasoline and diesel ) delivered to Suncor&amp;rsquo ; s terminals in British Columbia by approximately 30-36 ( million litres ) per month , which resulted in an increase in the more costly transportation of these products by rail and truck , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;98283;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;wrote Suncor&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The transportation of gasoline and diesel via truck and rail has increased significantly since 2015 &amp;hellip ; due to the decrease in TMPL line space ( capacity ) allocated to Suncor&amp;rsquo ; s finished products . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;98318;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Imperial Oil&lt;/a&gt; , which transports gas and diesel from its Strathcona refinery near Edmonton but does not operate retail gas stations in B.C. , said Trans Mountain has let it ship less product to B.C. in the past five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a result of pipeline apportionment on the Trans Mountain Pipeline , Imperial has : increased the amount of refined products it ships to British Columbia by rail and marine vessel , which are typically more expensive means of transportation than transportation by pipeline ; and secured increased storage to serve the Vancouver area and increased marine logistics from that facility to Imperial&amp;rsquo ; s existing distribution facilities in British Columbia . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Parkland Fuel Corp. , which operates B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s largest refinery in Burnaby , said Trans Mountain is the only viable way to get the light crude it needs to refine into gasoline , but it can not get enough product . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because of capacity constraints on the Trans Mountain Pipeline and the method used to allocate capacity on the pipeline , the refinery is often forced to purchase space ( if possible ) from other shippers , which comes at a significant premium over tariff rates , &amp;rdquo ; it wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. Utilities Commission will not examine whether the twinned Trans Mountain Pipeline expansion project &amp;mdash ; recently approved by Ottawa &amp;mdash ; would help drive down Vancouver gas prices . But Parkland said that expansion would help address the supply issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;98353;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;98395;TOOLONG ... Palmer : Hurry-up inquiry will feature duo of petroleum industry **28;98430;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;98460;TOOLONG ... energy watchdog confirms taxes are n't part of gas price **28;98495;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trans Mountain said Tuesday its pipeline has operated at capacity for more than a decade , and the amount of requests to ship product exceeds the available space . Capacity is allotted using a process the National Energy Board approves , which mixes historical usage and export contracts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s low-carbon fuel standards were also cited as a cost pressure by several companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suncor said the wholesale price of gas is driven by North American and international markets , while &amp;ldquo ; wholesale ( rack ) prices in Vancouver are highly correlated to wholesale market prices in the U.S. Pacific North West ( Portland ) . &amp;rdquo ; An extended unplanned refinery outage on the U.S. west coast in 2019 drove up the Portland price higher than other markets , which also drove up Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s price , Suncor wrote in its submission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suncor , which operates an Edmonton refinery , also blamed a 30-per-cent increase in overall petroleum costs over the past five years , as well as a 30-per-cent increase in maintenance , 20-per-cent increase in rental expenses , and a 21-per-cent increase in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s minimum wage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shell cited increased operational costs for storage , distribution by truck to gas stations , maintenance , municipal and property taxes , and other price drivers . The company barges refined gasoline from the U.S. Pacific Northwest to supply its storage terminals in Burnaby and Chemainus , as well as gathers diesel by rail from Alberta . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; However , in general , gasoline and diesel ( collectively , &amp;lsquo ; fuel&amp;rsquo ; ) prices , like all commodities , are strongly influenced by supply and demand , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;98525;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;the company wrote&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;ldquo ; Generally speaking , there are four primary cost components to Shell&amp;rsquo ; s retail fuel price : crude oil , taxes , refiner margin , and marketing margin . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Husky cited the carbon tax , B.C. Hydro rates , credit card fees , health taxes , leasing costs and an average two-per-cent annual increase to the cost of labour as factors that drove up wholesale prices in recent years , Husky said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Husky operates a refinery in Prince George , which is the smallest of its kind in Canada and provides most of the gas and diesel for northern B.C. It averages 83 per cent capacity in its refinery , using local crude , which Husky said is in the &amp;ldquo ; top half&amp;rdquo ; for performance at North American refineries. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Minimum wage has had a substantial negative impact on site level costs and has been the largest driver to cost increases at the retail site level for gasoline and diesel , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;98560;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;wrote Husky&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas prices at the pump at corporately owned Husky stores are set by a &amp;ldquo ; district manager in the region&amp;rdquo ; in consultation with headquarters in Calgary , whereas independently owned Husky stores set their own prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Generally , pricing is based on overall market conditions , particularly movement in the prevailing publicly posted local rack price and information conveyed to the District Manager on local market conditions by site Operators ( &amp;lsquo ; Retailers&amp;rsquo ; ) , &amp;rdquo ; Husky wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Husky headquarters also may direct the District Manager to either increase or reduce prices in order to increase either profits per litre or volume of gasoline sold respectively . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 7-Eleven &lt;a href= " https : **33;98595;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;redacted most of its cost data&lt;/a&gt; as part of the detailed retail margins it submitted to the commission in confidence . However , it did cite the higher cost of summer gasoline and wildfires as cost drivers on the price at the pump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;98630;TOOLONG " **33;98658;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;98693;TOOLONG " &gt;t ... &lt;/p&gt; Rob ShawWed , 03 Jul 2019 16:38:11 **45;98722;TOOLONG ... NewsPoliticsFeds spend $275 million on $40-billion LNG Canada project , including cash to buy gas turbineshttp : **32;98769;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;98803;TOOLONG ... title= " Federal Finance Minister Bill Morneau announced a $275-million contribution to support LNG development in Kitimat. " alt= " Federal Finance Minister Bill Morneau announced a $275-million contribution to support LNG development in Kitimat. " data-portal-copyright= " Jonathan Hayward " **28;98839;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 809980 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;98869;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa is putting up $275 million in federal support for LNG Canada&amp;rsquo ; s $40-billion liquefied natural gas development in Kitimat as an investment in &amp;ldquo ; cleaner technology&amp;rdquo ; to get Canadian resources to new markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The contribution will include $220 million to help LNG Canada buy more energy-efficient gas turbines to power its natural gas liquefaction plant , and $55 million to replace an aging highway bridge in Kitimat on the road that leads to the town&amp;rsquo ; s industrial area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal Finance Minister Bill Morneau travelled to Kitimat on Monday for the announcement , where he said the project , the largest private-sector investment in the country&amp;rsquo ; s history , would diversify Canada&amp;rsquo ; s trade , grow the economy and create middle-class jobs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a vote of confidence in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s resource industry and is good news for Canadians right across the country , &amp;rdquo ; Morneau said in a written statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To B.C. Green party leader Andrew Weaver , however , the move is another burst of corporate welfare to subsidize the fossil fuel industry a week after Prime Minister Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s government declared a climate emergency and re-approved the Trans Mountain Pipeline expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You wonder why Canadians are cynical about the way this government is dealing with the climate crisis , &amp;rdquo ; Weaver said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Government , however , is betting on being able to earn credit for helping countries displace more carbon-intensive energy with lower greenhouse-gas-emission LNG. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; LNG Canada&amp;rsquo ; s facility will help bring a cleaner Canadian energy source to replace coal in some of the world&amp;rsquo ; s fastest-growing economies , &amp;rdquo ; said Navdeep Bains , the federal minister of innovation , science and economic development , whose Strategic Innovation Fund will put up the $220 million for the high-efficiency turbines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; LNG Canada CEO Andy Calitz said the payment will enable the company to develop &amp;ldquo ; the lowest carbon content LNG for export in the world today . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To Weaver , however , the spending is still a subsidy to what will become B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s single-largest source for carbon dioxide emissions and one of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s biggest , in addition to other tax breaks and support that the industry has received . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renewable energy proponents have leaned on the LNG sector to electrify its process as much as possible to reduce the industry&amp;rsquo ; s greenhouse-gas impacts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last fall , when LNG Canada announced its final investment decision , Clean Energy B.C. called on the province to &amp;ldquo ; seize the opportunity to extensively electrify the existing fossil fuel infrastructure as much as possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a white paper , the organization , which represents B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s private-power producers , argued that&amp;nbsp ; electrifying LNG , from drilling natural gas in the northeast to transporting it by pipeline for processing in Kitimat , could reduce total emissions of conventional LNG production by up to 65 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Monday , Clean Energy B.C. executive director Martin Mullany said they understand that LNG Canada&amp;rsquo ; s timelines are too short to electrify the process to compress and freeze natural gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Hydro wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have enough time to plan for , submit to an environmental review , then build an additional power transmission line to deliver the amount of electricity such a plant would need , Mullany said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , he argued there is still time to make sure the upstream natural gas drilling , processing and pipeline are powered by electricity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If the federal government wants to spend funds subsidizing a project where a final investment decision has been made , that is their choice , &amp;rdquo ; Mullany said in an emailed response to questions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We think it would have been better spend on electrifying the upstream value chain , but perhaps it is not an either/or decision , &amp;rdquo ; Mullany said . &amp;ldquo ; Perhaps they intend to fund electrification as well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;98905;TOOLONG " **36;98936;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;98974;TOOLONG " &gt;tw ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerTue , 25 Jun 2019 15:15:40 **45;99003;TOOLONG ... Click here . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; The expansion of the Trans Mountain Pipeline approved this week by the federal government will increase its capacity to 890,000 barrels a day of crude oil , diluted bitumen and refined fuels such as gasoline and aviation fuel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Burnaby officially opposes the expansion project , it must nonetheless plan for its arrival . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trans Mountain&amp;rsquo ; s Burnaby Terminal will require 14 new storage tanks in addition to the 13 already on site , raising the total capacity from 1.6 million barrels to 5.5 million barrels . The expansion will include new containment berms , a larger fire-protection water reservoir , and a foam cannon for fire suppression . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have residents that live very close by , and there are residents on the Simon Fraser University Campus that would have to be evacuated . The area will be a forest of high-rise residential towers by then . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concord Pacific Metrotown is a three-tower behemoth topping out at 65 storeys and more than 1,300 housing units that is now under construction . Nearby , Two Gilmore Place is a towering 64 storeys . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Density in the area could eventually rival Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s West End . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lougheed Town Centre is also expanding with a master plan that allows 23 new towers built with four 65-storey towers and seven others up to 55 storeys . Southgate City at Edmonds Station is expected to see 20 more residential towers constructed over the next 20 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Those towers present quite a challenge , so we are talking about that with our chiefs and bringing experts through this ( request for proposal ) to ensure we have the right coverage in those massive buildings , &amp;rdquo ; said Hurley . &amp;ldquo ; The number of firefighters needed to get equipment to the 50th floor and above is substantial . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Burnaby Fire Department has 286 members and a $40-million annual budget . The terminal will need to be expanded with new berths , offices and equipment to handle the tripled capacity of oil once the expansion project is finished . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Westridge undergoes construction , the actual pipeline will continue to be built west of Edmonton , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;100105;TOOLONG ... title= " Construction work on the Kinder Morgan Burnaby Terminal and Tank Farm is pictured in Burnaby , British Columbia on June 20 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;100207;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1038230 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Coldwater Indian Band announced Thursday it will apply to the Federal Court of Appeal for a judicial review of the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s approval of the project because Ottawa has failed to take steps to protect its main water reserve , 12 kilometres outside of Merritt , from contamination . The approved route passes directly through the community&amp;rsquo ; s aquifer recharge zone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We had hoped for meaningful dialogue about measures to protecting our water , &amp;rdquo ; Chief Lee Spahan said in a statement Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That has not occurred . Instead , Canada has deferred to Trans Mountain to talk to us about routing in the future , with no guarantee our water or other rights and title interests will be protected . To make matters worse , this all happened at the very last-minute , so there has been no meaningful dialogue on the commitment . It may have left us in a worse position . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anderson said he is committed to Coldwater that the pipeline route could change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;100237;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;100279;TOOLONG ... fire department bracing for expanded pipeline and a forest of **32;100314;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;100348;TOOLONG ... tanker-ban bill squeaks through final vote in **27;100383;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have several alternatives that we&amp;rsquo ; re prepared to consider , different than the route that has been approved currently , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m committed to that community to undertake reviews of those alternatives with them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spahan said a key hydrogeological study to understand the risks posed to the aquifer by the pipeline corridor is not yet done , and that key information is being undermined by the pipeline approval and fast-tracked discussions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anderson said timing isn&amp;rsquo ; t a pressure . &amp;ldquo ; We have time build into the schedule to accommodate any route deviation of Coldwater that we agree to with them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Trans Mountain project&amp;rsquo ; s varying terrain from Burnaby to Edmonton also impacts the construction schedule , said Anderson . The company can&amp;rsquo ; t do any work related to clearing trees before Aug. 25 , he said . There are also annual periods of non-construction to protect migratory birds , as well as fisheries timing restrictions at the Westbank terminal . Work on the Coquihalla is also limited due to the early and long winter conditions , said Anderson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;100412;TOOLONG " **33;100440;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;100475;TOOLONG " &gt;t ... &lt;/p&gt; Rob ShawFri , 21 Jun 2019 16:51:34 **45;100504;TOOLONG ... **25;100551;TOOLONG Nations ownership in Trans Mountain poses public relations problem for opponentshttp : **31;100578;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;100611;TOOLONG ... title= " Michael LeBourdais , chair of the Western Indigenous Pipeline Group and chief of the Whispering Pines/Clinton Indian Band , said his group has been talking about an ownership state in the pipeline since 2013. " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;100647;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1037416 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;100677;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The re-approval of the Trans Mountain expansion throws open the door for pro-pipeline First Nations to gain an ownership stake in the project , which could pose a public relations dilemma for opponents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa will kick off a series of meetings with interested First Nations starting July 22 in Vancouver , with stops in Victoria , Kamloops and Edmonton . The government is prepared to discuss equity ownership , revenue sharing and royalty agreements with 129 First Nations , according to the department of finance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Interest from First Nations is considerable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Indian Resource Council &amp;mdash ; which represents more than 130 First Nations with oil and gas resources on their territories &amp;mdash ; has already consulted with the federal government and led preliminary meetings with First Nations about making a bid for the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government bought the Trans Mountain pipeline for $4.5 billion last summer when Kinder Morgan threatened to cancel a planned expansion due to delays in the approval process and legal challenges brought by a handful of First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;100713;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;100755;TOOLONG ... Those benefits are typically paid out over 20 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; First Nations ownership would undercut the environmental movement , because many of the people who oppose the pipeline are likely to support self-determination for First Nations , &amp;rdquo ; said a UBC sociology professor , David Tindall. &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s going to be big dilemma for the environmental movement and from a public opinion point of view , I think people could question continuing to fight the pipeline in that context . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;101257;TOOLONG ... title= " A rally of opponents to the Trans Mountain pipeline and tanker project in Vancouver BC. , June 9 , 2019. Indian Chiefs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not an issue of who owns the pipeline and its infrastructure , it&amp;rsquo ; s a matter of what flows through the pipeline because bitumen is the most toxic material on the planet , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are layers of elected and hereditary leaders in First Nations governance and their interests sometimes conflict , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While elected councils can negotiate benefits and have authority over reserve lands , hereditary leaders are often charged with protecting much larger traditional territories . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Title is territory-wide , it encompasses the entire footprint of the territory and the proper titleholders are the individual members of the First Nation community , not the elected band councils , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two coastal First Nations &amp;mdash ; Squamish and the Tsleil-Waututh &amp;mdash ; have served notice that they intend to appeal Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s approval of the expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Squamish Nation has not explored ways to derive economic benefit from the project nor do they plan to join First Nations exploring the purchase of the project and its assets , said Squamish First Nation Councillor Khelsilem. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;101323;TOOLONG ... title= " Squamish First Nation Coun. NewsNationalB.C. and Ottawa still sparring over ' gaps ' in oil spill response planshttp : **35;101567;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;101604;TOOLONG ... title= " Tug boats prepare a oil tanker to go under the Second Narrows bridge in Burrard Inlet , just outside of metro Vancouver , B.C. , Tuesday , May , 1 , 2018.
