" data-portal-copyright= " THE CANADIAN PRESS/Jonathan Hayward " **28;101640;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 996055 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s fisheries minister says he doesn&amp;rsquo ; t know about the &amp;ldquo ; gaps&amp;rdquo ; B.C. keeps saying exist in Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s oil spill response plan , though the province insists it has been highlighting them for more than a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fisheries and Oceans Minister Jonathan Wilkinson said his government doesn&amp;rsquo ; t know why Premier John Horgan again cited &amp;ldquo ; gaps in the ocean protection plan&amp;rdquo ; at a news conference Tuesday . Horgan was responding to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s re-approval for the expansion of the Trans Mountain pipeline expansion from Alberta to B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; To be honest with you , I&amp;rsquo ; m not sure we fully understand what gaps the province is referring to , &amp;rdquo ; Wilkinson said Wednesday . &amp;ldquo ; But we have said to them repeatedly we are more than open to have a conversation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson said he has a good relationship with the B.C. government , monthly discussions with Horgan and talked to provincial officials on Wednesday . Yet , the &amp;ldquo ; gaps&amp;rdquo ; remain ill-defined , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If B.C. can more clearly articulate to us the specific issues they are concerned about and some of the ideas they &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa just finished a third phase of consultations with First Nations communities , in which 60 officials from 13 federal departments held 402 meetings with Aboriginal groups with the goal to amend the Trans Mountain plans where possible to address their concerns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When asked how it&amp;rsquo ; s possible the federal government could still be unclear about B.C. concerns regarding oil spill response after a year of intense B.C. community consultations , Wilkinson pointed to Premier Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s longtime political opposition to the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the B.C. government has made no secret of the fact that it has opposed this project , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; As they said during the election campaign , they were intending to use all the tools in the tool box . I think there is an increasing recognition on the part of the premier and his government that the pipeline rests in federal jurisdiction . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;101670;TOOLONG ... title= " The Coast Guard on the scene of a spill in English Bay April 15 , 2016. " alt= " The Coast Guard on the scene of a spill in English Bay April 15 , 2016. " data-portal-copyright= " Photo by Bryce Westlund , SKY Helicopters " **28;101706;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 449138 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Environment Minister George Heyman said he&amp;rsquo ; s surprised Ottawa refuses to acknowledge B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s concerns about marine response after almost two years of meetings among ministers , deputy minsters and provincial officials , as well concerns put into letters to federal departments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I honestly can&amp;rsquo ; t explain why Minister Wilkinson would say they don&amp;rsquo ; t know or are not clear , &amp;rdquo ; said Heyman. &amp;ldquo ; We were pretty clear in our written submissions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. has identified five gaps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They include wanting a &amp;ldquo ; transparent and comprehensive emergency towing strategy that has a clear requirement regarding response times to ensure that towing capacity is available , &amp;rdquo ; said Heyman. &amp;ldquo ; We think that should be completed and it hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been and it&amp;rsquo ; s not a condition of the recent decision . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province also &amp;ldquo ; needs some clarity on enhanced cost recovery provisions in the event of a spill&amp;rdquo ; so that First Nations , local communities and the provincial government get &amp;ldquo ; guaranteed fair and timely access to funds&amp;rdquo ; and don&amp;rsquo ; t have to wait for lengthy federal reimbursement procedures , said Heyman . He pointed to the Heiltsuk Nation&amp;rsquo ; s frustrations getting compensation from Ottawa after helping to contain the Nathan E. Stewart diesel spill near Bella Bella in 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;101736;TOOLONG ... title= " An aerial view of the Nathan E. Stewart tug , which went aground spilling diesel fuel into waters claimed by the Heiltsuk Nation territory . " alt= " An aerial view of the Nathan E. Stewart tug , which went aground spilling diesel fuel into waters claimed by the Heiltsuk Nation territory . " data-portal-copyright= " Marilyn Slett " **28;101772;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 755123 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two other B.C. concerns were partly addressed by Ottawa as part of its approval announcement &amp;mdash ; support for co-developing community response plans to a spill in specific areas , and funding to train and purchase marine spill equipment for local communities and First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Heyman said that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t go far enough and B.C. wants specific geographic response plans instead of regional plans , and a long-term commitment to Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s equipment funding instead of the current five-year promise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. also wants more transparent information from Ottawa on the types and volumes of substances being transported by rail , pipeline and marine vessels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government has promised a $1.6 billion oceans protection plan that includes new Canadian Coast Guard ships to respond to oil spills faster and with more on-scene environmental cleanup tools . It also reopened the Kitsilano Coast Guard base , installed six new radar stations , is mapping of high-traffic commercial waterways , and will spend more than $220 million to protect endangered killer whales by reducing vessel noises , expand buffer zones and restricting chinook salmon fishing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New measures announced Tuesday with the pipeline approval include real-time marine information for First Nations communities , fish habitat restoration and long-term monitoring of the Salish Sea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s continued concerns are echoed by environmental groups . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;101802;TOOLONG ... title= " Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman at a February 2018 news conference in Victoria . " alt= " Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman at a February 2018 news conference in Victoria . " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;101838;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 779707 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Critics of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; world class&amp;rdquo ; spill response often point to the International Tanker Owners Pollution Federation&amp;rsquo ; s estimate that even in ideal conditions in oceans around the world , only 10 to 15 per cent of spilled oil is likely to be recovered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The concept of world-class spill response is somewhat meaningless , &amp;rdquo ; said Jay Ritchlin of the David Suzuki Foundation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ritchlin said the foundation has modelled local winds , waves and currents and has concerns that a spill would drift up the Burrard Inlet into sensitive bird habitat , and into the Georgia Strait where orcas and the chinook on which they feed would be affected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New federal rules introduced this month to protect southern resident killer whales off B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s coast may not go far enough to protect that population , and an increase in tanker traffic will only increase their stress , Ritchlin said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meantime , he sees a number of potential threats : &amp;ldquo ; The ongoing presence of additional ships , the noise they create , the possibility for hitting the whales and , of course , the very rare but incredibly catastrophic chance of an actual major spill . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chief Leah George-Wilson of the Tsleil-Waututh Nation is troubled by continuing scientific debate about the behaviour of diluted bitumen , or dilbit , when it is spilled in water . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A 2016 study funded by the federal government found that dilbit doesn&amp;rsquo ; t sink as readily as conventional oil when spilled in fresh water . But a 2015 report by the U.S. National Academy of Science showed that dilbit tended to quickly sink after being spilled in fresh water . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think they don&amp;rsquo ; t have enough science to understand how dilbit will react in salt or fresh water , &amp;rdquo ; George-Wilson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;101868;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;101910;TOOLONG ... Mountain pipeline construction should resume by September , says CEO&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;101945;TOOLONG ... If it sinks , it&amp;rsquo ; s going to be harder to clean up . If it impacts the Fraser River estuary , that&amp;rsquo ; s huge . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; George-Wilson said Canadians should be worried about how increased tanker noise might affect orcas off B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s coast . She pointed to recent orca sightings in the Burrard Inlet , where the herring population has recovered . Howe Sound experienced a similar return of herring and , subsequently , orcas , in recent years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s amazing , &amp;rdquo ; Georga-Wilson said . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s showing us that the ecosystem is still viable , and we can still save it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sarah Beuhler , a spokeswoman for Protect the Inlet , is also concerned about sinking dilbit. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Regular crude oil &amp;mdash ; just light crude oil &amp;mdash ; ( cleanup ) has a 10 to 15 per cent success rate only , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s what they&amp;rsquo ; re trumpeting as world class , and that&amp;rsquo ; s not good enough for our coast . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Beuhler said that beyond the environmental concerns a spill would bring , 100,000 tourism jobs and Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s brand as a green city depend on having clean beaches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She described the use of booms during cleanups as &amp;ldquo ; theatre , &amp;rdquo ; given their ineffectiveness in poor weather . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It dissipates , it sinks , it&amp;rsquo ; s gone , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know the B.C. government is supposed to come out with new ( oil spill regulations ) soon but , frankly , until somebody can prove that dilbit can be cleaned up instead of sitting down there and poisoning the water for hundreds of years , we&amp;rsquo ; re not moved . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;102012;TOOLONG " **33;102040;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;102075;TOOLONG " **36;102106;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Rob Shaw , Nick EaglandThu , 20 Jun 2019 00:49:30 **45;102144;TOOLONG ... This is like declaring war on cancer and then announcing a campaign to promote smoking . But this is far from a done deal . First Nations and Canadian environmentalists will continue to fight this project and their international allies will support them in whatever way they can . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Will George , who led the protest movement called Protect The Inlet , promised &amp;ldquo ; undeterred resistance&amp;rdquo ; to the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; No matter who approves it , this pipeline will not be built , &amp;rdquo ; George said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People in British Columbia are the ones risking disaster from spills and we&amp;rsquo ; re prepared to do whatever it takes to stop this pipeline , &amp;rdquo ; George continued . &amp;ldquo ; Tonight in downtown Vancouver we&amp;rsquo ; ll show our opposition to this destructive pipeline and on Saturday we will further escalate our actions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Supporters of the pipeline held a rally at noon in downtown Vancouver and opponents are set to rally at 5:30 pm in downtown Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saturday&amp;rsquo ; s protest will be centred around a 20km march in Victoria between Centennial Square to Island View Beach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;JUST IN : The Trans Mountain Expansion Project has received a green light from the federal cabinet . Read &lt;a href= " https : **27;102796;TOOLONG ? src=h ... full reaction and news release here : &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/akjnP5wyF1 " &gt;https : //t.c ... &lt;a href= " https : **29;102825;TOOLONG ? src ... &lt;a href= " https : **28;102856;TOOLONG ? src= ... &lt;a href= " https : **25;102886;TOOLONG ? src=has ... &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/avhKONQK32 " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- Greater Vancouver Board of Trade ( @BoardofTrade ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;102913;TOOLONG ... 18 , **33;102948;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;102983;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Greater Vancouver Board of Trade issued a released stating the approval was the right decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; After careful consideration , analysis , and a rigorous environmental assessment , cabinet has rightfully chosen to forge ahead with this project , given the critical importance of this infrastructure to our national economy , &amp;rdquo ; said Iain Black , president of the Greater Vancouver Board of Trade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We applaud the federal government for making this decision . It is high time that we move forward , collectively as Canadians , to build this infrastructure and get our natural resources to international markets . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Business Council of B.C. issued a string of statements from members , all in favour of the project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Today&amp;rsquo ; s news that the TMX pipeline has been approved is welcome news &amp;ndash ; but it&amp;rsquo ; s long overdue . We call on the federal government to move quickly from approval to construction , so we can the unlock benefits of this project for all Canadians , &amp;rdquo ; said Val Litwin , president of the B.C. Chamber of Commerce . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anita Huberman , of the Surrey Board of Trade , said pipelines were crucial to the economy and generated investment , created jobs and contributed to growth in all areas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have some semblance of economic certainty again in Canada , &amp;rdquo ; said Huberman. &amp;ldquo ; International investment confidence in Canada has become a concern as a result of this prolonged delay . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thousands of construction workers are now waiting for the go ahead to get to work on building the pipeline , said Paul de Jong , president of the Progressive Contractors Association of Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll believe this project is moving forward when the shovels are finally in the ground , &amp;rdquo ; said de Jong . &amp;ldquo ; While today&amp;rsquo ; s decision is the right one , it&amp;rsquo ; s far from a ringing endorsement when the government is working so hard to kill major resource projects through legislation and regulations designed to make energy development less likely in the future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; With thousands of jobs , billions in economic benefits and Canada&amp;rsquo ; s credibility on the line , there&amp;rsquo ; s too much at stake not to get this project built now , not months from now after an election . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The expansion of the pipeline from Edmonton , Alberta , to the Burnaby waterfront would add 590,000 barrels of daily shipping capacity , a 15 per cent boost to Western Canada&amp;rsquo ; s current four million . The project would still need to get local building permits , overcome legal challenges and likely face pushback from environmentalists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Green party leader Andrew Weaver said the decision was &amp;ldquo ; an abdication of their responsibility to Canadians to show climate leadership . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; No compelling business case has been made for the expansion , with Kinder Morgan offloading the risk onto the Canadian taxpayer . Proceeding with the Trans Mountain expansion is a reckless use of taxpayer money . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt; " The Liberal government 's decision to forge ahead with the Trans Mountain Expansion project is an abdication of their responsibility to Canadians to show climate leadership , " says &lt;a href= " https : **27;103018;TOOLONG ? refs ... @AJWVictoriaBC&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **28;103047;TOOLONG ? src= ... &lt;a href= " https : **25;103077;TOOLONG ? src=has ... href= " https : //t.co/0Mmu3RsueK " &gt;https : //t.c ... &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/8bLGDhKeVB " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- BC Green Party ( @BCGreens ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;103104;TOOLONG ... 18 , **33;103139;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;103174;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The group , led by Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs president Grand Chief Stewart Phillip , and representatives from the Secwepemc Nation , the Tsleil-Waututh First Nation and the Squamish Nation are opposed to the pipeline extension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;With &lt;a href= " https : **25;103209;TOOLONG ? src=has ... decision minutes away , important to recall that cabinet must wait for the report of consultations with First Nations as per the legal requirement set by the Fed Court last year . Today is likely to be a re-iteration of support by Ottawa . I will be looking for a timetable&lt;/p&gt; -- Dan McTeague ( @GasBuddyDan ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;103236;TOOLONG ... 18 , **33;103271;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;103306;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; The expansion could be the last pipeline to get built under current permitting rules and , according to the business sector , would go a long way to improve investor sentiment on the Canadian energy sector . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s government bought the pipeline last year from Kinder Morgan in a bid to save its expansion , only to see a court strike down the permit due to lack of consultations . That ruling set out a framework for the current approval by adding additional regulatory requirements and outlining the need for more consultation with Indigenous communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , Trudeau said the government wanted to sell the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Project Reconciliation &amp;mdash ; an Indigenous-led organization that aims to buy a majority share in the pipeline &amp;mdash ; commended the government Tuesday on its consultation record in Phase III . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s inviting First Nations to the table , &amp;rdquo ; said Delbert Wapass , founder and executive chair of the organization , who is also a former chief . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wapass said in an interview with Postmedia that he shares the same vision of shared prosperity with Indigenous peoples and environmental protection that the prime minister articulated in his remarks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are the first stewards of the land , &amp;rdquo ; said Wapass , noting that this puts Indigenous peoples in the ideal position to lead the project . The environment is paramount . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kent Fellows , a research associate in energy and environmental policy at the University of Calgary School of Public policy said that the announcement that all profits from the eventual sale of the project would be invested in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s transition to a clean energy economy is a sign the government is taking climate change seriously . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Regardless of how big a contribution ( to emissions reduction ) , every little bit does help , &amp;rdquo ; said Fellows on Tuesday . &amp;ldquo ; This is the federal government doing as much as it can right now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But not all experts agree this is the right move given the national climate emergency declared by the House of Commons on Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kathryn Harrison , a professor of political science at the University of British Columbia , isn&amp;rsquo ; t surprised by the decision but is skeptical that approving the project and taking climate emergency seriously can be aligned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;103341;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s very hard to reconcile the two , &amp;rdquo ; said Harrison on Tuesday , pointing out that the &amp;ldquo ; scale and rapidity&amp;rdquo ; of climate action urged to prevent &amp;ldquo ; irreversible&amp;rdquo ; damage by an October report by the International Panel on Climate Change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Harrison pointed out that further investing in fossil fuels to fund a clean transition could leave taxpayers on the hooked for a &amp;ldquo ; stranded asset&amp;rdquo ; if the global economy does in fact transition away from extractive energy sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If not , Harrison said Canada is essentially betting on the failure of the Paris Agreement on Climate Change , an international agreement aimed at limiting global warming to 1.5 degrees Celcius that was ratified by Canada in 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At a time when we need to try harder , how do we reconcile that with increasing emissions ? &amp;rdquo ; said Harrison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Environmental groups in Canada are equally skeptical that expanding Canada&amp;rsquo ; s most carbon-intense industry will help fight climate change . Since 1990 , fossil fuel extraction accounted for 75 per cent of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s carbon emissions growth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Approving the Trans Mountain Pipeline is inconsistent with our government&amp;rsquo ; s declaration of a climate emergency , &amp;rdquo ; said Tzeporah Berman , international program director at environmental organization Stand.earth , in a Tuesday news release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If we are going to fight climate change in Canada , we need to face the fact that we can no longer expand fossil fuel production and infrastructure , &amp;rdquo ; she continued . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The David Suzuki foundation echoed Harrison in pointing out that potential pipeline profits are not the only option government has to fund a green economic transition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Funding for clean energy projects is essential , but there are better ways than by expanding fossil fuel infrastructure , &amp;rdquo ; said science and policy director Ian Bruce in a news release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Today&amp;rsquo ; s decision locks Canada into carbon-intensive infrastructure , challenging our country&amp;rsquo ; s ability to move toward a healthier environment and to lead in the growing clean-energy sector . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Check here for live updates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; with files from Postmedia News and Canadian Press &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;103376;TOOLONG " **36;103407;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; David CarriggWed , 19 Jun 2019 18:21:23 **45;103445;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsNationalDisappointed Horgan says B.C. will continue with Trans Mountain pipeline fighthttp : **35;103492;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;103529;TOOLONG ... title= " Premier John Horgan , right , and B.C. Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman , left . " alt= " Premier John Horgan , right , and B.C. Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman , left . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;103565;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1036673 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;103595;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s premier is vowing to continue legal challenges against the Trans Mountain pipeline expansion , in what may now be mostly symbolic opposition to a project Ottawa has again approved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John Horgan said he&amp;rsquo ; s disappointed the federal government gave another green light Tuesday to a plan to twin the existing pipeline from near Edmonton to Burnaby , which will triple capacity to 890,000 barrels a day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. has maintained an oil spill on the ocean from increased tanker traffic would be catastrophic , though it has also acknowledged it lacks the power to regulate or ban tankers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While B.C. will continue two court challenges , Horgan said the province will grant any lawfully requested permits to start construction on the twinned pipeline this summer . &amp;ldquo ; Although I regret the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s decision , it is within their authority to make that decision , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The premier did not rule out throwing the B.C. government&amp;rsquo ; s support behind future First Nations or environmental challenges , but said he&amp;rsquo ; d consider it on a case-by-case basis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s approval did not come as a surprise , given the federal government purchased the pipeline from Kinder Morgan for $4.5 billion in 2018 , saying its expansion was in the national interest to get more oil from Alberta to overseas markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s entrenched opposition is equally unsurprising , given the B.C. NDP campaigned in 2017 &amp;ldquo ; to use every tool in our tool box to stop the project from going ahead&amp;rdquo ; and its minority government holds power through an agreement with the B.C. Green party that says the province must do everything it can to block Trans Mountain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it appears Horgan is mostly out of options , said Richard Johnston , Canada Research Chair in public opinion , elections and representation at the University of B.C. &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re into a kind of symbolic phase now as far as the B.C. government is concerned , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What else can he do ? It&amp;rsquo ; s clear he can&amp;rsquo ; t engage in permitting actions whose obvious intent is to destroy the pipeline , the courts have made that clear . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. has issued 310 permits so far for the Trans Mountain project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;103631;TOOLONG ... title= " Premier John Horgan , right , and B.C. Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman , left . " alt= " Premier John Horgan , right , and B.C. Minister of Environment and Climate Change Strategy George Heyman , left . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;103667;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1036678 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan said B.C. is asking the Supreme Court of Canada to review proposed legislation &amp;mdash ; which the B.C. Court of Appeal ruled unconstitutional &amp;mdash ; to restrict the flow of oilsands bitumen into B.C. on environmental grounds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And B.C. will also continue to challenge Alberta legislation that threatens to curtail the flow of oil to B.C. through the existing pipeline if B.C. continues to oppose the expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta Premier Jason Kenney praised the federal approval , and called for immediate construction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This second approval of the Trans Mountain pipeline isn&amp;rsquo ; t a victory to celebrate , it&amp;rsquo ; s just another step in a process that has , frankly , taken too long , &amp;rdquo ; said Kenney. &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s why we&amp;rsquo ; ll measure success not by today&amp;rsquo ; s decision , but by the beginning of actual construction and , more importantly , by completion of the pipeline . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau said Canada must twin the pipeline to improve the economy , but that he is also confident of the environmental protections put in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; " To British Columbians who worry about a spill , for example , know that we take your concerns very seriously , " said Trudeau . &lt;/span&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our top priority is making sure there&amp;rsquo ; s no spill in the first place . But we know we need to be prepared for anything . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Environment Minister George Heyman said there are gaps in Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s proposed marine response plan &amp;mdash ; mainly around spill preparedness and response capacity for local governments and First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First Nations and environmental groups lined up Tuesday to denounce the decision and promise protests and court action . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau said he&amp;rsquo ; s confident his government has addressed concerns about a lack of meaningful First Nations consultation , and impact on endangered killer whales , raised by the Federal Court of Appeal when it quashed approval for the pipeline in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau also said he&amp;rsquo ; d welcome Aboriginal investment in the pipeline , which several Indigenous-backed groups have shown interest in pursuing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It could be an equity stake , revenue sharing , or something else altogether . We&amp;rsquo ; re coming to the table with an open mind . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Green Leader Andrew Weaver said he&amp;rsquo ; ll work to find additional ways to oppose Trans Mountain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Mr. Trudeau fails on the climate file , he fails on this file and frankly I think Canadians deserve better , &amp;rdquo ; said Weaver . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a sad day for all , that this has moved forward purely for political reasons and not for economic of environmental reasons . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Protests and legal challenges are likely to ramp up as construction begins in the coming weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is still a number of immediate steps to do in terms of permitting , &amp;rdquo ; said Trudeau . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; But the plan is to have shovels in the ground this summer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s approval of the pipeline sends a message that &amp;ldquo ; this is a government prepared to stay the course on the courses of action that it adopts , &amp;rdquo ; said Johnston . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;103697;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it could also come with serious ramifications for the Trudeau government&amp;rsquo ; s chances in B.C. during this fall&amp;rsquo ; s federal election , said Johnston . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the Liberals were already in serious trouble in parts of the country with the parts of the electorate that would be most concerned with this decision , environmentalists in B.C. and residents of the coast generally , &amp;rdquo ; said Johnston . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Liberals have lost ground in the polls . I think they are in serious trouble . In Alberta , this isn&amp;rsquo ; t going to salvage their position . No change there , they are just going to lose those seats I think . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And then for the rest of the country the election will turn on other questions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;103732;TOOLONG " **33;103760;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;103795;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;103824;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;mdash ; with a file from The Edmonton Journal &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;103855;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; ... " data-portal-copyright= " Zak Vescera " **28;104373;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1036804 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;104403;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The camp is empty except for a tattered banner reading &amp;ldquo ; Protect the Inlet . &amp;rdquo ; In front of it , a set of lawn chairs overlook the terminus of the Trans Mountain pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This observatory is the last remnant of Camp Cloud , a settlement of water protectors and activists on the edge of Burnaby Mountain who sought to stall the pipeline&amp;rsquo ; s expansion , a reminder that this community can not fully escape the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We talk about it a lot around here , &amp;rdquo ; says Tricia Drover , who has lived in the area for six years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drover doesn&amp;rsquo ; t oppose the pipeline , but she&amp;rsquo ; s worried about the procedures to follow if there is an accident at the terminal , especially as her child attends a nearby school , and the pipeline&amp;rsquo ; s impact on marine life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not against them being there &amp;mdash ; we obviously bought the house knowing the terminal is there &amp;mdash ; but I would appreciate more transparency , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; If there&amp;rsquo ; s an explosion , what do we do ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tuesday , the federal cabinet approved the project for a second time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Residents say the construction and consternation around the pipeline have changed the neighbourhood . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;104439;TOOLONG ... title= " Tara Shushtarian of Burnaby. " alt= " Tara Shushtarian of Burnaby. " data-portal-copyright= " Zak Vescera " **28;104475;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1036800 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s been a lot of tree cutting . Our streams have dried up . It&amp;rsquo ; s affected the biodiversity , &amp;rdquo ; says Tara Shushtarian. &amp;ldquo ; We used to have blue jays , chickadees &amp;mdash ; now nothing . &amp;ldquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shushtarian , who opposes the pipeline because of the climate-change crisis , is frustrated city officials haven&amp;rsquo ; t acted on what she calls &amp;ldquo ; constant&amp;rdquo ; noise and the smell of petroleum in the area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone&amp;rsquo ; s passing the buck around and no one&amp;rsquo ; s doing a thing about it . &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this community , Trans Mountain is a divisive topic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three residents told Postmedia they supported the project , but all refused to give their names citing fear of retaliation from neighbours or protesters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drover supports the right to protest , but says the overnight camp and the fires and pollution it produced were just as much a safety concern as the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;104505;TOOLONG ... title= " Scenes from Camp Cloud , summer of 2018. Photo : Nick Procaylo " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;105366;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1030584 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;105396;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Voices for and against the Trans Mountain pipeline and tanker project in B.C. are pushing to and beyond June 18 , when the Liberal government will decide whether or not to give it a green light . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re hopeful in anticipating the federal government will give a final approval , &amp;rdquo ; said Chris Gardner , president of the Independent Contractors and Businesses Association , which is in favour because the pipeline &amp;ldquo ; has been reviewed extensively&amp;rdquo ; and would bring jobs and tax revenues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government will decide at a cabinet meeting next week . Photo : Nick Procaylo " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;105468;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1030581 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Sunday , a few hundred people , including environmental and First Nations groups as well as concerned residents gathered for a few hours next to Science World to lodge their protest . NewsNationalPoliticsNic Rivers and Mark Jaccard : Greener fuels are n't as expensive as some claimhttp : **35;105647;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;105684;TOOLONG ... title= " A Vancouver gas station recently advertised prices starting at 170.9 cents per litre . " alt= " A Vancouver gas station recently advertised prices starting at 170.9 cents per litre . " data-portal-copyright= " JONATHAN HAYWARD " **28;105720;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1012561 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A jump in gasoline prices attracts a lot of attention . With prices recently hitting $1.70 per litre in the Vancouver area , Premier John Horgan has commissioned the B.C. Utilities Commission to assess whether this increase reflects the typical conditions of competitive markets or if the unusually high margins received by producers reflects insufficient competition and perhaps other factors . Understandably , the inquiry will not examine the effect of government policies on the gasoline price since the levels of various taxes are already publicly available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is one government policy , however , whose effect on the gasoline price is less transparent , which can lead to misinformation about its contribution . B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s low carbon fuel standard is one of a portfolio of greenhouse gas-reducing policies implemented by the B.C. Liberals in 2008 under former Premier Gordon Campbell . It requires that companies providing energy used in transportation reduce the carbon intensity of that energy by 10 per cent by 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To achieve this target , sellers of gasoline and diesel must either purchase and blend more low-emission fuels like ethanol and biodiesel into their fuels or pay other energy companies to increase their sales of low-emission energy used in transportation . They make these payments by purchasing the low-carbon energy credits , which the government allocates to sellers of ethanol , biodiesel , electricity , hydrogen and other low-emission forms of energy . Thus , B.C. Hydro sells to gasoline and diesel sellers the credits it receives for providing electricity to B.C. Transit for trolley buses and SkyTrain. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The oil industry has mostly complied with the low-carbon fuel standard by blending more biofuels into its products . Today , approximately 7.3 per cent of gasoline is ethanol and 5.4 per cent of diesel is biodiesel . If these gasoline and diesel substitutes are more expensive than the conventional fuels they replace , their addition has an upward effect on the price of gasoline and diesel . Indeed , a recent media report suggested that the low-carbon fuel standard has caused a 15-cent-per-litre increase in gasoline prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Our analysis reveals this report to be untrue . The low-carbon credits are publicly traded and their current price equates to $180 per tonne of CO2 . Assuming that money from low-carbon credit trades is used primarily to increase the consumption of ethanol and biodiesel , we estimate that wholesale ethanol costs on average over 20 cents more per litre than wholesale gasoline while wholesale biodiesel costs over 50 cents more than wholesale diesel . Given the current blend ratios for the two fuels , the low-carbon fuel standard adds only two to three cents per litre to the price of B.C. gasoline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The low impact of this policy on the price of gasoline is something the previous Liberal government was well aware of in 2008 , when we did a forecasting analysis of this and other climate policies . &amp;nbsp ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Rather than expose gasoline consumers to the high carbon taxes otherwise necessary for reducing transportation emissions , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;the&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; low-carbon fuel &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " **25;105750;TOOLONG &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; combines smaller price increases with support for increased use &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;of &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;low-emission forms of energy . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rapidly reducing transportation greenhouse gas emissions in wealthy countries like Canada supports the initiatives of China , Europe and other jurisdictions to replace gasoline consumption with electricity , biofuels and other low-emission energy . Quickly decarbonizing transportation around the globe is essential if humanity is to slow and then reverse the intensifying wildfires , floods , and storms , and the mass migration of people fleeing the worst impacts of climate change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a flexible regulation like the low-carbon standard to be effective in reducing gasoline and diesel consumption , the market must be competitive . To that end , an inquiry into B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s gasoline prices and its transportation energy market is welcome . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Nic Rivers is a Canada Research Chair at the University of Ottawa . Mark Jaccard is a professor at Simon Fraser University . Jaccard was chair of the B.C. Utilities Commission in 1996 when he led its inquiry into provincial gasoline prices . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //www.canadagaslng.com/ " &gt;&lt;i &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Letters to the editor should be sent to **27;105777;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;em&gt;The editorial pages editor is Gordon Clark , who can be reached at &lt;a href= " **27;105806;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;105835;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;105871;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? NewsCountries need renewable-energy allies to advance carbon-cutting developmentshttp : **28;107153;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;107183;TOOLONG ... title= " Catherine McKenna , minister of Environment and Climate Change , speaks at the 2019 Clean Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference in Vancouver on May 28. " alt= " Catherine McKenna , minister of Environment and Climate Change , speaks at the 2019 Clean Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference in Vancouver on May 28. " data-portal-copyright= " Derrick Penner / PNG " **28;107219;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1022992 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Countries need to co-operate in developing strategies to increase renewable energy and reduce carbon emissions to meet ambitious goals , a European Commission ( EC ) official said Tuesday at a major international conference in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The realization that countries aren&amp;rsquo ; t &amp;lsquo ; walking their talk&amp;rsquo ; enough to meet commitments made under the 2015 Paris climate agreement runs as an undercurrent to the Clean Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference , which is where finding allies is important , said Carlos Moedas , head of the EC&amp;rsquo ; s delegation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One of the things from the beginning that I put as a top priority of my agenda is collaboration between countries , &amp;rdquo ; said Moedas , the EC&amp;rsquo ; s commissioner for research , science and innovation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a time when things are so politically difficult outside of Europe &amp;ldquo ; we have to put more ( effort ) into collaboration , &amp;rdquo ; using science as the &amp;ldquo ; bridge-builder. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s conference has brought together energy ministers and top government officials from 25 countries , along with industry and non-governmental organization representatives to promote the development of renewable energy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And Moedas participated in a panel discussion sponsored by the online news outlet the National Observer that talked about how to make progress on climate goals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , at the outset to this year&amp;rsquo ; s conference , the International Energy Agency&amp;rsquo ; s ( IEA ) latest report highlighted a &amp;ldquo ; growing disconnect between climate ambitions and real-world energy trends . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a statement to Postmedia News , IEA executive director Fatih Birol said only one-third of global energy investment is flowing into low-carbon alternatives versus traditional energy sources and &amp;ldquo ; to meet our sustainable and climate goals , we need this proportion to reverse . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Clean Energy Ministerial ( meeting ) in Vancouver represents a very important opportunity to move forward on these important issues , &amp;rdquo ; Birol said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In that respect , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Natural Resources Minister , Amarjeet Sohi , said being able to demonstrate results is one way to encourage co-operation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Under ( the Paris Agreement ) countries are committed to doing their part to deal with the issue of climate change and the devastation it will cause if we don&amp;rsquo ; t tackle it , &amp;rdquo ; Sohi said , &amp;ldquo ; so I think the realization is there . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inside Canada , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s government is under fire for approving , and then buying , the Trans Mountain pipeline expansion project , which environmentalists view as contradictory to the country&amp;rsquo ; s Paris commitments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;107249;TOOLONG ... title= " From left : Amarjeet Sohi , Canada 's minister of Natural Resources ; Celine Bak , president of the consulting firm Analytica Advisors ; and Carlos Moedas , the European Commission 's commissioner for research , science and innovation during a panel discussion May 28 about progress in making the global transition to clean energy at the Clean Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference in Vancouver , a gathering of top energy officials from 23 countries . " alt= " From left : Amarjeet Sohi , Canada 's minister of Natural Resources ; Celine Bak , president of the consulting firm Analytica Advisors ; and Carlos Moedas , the European Commission 's commissioner for research , science and innovation during a panel discussion May 28 about progress in making the global transition to clean energy at the Clean Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference in Vancouver , a gathering of top energy officials from 23 countries . " data-portal-copyright= " Derrick Penner / PNG " **28;107285;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1022991 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tuesday , however , Sohi said the government remains committed to reducing carbon emissions , which includes both doubling Canada&amp;rsquo ; s investments into renewable energy and supporting the reduction in carbon intensity of existing oil-and-gas production . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think some of the work that ( Paris Agreement ) -member countries are doing can give hope that there are solutions out there , that there are opportunities&amp;rdquo ; to generate jobs and economic prosperity , Sohi said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among signs of progress in Europe , Moedas said , was a recent four-day stretch in Portugal when that country&amp;rsquo ; s entire electricity grid was powered by renewable energy sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And another sign of momentum , Moedas said , was attracting private-sector support from non-governmental sources such as the Bill-Gates-led Breakthrough Energy venture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a series of closed sessions , delegates took part in plenary discussions on how they&amp;rsquo ; re demonstrating their impacts and increasing their ambition related to the conference&amp;rsquo ; s overarching goals of transitioning to a low-carbon economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the public side , Canada used the international conference as a chance to promote its efforts with a series of incremental announcements related to transportation and renewable energy for First Nation communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Environment and Climate Change Minister Catherine McKenna said talking about the opportunities involved in such projects was &amp;ldquo ; the exciting part&amp;rdquo ; of her portfolio , which often deals with the doom-and-gloom of the global climate crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , McKenna co-hosted a workshop , in conjunction with the environmental NGO Clean Energy Canada , on Canada&amp;rsquo ; s pledge to increase support for zero-emission heavy transportation with electric trucks and buses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sohi announced $15.6 million in Natural Resources Canada commitments to four First Nation clean-energy projects , including a $10.4-million contribution to the Skidegate Band Council and Old Masset Village Council&amp;rsquo ; s $30.42-million project to expand renewable power in their communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;107315;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;107346;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;107384;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;107413;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerWed , 29 May 2019 01:42:21 **45;107444;TOOLONG ... NewsVancouver plays host to global conference on boosting clean-power growthhttp : **34;107491;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;107527;TOOLONG ... title= " Federal Infrastructure Minister Amarjeet Sohi has announced $10 billion in new infrastructure spending . " alt= " Federal Infrastructure Minister Amarjeet Sohi has announced $10 billion in new infrastructure spending . " data-portal-copyright= " Dax Melmer " **28;107563;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 201039 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;107593;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world&amp;rsquo ; s transition to clean-energy sources is falling behind what is needed to meet the Paris Climate Agreement goals , officials from 25 countries learned at the start of a major clean-energy conference in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Countries are on track in increasing solar-power generation and power-storage technologies , according to the International Energy Agency ( IEA ) report unveiled Monday at the &lt;a href= " http : **26;107629;TOOLONG " &gt;Cle ... Energy Ministerial and Mission Innovation conference . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , global carbon emissions from power generation increased in 2017 and 2018 after three years of declining emissions , the IEA concluded , and countries are only on-track to meet their goals in seven of 45 categories that the IEA is tracking . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Despite some positive developments over the past year , current technology deployment rates , policy ambition and industry efforts are still falling well-short , &amp;rdquo ; according to the IEA report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; IEA executive director Fatih Birol was scheduled to be in attendance to help push attendees on progress , with Canada&amp;rsquo ; s minister of Natural Resources , Amarjeet Sohi , as host of the event . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Climate change and clean energy may be two of the biggest challenges of our generation , but they are also an opportunity , &amp;rdquo ; Sohi said in his remarks to a pre-opening discussion on energy and Indigenous reconciliation . &amp;ldquo ; An opportunity to drive innovation , to drive clean jobs and build a more sustainable and inclusive economy for generations to come . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And Sohi said working with Aboriginal communities will remain a central part of &amp;ldquo ; all energy decisions in this country . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Clean-energy opportunities are often decentralized and local , which is why community leadership is more important than ever , &amp;rdquo ; and why he chose the session to be his first appearance at the conference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The panel discussion , which saw a handful of community leaders share success stories about building renewable energy projects , was one of a handful of sessions that preceded Monday&amp;rsquo ; s official opening where Sohi unveiled a new federal green-energy initiative . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Under the name Breakthrough Energy Solutions Canada , Sohi&amp;rsquo ; s department will spend $30 million on cutting-edge research on electricity production , transportation and construction technologies for low-carbon alternatives to advance &amp;ldquo ; a sustainable and resilient clean-energy future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The overall conference combines the 10th annual Clean Energy Ministerial meeting , an international gathering of energy ministers , industry executives and Mission Innovation , an initiative from the 2015 Paris Climate Agreement aimed at increasing research into and reducing the cost of renewable energy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sohi is one of a handful of ministerial-level delegates to the conference , which includes delegations from countries including Australia , Austria , China , South Korea and Russia . The European Union is represented along with a strong list of nations such as Spain , Sweden , Norway , Germany , France and the U.K. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The conference includes the participation of observer groups such as the World Bank and International Energy Agency , and going into the event Birol expressed concern about global progress on clean-energy development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to do more to put the world on track to meet all ( sustainable development ) targets , &amp;rdquo ; Birol said in a statement included with the IEA&amp;rsquo ; s release of the report May 22. &amp;ldquo ; I am particularly concerned by the dramatic lack of access to reliable , modern and sustainable energy in certain parts of the world , especially in sub-Saharan Africa . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One positive note at the conference , however , was the &lt;a href= " https : **33;107657;TOOLONG ... IEA report released Monday&lt;/a&gt; showing that the world is on track for goals to promote the use of electric cars and trucks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The IEA&amp;rsquo ; s Mechthied Worsdoffer , director of sustainability , technology and outlooks , addressed the conference to reveal global manufacturers turned out a record 1.98 million electric cars in 2018 , bringing the worldwide fleet of electrics to 5.12 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That is still only a small fraction of all the vehicles on the road , according to the agency , but sales in 2018 did increase by 68 per cent from the previous year with government incentives and policies being the biggest factors influencing adoption . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;107692;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;107723;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;107761;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;107790;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; Derrick PennerTue , 28 May 2019 16:41:22 **45;107821;TOOLONG ... NewsSarah Smith : Capitalizing on domestic opportunities for B.C. 's LNGhttp : **37;107868;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;107907;TOOLONG ... title= " The Spirit of Vancouver Island spent time in Poland , where it was converted to run on liquefied natural gas rather than diesel only . " alt= " The Spirit of Vancouver Island spent time in Poland , where it was converted to run on liquefied natural gas rather than diesel only . " data-portal-copyright= " Times Colonist " **28;107943;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 989330 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;107973;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; LNG Canada&amp;rsquo ; s recent decision to move forward with the largest private investment in our country focused attention on the export-oriented benefits that have dominated public discourse about LNG &amp;mdash ; expanded trading opportunities with Asia , monetizing B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s natural gas deposits and increased use of cleaner natural gas in new international markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While these are among the chief benefits of natural gas , B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s LNG can also be used domestically to reduce greenhouse gas and air contaminant emissions and lower the costs of transporting goods and people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As one of the most economical and clean fuel sources , LNG can play a key role in supporting B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s climate action plan , presenting a way to reduce our net carbon and air pollutant emissions , especially in the transportation sector . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With transportation responsible for roughly 40 per cent of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s GHG emissions , the potential environmental impact of a transition to natural gas is significant . If B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s commercial fleets and vehicles adopt natural gas for fuel , for example , carbon dioxide emissions could be reduced by 20 to 30 per cent . Those fleets would also save up to 50 per cent in fuel costs compared to diesel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Transitioning maritime vessels to natural gas has a similarly dramatic impact . Traditionally reliant on carbon-heavy fuel sources , the use of LNG as a marine fuel is projected to reduce GHG emissions in the marine shipping sector by 21 per cent and offer major cost savings for operators . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The economic and environmental benefits of natural gas have not gone unnoticed around the globe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;108009;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;108051;TOOLONG ... is moving LNG projects forward , but industry sees need to renew sales **26;108086;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;108114;TOOLONG ... Palmer : NDP gets Green scorn , Liberals ' props for getting LNG to a finish **25;108149;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;108176;TOOLONG ... supersized Kitimat LNG project a ' glimmer of hope ' for struggling gas **30;108211;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The International Maritime Organization set a target to reduce GHG emissions in marine shipping by 50 per cent by 2050 . The policy is expected to trigger an increase in the number of LNG-capable vessels and availability of LNG at ports . In fact , the Vancouver Fraser Port Authority offers discounts to ships that use cleaner fuels , is currently working with industry and government to facilitate the broader use of LNG by ships and recently became the first North American port authority to join SEALNG , an organization dedicated to promoting adoption of LNG as a marine fuel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In order to facilitate wider adoption of LNG , more education and awareness is needed on its environmental and economic benefits . The Canada Gas &amp;amp ; LNG Conference in Vancouver last week was an important step in that process . But we also need continued collaboration between private- and public-sector partners , which has already led to meaningful innovation and stewardship at a local level . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For example , FortisBC has worked with Seaspan Ferries Corp . and B.C. Ferries to develop a new process that delivers LNG to fuel their ships via tanker truck delivery on deck &amp;mdash ; a solution that significantly lowers the effective cost of LNG fuelling . The results speak for themselves : Seaspan estimates its carbon dioxide emissions will be reduced by approximately 22 per cent while B.C. Ferries expects to both reduce its fuel costs by millions of dollars and reduce its CO2 emissions by 21,500 tonnes annually &amp;mdash ; the equivalent of taking about 4,400 vehicles off the road per year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another example is how industry has rallied around the government&amp;rsquo ; s CleanBC policies , which call for improved air quality by reducing emissions from short-haul trucking and the transport of goods . In response , the Vancouver Fraser Port Authority , TransLink and the province have introduced a pilot program aimed at giving container truck drivers the opportunities to trial cleaner fuels like natural gas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Collaborative initiatives like these serve as a testament to our domestic market&amp;rsquo ; s potential as both a production source of natural gas and a trial ground for widespread LNG adoption &amp;mdash ; a natural fit , given B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s abundant natural resources , sustainability-focused regulatory framework and collaborative industry environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Sarah Smith is director of natural gas for transportation , regional liquefied natural gas and Renewable Natural Gas for FortisBC. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;108243;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;108279;TOOLONG &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Letters to the editor should be sent to **27;108315;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;em&gt;The editorial pages editor is Gordon Clark , who can be reached at &lt;a href= " **27;108344;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;108373;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;108409;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? Hydro . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The provincial power utility estimates that consumers could save thousands of dollars a year by switching from a vehicle powered by fossil fuels to one powered by electricity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A commuter driving the 80-km round trip from Surrey to Vancouver , for example , would spend $409 a year in an electric Nissan Leaf , according to the survey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Driving a fossil-fuelled Honda Civic would cost an estimated $2,200 or about $1,700 more a year ; a Toyota RV4 , $2,519 , or $2,000 more ; and a Ford F150 , $3,779 or $3,200 more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. Hydro calculations are based on an electric vehicle costing the equivalent of 25 cents a litre in gasoline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;109494;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **26;109529;TOOLONG on Thursday listed 10 gas stations in Vancouver with per litre prices ranging from $1.55 to $1.66. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Fuelling costs for an electric vehicle are about 80 per cent less , &amp;rdquo ; said Tanya Fish , a senior media relations adviser for B.C. Hydro . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The more you drive , the more you save on your fuelling costs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fish said B.C. Hydro wanted to look at the cost of commuting with an electric vehicle given that gasoline prices are so high in Metro Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s very topical , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " https : **34;109557;TOOLONG ... &lt;div style= " width:100% ! ; margin-top:4px ! important ; text-align:right ! important ; " &gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;109593;TOOLONG ... target= " top " class= " flourish-credit " &gt;&lt;img style= " width:105px ! important ; height:16px ! important ; border:none ! important ; margin:0 ! important ; " src= " https : **34;109628;TOOLONG ... alt= " Made with Flourish " **26;109664;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think more and more people are exploring the switch to electric vehicles given that they&amp;rsquo ; ll save significantly on fuelling and maintenance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fish said &lt;a href= " https : **33;109692;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;B.C . Hydro estimates there will be about 350,000 electric vehicles in the province . " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Payne " **28;109763;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1016066 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said &lt;a href= " https : **33;109793;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;B.C . Hydro&lt;/a&gt; plans to add another 23 fast-charging electric vehicle charging stations this year to the existing network of 58 . A fast-charge station can charge an electric vehicle to 80 per cent in 30 minutes or less . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province estimates that consumers can choose from among 44 clean energy vehicles in B.C. priced at $33,000 to $50,000 , before incentives . The lowest EV starts at about $29,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In B.C. , there are &lt;a href= " https : **33;109828;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;three programs available&lt;/a&gt; that could provide a consumer with up to $16,000 in rebates and incentives for buying an electric vehicle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;109863;TOOLONG CEV for B.C. Vehicle Incentive program provides point-of-sale incentives of up to $5,000 to buy or lease a new battery electric vehicle ( it&amp;rsquo ; s up to $6,000 if you buy or lease an hydrogen-fuel cell vehicle ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;109895;TOOLONG Scrap-It Program is a $6,000 incentive available for buying a new , qualifying electric vehicle from participating car dealerships if you&amp;rsquo ; re replacing an internal combustion vehicle older than 2008 . The provincial government estimates about 40 incentives are still available in 2019 from selected car dealers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;109927;TOOLONG The federal iZEV program offers up to a $5,000 point of sale rebate for electric battery vehicles up to $60,000 . ( The rebate is up to $6,000 for a hydrogen-fuel cell vehicle. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;109959;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Norway&lt;/a&gt; expects 50 per cent of new car sales this year will be electric . Cars - Driving UnpluggedEnergyLocal NewsRichard Mackintosh : B.C. LNG will help countries cut their emissionshttp : **31;110419;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;110452;TOOLONG ... title= " This picture taken on January 22 , 2013 shows a thermal power plant discharging heavy smog into the air in Changchun , northeast China 's Jilin province . China has cleaned up its air before but experts say that if it wants to avoid the kind of smog that choked the country this week , it must overhaul an economy fuelled by heavily polluting coal and car use . " alt= " This picture taken on January 22 , 2013 shows a thermal power plant discharging heavy smog into the air in Changchun , northeast China 's Jilin province . China has cleaned up its air before but experts say that if it wants to avoid the kind of smog that choked the country this week , it must overhaul an economy fuelled by heavily polluting coal and car use . " data-portal-copyright= " STR " **28;110488;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 471669 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;110518;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Over the past few years , I have been watching the B.C. LNG industry from afar , as someone knowledgeable about the industry 's global dynamics and the huge potential in front of the province . Until now , like many others in Europe and Asia , I have waited for the first shoe to finally drop . And now that it has , we see B.C. making good on a decade-long promise that has been stalled for far too long . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;When LNG Canada announced it &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;would go ahead with a large-scale LNG export terminal in Kitimat B.C. last fall&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; , it was not just industry supporters and thousands of jobseekers that rejoiced ; backers and LNG buyers in Europe and Asia , and advocates of cleaner air also joined in the celebration . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;As the London , England-based organizer of an LNG conference to convene in Vancouver this month , I must admit I was pleased when I heard about LNG Canada 's decision . But I was n't surprised . B.C. 's position is simply too good in the global LNG and cleaner energy markets for the industry to pass it by . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The long-awaited news that the first major Canadian LNG export project was going ahead created deep excitement in the global energy industry . It all started with something as potentially sleep-inducing as B.C. 's changes to the Income Tax Amendment Act ( stay with me here ) that sets the conditions for a fiscal framework that will allow the LNG opportunity to flourish further . How much further ? Consider LNG Canada 's massive investment and the economic impacts if similarly positive investment decisions are made by other players in B.C. LNG , including Kitimat LNG and Woodfibre LNG in Squamish. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Most recently , Kitimat LNG applied to nearly double the potential size of its facility to export 28.23 billion cubic metres of natural gas per year . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;For the province , these numbers are a signal of intent : to capitalize on LNG and provide British Columbians with associated employment , training , skills and education in a new energy future . And the world needs B.C. 's LNG . It is the logical step to help countries most impacted by the devastating effects of climate change to clean their air . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The UN states that by 2050 , urban populations will have increased by 2.5 billion , with 90 peer cent of this growth concentrated in Asia . In markets like India , China , Bangladesh and Indonesia , where poor air quality continues to claim millions of lives , there is political will for cleaner air . To do this , policy makers need to invest in cleaner sources of energy to displace the dirty coal that is currently burned to produce power . LNG leaving the shores of B.C. can play a key role in moving away from this coal reliance . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The global net benefit of LNG produced in western Canada is clear , reducing emissions for importers on a large scale in addition to pollution-related deaths and disease , and improving air quality for millions . A recent study by Canadian Association of Petroleum Producers suggests that for every LNG export facility built in Canada , global emissions are reduced by 100 million tons of CO2 per year . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; Armed with this data , energy buyers in Asia are lining up to make the switch . China 's LNG demand dominates industry headlines , but in India , four additional LNG receiving terminals are under construction to cater to a very deliberate increase in their own demand . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;With vast reserves of gas in B.C. , and with the technology and resources to produce , process and market this in a cleaner way than anyone else , B.C. can make a difference . And that 's something the world can get behind . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;&lt;i&gt;Richard Mackintosh is event director of the Canada Gas and LNG Exhibition and Conference , which takes place at the Vancouver Convention Centre East , May 21-23. &lt;/i&gt;&lt;a href= " http : //www.canadagaslng.com/ " &gt;&lt;sp ... class= " s3 " **33;110554;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Letters to the editor should be sent to **27;110589;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;em&gt;The editorial pages editor is Gordon Clark , who can be reached at &lt;a href= " **27;110618;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;110647;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;110683;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;110896;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1007277 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;110926;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; First Nations seeking an equity stake in the Trans Mountain pipeline and its proposed expansion aren&amp;rsquo ; t just seeking profit , they want &amp;ldquo ; influence and control&amp;rdquo ; over its environmental impact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As shareholders of this pipeline , we want to be able to appoint a director who will promote Indigenous concerns , who will provide environmental oversight , &amp;rdquo ; said Michael LeBourdais , chair of the Western Indigenous Pipeline Group and chief of the Whispering Pines/Clinton Indian Band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The band had sought an ownership stake during negotiations with Kinder Morgan Canada on a mutual benefits agreement related to an expansion that would triple the capacity of the 60-year-old pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The Trans Mountain pipeline was purchased by the federal government from Kinder Morgan in August of last year for $4.5 billion . Ottawa is expected to approve a $9.3 billion expansion in June after having completed a new round of consultations with First Nations ordered by the court . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several First Nations have expressed interest in buying a stake in the pipeline since the purchase . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We always wanted to buy , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; We always wanted equity . But in our negotiations with Kinder Morgan , equity was not on the table . When the government of Canada bought the pipe it opened the door to equity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whispering Pines is one of 43 First Nations in B.C. and Alberta that have signed &lt;a href= " https : **33;110962;TOOLONG ... benefits agreements&lt;/a&gt; with Trans Mountain worth about $400 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But a number of First Nations have opposed the project in court on a legal landscape that is exceptionally complex . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While elected band councils have signed agreements with the pipeline proponents , the courts have recognized that hereditary First Nations leaders must also be consulted and accommodated on matters concerning the use of their traditional territories . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Grand Chief Stewart Phillip of the Union of B.C. Indian Chiefs wrote &lt;a href= " https : **31;110997;TOOLONG ... open letter to B.C. dead wrong . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Grand Chief Phillip&amp;rsquo ; s letter is indicative of an old way of thinking &amp;hellip ; where indigenous peoples are left out from the benefits of developments on their title land and decisions are made without their consultation , &amp;rdquo ; he wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ownership will give First Nations along the pipeline route the power to lead environmental risk assessments and &amp;ldquo ; realize the largest economic benefits . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This pipeline goes right through the middle of our reserve &amp;hellip ; so we are very familiar with where this pipeline goes and how it works , &amp;rdquo ; said LeBourdais. &amp;ldquo ;
