BusinessLocal NewsPrime Minister called out in B.C.-Alberta gas fight that hit the courts Wednesdayhttp : **31;111309;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;111342;TOOLONG ... title= " Alberta premier Jason Kenney ( left ) and B.C. premier John Horgan. " alt= " Alberta premier Jason Kenney ( left ) and B.C. premier John Horgan. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;111378;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1005315 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;111408;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Premier John Horgan called on Prime Minister Justin Trudeau on Wednesday to pump more refined fuel through the Trans Mountain Pipeline , hours after his lawyers filed a legal challenge to Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s freshly enacted &amp;ldquo ; turn off the taps&amp;rdquo ; legislation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan told reporters he hoped to speak to Trudeau by the end of the day &amp;ldquo ; about what the new owners of that pipeline can do about relieving pressure here in the Lower Mainland . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I can&amp;rsquo ; t rationalize the outrageous spike in prices here relative to other jurisdictions , beyond what I&amp;rsquo ; ve said several times &amp;mdash ; a lack of supply . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The price for a litre of regular unleaded gasoline hovered just shy of $1.70 at many stations in Vancouver at midday Wednesday . Horgan said his message to worried drivers is that he was worried too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said he was taking steps to work with &amp;ldquo ; the owners of the pipeline , the federal government , &amp;rdquo ; to try to reduce the export of bitumen through the pipeline and increase the volume of refined product . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked whether that would change his position on the proposed pipeline expansion , Horgan responded that the use of Burrard Inlet as &amp;ldquo ; an export terminus exclusively&amp;rdquo ; is not in the interest of B.C. &amp;ldquo ; I think there is an opportunity for the three governments to find a way forward . But there is a lot of work to do and what we&amp;rsquo ; re focused on right now is the unacceptably high retail price of gasoline in the Lower Mainland . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Prime Minister&amp;rsquo ; s Office passed the opportunity to comment to Alexandre Deslongchamps , the director of communications for the minister of Natural Resources Canada . &amp;ldquo ; Our government will continue working with our provincial counterparts to ensure a secure , reliable and affordable supply of energy for all Canadians , and support the good , middle-class jobs our energy sector creates , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau is slated to meet with Alberta Premier Jason Kenney on Parliament Hill on Wednesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; **30;111443;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; ... Mike Smyth and Rob Shaw break down the pipeline dispute between Alberta and B.C. , analyze the comments of Premiers Jason Kenney and John Horgan , take look at the NDP government 's new labour bill and also blurt out massive spoilers about Game of Thrones and Avengers : Endgame. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;111475;TOOLONG ... &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s Preserving Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Economic Prosperity Act gives that province the power to impose licences on companies exporting petroleum products and place restrictions on licence holders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its lawsuit filed in Calgary , the B.C. government says that the terms of the licences may restrict or otherwise interfere with or impose cost burdens on the supply of petroleum products to B.C. The suit claims that the Alberta law is illegal and unconstitutional . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The government of Alberta introduced and supported the Act because it asserts British Columbia is responsible for &amp;lsquo ; delays&amp;rsquo ; to an expansion of the Trans Mountain Pipeline , which transports petroleum products from Edmonton to Kamloops , Burnaby and Washington , &amp;rdquo ; says the suit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta seeks to utilize the powers under the legislation to punish and exert pressure upon B.C. with the view to forcing it to discontinue its own court reference case on the pipeline issue , it adds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lawsuit seeks an order suspending operation of the Alberta law pending the outcome of the court case , including the expiry of all applicable appeal periods . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It says that there is a serious issue to be tried and claims that &amp;ldquo ; irreparable harm&amp;rdquo ; would result to British Columbians if the law is carried out . The Alberta government has 20 days to respond to the suit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;111517;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;111559;TOOLONG ... Jason Kenney to British Columbians : ' We will never be afraid to stand up for Alberta ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;111594;TOOLONG ... Alberta really ' turn off the taps ' to B.C. ? Here 's what we know about Jason Kenney 's **25;111629;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;111656;TOOLONG ... Poll : Do you believe Alberta premier Jason Kenney will ' turn off the taps ' ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; A violation of the Alberta law could result in fines of up to $10 million per day for corporations and $1 million per day for individuals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joel Bakan , a professor at the University of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s Allard School of Law , explained how the court battle could play out . After B.C. argues its case at the first level of Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s Superior Trial Court , the court will likely reserve judgment . The eventual ruling will likely be taken to the Court of Appeal in Alberta , and then that ruling may be appealed at the Supreme Court of Canada , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A final verdict could take years , but in the meantime , B.C. will ask for an injunction against the operation of the Act , Bakan said . He guessed B.C. would likely win the injunction and also win on the merits of the case . One real problem for Alberta is that a government can not pass a law that regulates exports in a way that discriminates against another province or region , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lawsuit notes that a significant percentage of the gas and diesel consumed in B.C. is imported from Alberta refineries , either by pipeline , train or tanker truck . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A significant disruption in the supply of gasoline , diesel and crude oil from Alberta to British Columbia would cause British Columbia irreparable harm , &amp;rdquo ; says the suit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In addition to economic harm , a sudden disruption in supply could injure human health and safety in remote communities . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The battle between the two provinces over the pipeline extends back to 2018 when the B.C. government announced plans to look at proposed legislation to improve preparedness , response and recovery from potential spills into the environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In response , then-Alberta Premier Rachel Notley announced that imports of B.C. wine into Alberta would be terminated . B.C. responded by referring the Alberta measure to a dispute resolution process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Horgan then announced that he intended to ask cabinet to set a court reference case on the pipeline issue , a case that has been heard by the B.C. Court of Appeal and is awaiting a decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Andrew Wilkinson , the leader of the B.C. Liberal Party , called for Horgan to cut fuel taxes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The premier can cut the price of gas any time he wants to by reducing taxes , but he refuses to do so . So we&amp;rsquo ; re left paying the price at the pumps and the premier has no plan at all . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;111691;TOOLONG " **37;111723;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **28;111762;TOOLONG " **35;111792;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with file from Rob Shaw&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Keith Fraser , Matt RobinsonThu , 02 May 2019 16:45:41 **45;111829;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalB.C. ready for court battle as Alberta proclaims turn-off-the-taps lawhttp : **37;111876;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;111915;TOOLONG ... title= " Alberta Premier Jason Kenney , in a message to British Columbians , says his cabinet has proclaimed the so-called ' turn-off-the-taps ' law , but still hopes to not have to use it . " alt= " Alberta Premier Jason Kenney , in a message to British Columbians , says his cabinet has proclaimed the so-called ' turn-off-the-taps ' law , but still hopes to not have to use it . " data-portal-copyright= " CODIE MCLACHLAN " **28;111951;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1004752 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;111981;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta has proclaimed a law that allows it to slow the flow of oil and gas to B.C. For its part , the B.C. government says it is ready to fight in court right away . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta Premier Jason Kenney&amp;rsquo ; s new cabinet was sworn in Tuesday and immediately held its first meeting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kenney , &lt;a href= " http : **34;112017;TOOLONG ... an opinion piece addressed specifically to British Columbians&lt;/a&gt; in Wednesday&amp;rsquo ; s Vancouver Sun , said cabinet at that meeting proclaimed &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;into law &lt;/span&gt; Bill 12 , &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;the Preserving Canada 's Economic Prosperity Act&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The law gives the Alberta government the power to impose licences on companies exporting petroleum products and place restrictions on licence holders . It gives the province the power to limit the allowable daily quantities of petroleum products shipped , the method of shipment and point of export , and length of time a licence is in effect . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kenney campaigned on a promise to proclaim the act &amp;ldquo ; within an hour of being sworn in&amp;rdquo ; so that Alberta could &amp;ldquo ; turn off the taps&amp;rdquo ; to B.C. if necessary . It has been touted as potential retaliation against the B.C. government for fighting the proposed Trans Mountain pipeline expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Attorney General David Eby said B.C. was ready to immediately file a legal challenge against Bill 12 as soon as it became law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our materials are all prepared , &amp;rdquo ; Eby said Tuesday . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; And the court was clear with us , when we were there already on this law , that it needed to be proclaimed into law before we were in Alberta court . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NDP passed Bill 12 a year ago but did not proclaim it into law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In February , a B.C. government court challenge of Bill 12 was shot down by a Calgary judge who said B.C. took the case to court prematurely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;112053;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;112095;TOOLONG ... Jason Kenney to British Columbians : ' We will never be afraid to stand up for Alberta ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eby said his government believes it will be straightforward , this time , for a court to determine that the law is unconstitutional , because of a provision in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Constitution preventing provinces from restricting the flow of refined products between provinces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That is what Alberta is attempting to do , &amp;rdquo ; Eby said . &amp;ldquo ; So we&amp;rsquo ; ll be making that argument . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We did not proclaim this law to reduce energy shipments to B.C. but to have the power to protect Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s ability to get full value for our resources should circumstances require , &amp;rdquo ; Kenney wrote in his statement to British Columbians. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The impetus for the act is to alleviate the resource backlog that has been exacerbated by delaying TMX , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; the Trans Mountain expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;112130;TOOLONG ... title= " A no trespassing sign is seen posted outside double security fencing at the Kinder Morgan Burnaby Terminal tank farm , the terminus point of the Trans Mountain pipeline , in Burnaby , B.C. , on Tuesday April 30 , 2019. " alt= " A no trespassing sign is seen posted outside double security fencing at the Kinder Morgan Burnaby Terminal tank farm , the terminus point of the Trans Mountain pipeline , in Burnaby , B.C. , on Tuesday April 30 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " DARRYL DYCK " **28;112166;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1004609 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kenney said he had no immediate plans to cut reduce energy shipments to B.C. , but it would give his government the ability to use the law if it felt it was necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is my job as premier to defend Alberta , to preserve the value of our resources and stand up for our workers , &amp;rdquo ; Kenney wrote . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Our government wants to co-operate with B.C. and other provinces whenever we can . But we will never be afraid to stand up for Alberta when we must . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Green Leader Andrew Weaver dismissed Kenney&amp;rsquo ; s campaign threat as &amp;ldquo ; bluster&amp;rdquo ; and said the law would fall apart in court . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While it may serve well during an election campaign , where you are trying to instil this kind of power and strengthen its bluster , legally , he can&amp;rsquo ; t do it , &amp;rdquo ; Weaver said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Weaver said that if Kenney used Bill 12 to turn off the taps , he&amp;rsquo ; d also be turning off the taps to Washington state and creating turmoil with the North American Free Trade Agreement and oil companies who have signed supply agreements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;112196;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; d have to deal with our Constitution , which is quite clear , &amp;rdquo ; Weaver said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; He would open himself up to a liability and frankly it wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be him &amp;mdash ; it would be the people of Alberta . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Liberal Leader Andrew Wilkinson wrote an open letter on Tuesday to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau and the B.C. and Alberta premiers , accusing Kenney of &amp;ldquo ; posturing&amp;rdquo ; and John Horgan of &amp;ldquo ; divisive rhetoric&amp;rdquo ; which could significantly increase B.C. gas prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gas prices in B.C. have recently hit $1.79 a litre in some municipalities. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson called on the three leaders , and Indigenous governments , to &amp;ldquo ; to sit down and hammer out a plan for a path forward&amp;rdquo ; within the next 60 days . Discussions should take into consideration B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s energy needs and environment concerns , and Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s need to get its petroleum products to market , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson urged Alberta to commit to not &amp;ldquo ; turning off the taps&amp;rdquo ; and urged B.C. to hold off on a court challenge while the leaders work on a resolution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;112232;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;div&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;With files from Rob Shaw and The Edmonton **28;112268;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href= " **29;112298;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **46;112329;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **25;112377;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;112404;TOOLONG ... &lt;/div&gt; Nick EaglandWed , 01 May 2019 15:52:18 **45;112437;TOOLONG ... NewsPremier Jason Kenney to British Columbians : ' We will never be afraid to stand up for Alberta'http : **32;112484;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;112518;TOOLONG ... title= " Alberta Premier Jason Kenney announced his plan to create jobs in the Alberta energy sector while on the campaign trail in Turner Valley at Dynamite Buckers in early April . " alt= " Alberta Premier Jason Kenney announced his plan to create jobs in the Alberta energy sector while on the campaign trail in Turner Valley at Dynamite Buckers in early April . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;112554;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 997110 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;112584;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , at the first cabinet meeting of our new Alberta government , we proclaimed into law the Preserving Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Economic Prosperity Act , which gives our government the ability to curtail oil shipments from Alberta . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We did not proclaim this law to reduce energy shipments to B.C. , but to have the power to protect Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s ability to get full value for our resources should circumstances require . Let me explain why Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s new government has taken this step . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Trans Mountain Pipeline , connecting Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s petroleum refineries to B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s Lower Mainland , has been safely operating since 1953 and it remains the way that most B.C. gasoline is delivered . In recent years , industry has proposed a major increase to the capacity of this pipeline system through the Trans Mountain Expansion project , or TMX. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; TMX was approved by the National Energy Board and the federal cabinet with 157 legally binding conditions designed to ensure the highest environmental standards on Earth . The federal government is currently taking more time to ensure proper completion of indigenous consultations &amp;mdash ; a necessary step to fully discharge the Crown&amp;rsquo ; s duty to consult . It is worth noting that more than 100 First Nations support the expansion , as do the majority of British Columbians. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The impetus for the act is to alleviate the resource backlog that has been exacerbated by delaying TMX . Unfortunately , since coming into office in July 2017 , the B.C. government has opposed the expansion project every step of the way , most recently in the B.C. Court of Appeal . One province claiming to have the power to block exports from the rest of Canada would undermine one of the principles of our Confederation , the economic union between our provinces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;112620;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;112662;TOOLONG ... Kenney says he will not immediately enact ' turn off the taps ' **32;112697;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;112731;TOOLONG ... ready for court battle as Alberta proclaims turn-off-the-taps law&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; British Columbians are currently facing a gasoline crisis , paying an unsustainable $1.70 per litre or more in Metro Vancouver . Premier John Horgan has suggested that Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s lack of refinery capacity is the problem while doing everything he can to block the expansion of the very pipeline that would get Alberta oil , refined or otherwise , to domestic and international markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The campaign to landlock Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s resources , resulting in the failure of several pipeline projects to Canada&amp;rsquo ; s West and East coasts , has been economically devastating , contributing to high unemployment , bankruptcies and insolvencies . This is not sustainable , nor is it in the national interest . Alberta must have the ability , if and only for so long as needed , to control the export of its natural resources in order to maximize their value , whether through B.C. or otherwise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Albertans are feeling deep frustration , a sense that we have contributed massively to the rest of Canada but are being blocked and pinned down at every turn . Albertans have contributed more than $600 billion to the rest of Canada since 1961 through fiscal transfers , creating hundreds of thousands of well-paid , family supporting jobs , including for British Columbians , and all those who have left behind the despair of unemployment for the dignity of work in Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s opportunity society . We are proud to have helped our fellow Canadians when times were tough but now it is Albertans who are going through a time of trial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s former NDP government passed Bill 12 in our legislature but stopped there . By proclaiming this law , we are showing that we are serious about protecting Canada&amp;rsquo ; s vital economic interests . This does not mean energy shipments will immediately be reduced but that our government will now have the ability to actually use the law should circumstances require . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is my job as premier to defend Alberta , to preserve the value of our resources and to stand up for our workers . Our government wants to co-operate with B.C. and other provinces whenever we can . But we will never be afraid to stand up for Alberta when we must . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Jason Kenney is premier of Alberta . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Letters to the editor should be sent to **27;112766;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;em&gt;The editorial pages editor is Gordon Clark , who can be reached at &lt;a href= " **27;112795;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **34;112824;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;112860;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? &lt;p&gt; During the campaign that swept his United Conservative Party into government , Kenney vowed to &amp;ldquo ; turn off the taps&amp;rdquo ; to B.C. with provincial legislation that would enable Alberta to squeeze petroleum shipments to the province to pressure Horgan over his government&amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of Friday , Metro Vancouver drivers faced average prices of $1.71 a litre for regular gasoline across the city , with prices as low as $1.56 per litre and as high as $1.73 , according to the crowdsourced price tracking website GasBuddy.com. &lt;/p&gt; NewsCompetition Bureau mum on Metro Vancouver gas-price investigationhttp : **27;114434;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;114463;TOOLONG ... title= " Prices across Metro Vancouver hit $1.73 per litre at many gas stations , with an absolute high of $1.79 per litre at one Delta location ( which has since dropped its price to $1.74 ) , according to crowdsourced reports to GasBuddy.com. " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;114499;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1000099 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;114529;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cracking down on gasoline price-fixing is a priority for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Competition Bureau , but the bureau , because of confidentiality concerns , can&amp;rsquo ; t say whether or not it&amp;rsquo ; s investigating what might be behind Metro Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s soaring fuel costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , Premier John Horgan called for consumer-protection officials to examine Metro&amp;rsquo ; s soaring gas prices and said he would push Prime Minister Justin Trudeau to take action on what he has termed as &amp;ldquo ; gouging&amp;rdquo ; by fuel companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We recognize that gasoline prices are an important issue for Canadians and cracking down on anticompetitive conduct in the gasoline sector is a priority for the Competition Bureau , &amp;rdquo ; bureau spokesman Marcus Callaghan wrote in an emailed response to Postmedia News . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prices across Metro hit $1.73 per litre at many gas stations , with an absolute high of $1.79 per litre at one Delta location ( which has since dropped its price to $1.74 ) , according to crowdsourced reports to GasBuddy.com. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since 2008 , the bureau has levelled $6 million in fines and conditional sentences to 33 individuals and 12 companies found to be manipulating gasoline prices , Callaghan said , but cautioned that &amp;ldquo ; identical prices ( being charged in a market ) in and of themselves are not evidence of illegal activity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bureau constantly monitors all consumer markets looking for signs that there is a lack of competition and manipulation of prices , said Jason Parent , vice-president of the fuel-industry consulting firm Kent Group , and charges are still not that common in the fuel market . Parent said the cases that do come to light often involve one or a few stations co-operating to maintain prices . Wider-scale collusion , however , has been more illusive to prove . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;114565;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;114607;TOOLONG ... Palmer : Horgan points fingers , but not at himself , for high gas **27;114642;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;114671;TOOLONG ... prices in B.C. : What to know about cheap gas , jerry cans and **25;114706;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not that ( price-fixing ) is difficult to prove , it&amp;rsquo ; s effectively not happening to the degree some people might expect that it is , &amp;rdquo ; Parent said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A 2003 report by the Parliamentary Standing Committee on Industry Science and Technology &amp;ldquo ; did not find any evidence of collusion by retailers , &amp;rdquo ; though it did recommend government create a petroleum monitoring agency . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2011 , then-Industry Minister Tony Clement tried another push at getting the industry to explain &amp;ldquo ; opaque&amp;rdquo ; pricing methods . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Wednesday , officials with the industry groups the Canadian Fuels Association and the Canadian Energy Research Institute declined to comment on pricing issues , instead pointing to third-party independent consultants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Parent said he doesn&amp;rsquo ; t speak for the industry , but acknowledged there are complex and related reasons for Lower Mainland gas prices that are higher than elsewhere in Canada . The region doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have enough of its own fuel-refining capacity , Parent said , and limited ability to transport it via pipeline . That leaves the market dependent on imports from elsewhere on the West Coast where prices are already high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And Parent said government can&amp;rsquo ; t ignore the role that taxes , including B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s carbon tax and transit taxes , play in increasing prices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You can make a really good point for why some of these environmental policies are necessary , &amp;rdquo ; Parent said . &amp;ldquo ; But at some point you&amp;rsquo ; ve got to be honest about the cost implications of that . It&amp;rsquo ; s a bit disingenuous to blame other things like collusion when again , it&amp;rsquo ; s really policy decisions and taxes that are behind a lot of it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;114733;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;114764;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;114802;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;114831;TOOLONG &l; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;114862;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? NewsOttawa should be ready to make Trans Mountain decision in June : Garneauhttp : **33;115540;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;115575;TOOLONG ... title= " Federal Transportation Minister Marc Garneau speaks to The Vancouver Sun and Province editorial board on Tuesday . " alt= " Federal Transportation Minister Marc Garneau speaks to The Vancouver Sun and Province editorial board on Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;115611;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1000060 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;115641;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal Transportation Minister Marc Garneau said Tuesday he expects his government to be in a position by mid-June to make a decision on whether to proceed with the $9.3-billion Trans Mountain Pipeline expansion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In an editorial board meeting with The Vancouver Sun and Province , Garneau said he would not prejudge the decision on the pipeline , now owned by the Canadian government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s government announced it had pushed back to June 18 from May 22 its deadline for the reconsideration of the controversial oil pipeline project in order to complete consultations with First Nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government was forced to reconsider the project after the Federal Court of Appeal ruled in August 2018 that Ottawa had not adequately consulted each of six First Nations that had challenged the project&amp;rsquo ; s approval , and &amp;ldquo ; unjustifiably&amp;rdquo ; had not considered increased tanker traffic related to the project , which will have a negative effect on endangered killer whales . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After taking into consideration new information on the marine effects , the National Energy Board ruled in an expedited re-examination of the project that it could go ahead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First Nations consultations are taking place with eight federal teams that include members from transportation , natural resources , justice and finance . Former Supreme Court Justice Frank Iacobucci is advising the government on the consultation process . Iacobucci and First Nations both recommended the deadline be pushed back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am not going to prejudge the outcome of that decision , &amp;rdquo ; said Garneau about a June announcement . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re focused on having all of the work done to enable us to make a decision based on having done our homework and satisfying the Federal Court of Appeal . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If the government was to make a positive decision in June , Garneau , who was in Vancouver to make a $6.5-million funding announcement for training marine workers , said he believed some work could be restarted relatively quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Industry observers have already noted that delays in constructing the pipeline will add to its pricetag. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;115677;TOOLONG ... title= " An aerial view of Kinder Morgan 's Trans Mountain marine terminal , in Burnaby , B.C. , is shown on Tuesday , May 29 , 2018. " alt= " An aerial view of Kinder Morgan 's Trans Mountain marine terminal , in Burnaby , B.C. , is shown on Tuesday , May 29 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " Jonathan Hayward " **28;115713;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 982306 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Trans Mountain company said Tuesday that if the federal government comes to a positive conclusion , the company is prepared to restart construction once necessary approvals are in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a written statement , Trans Mountain spokeswoman Ali Hounsell said there are regulatory and commercial steps including re-mobilizing the contractors , notifications and ensuring the company has met all its pre-construction conditions before it can get shovels in the ground . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have continued to work with our contractors to ensure planning and design is advancing in a way that would allow us to get back to work as soon as possible , &amp;rdquo ; said Hounsell. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada purchased the Trans Mountain expansion project and the existing pipeline from Alberta to Burnaby in the Spring of 2018 after Kinder Morgan signalled it was going to pull the plug because of uncertainty and opposition to the project , in particular from the B.C. NDP government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The project is meant to open up new markets for Alberta oilsands products in Asia . However , the project has faced opposition from environmental groups , some First Nations and some municipalities , including Vancouver and Burnaby , over concerns about the effects of an oil spill and climate change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **30;115743;TOOLONG " **37;115775;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;115814;TOOLONG " &gt; ... &lt;/p&gt; Gordon HoekstraTue , 23 Apr 2019 23:34:48 **45;115844;TOOLONG ...
